Dressings & Sauces

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup soy sauce
  • 1/4 cup water
  • 2 tsp Oriental mustard
  • 2 tsp heavy cream
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder

Method

  1. Whisk everything together until smooth.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup chopped onion
  • 1/4 cup soy sauce
  • 1 clove garlic, minced
  • 1/2 oz ginger root (nickel-size slice), peeled and chopped
  • Juice of 1/2 lemon (2 Tbsp)
  • 1/2 tsp sugar
  • 1/4 tsp white vinegar

Method

  1. Blend all ingredients until nearly smooth.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup chopped onion
  • 1/4 cup peanut oil
  • 2 Tbsp rice wine vinegar
  • 2 Tbsp water
  • 1 Tbsp minced fresh ginger
  • 1 Tbsp chopped celery
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1 1/2 tsp tomato paste
  • 1 1/2 tsp sugar
  • 1 tsp lemon juice
  • Dash each salt and pepper

Method

  1. Combine everything in a blender or food processor until almost smooth. Makes about 1 cup.

Ingredients

  • 3/4 cup vegetable oil
  • 1/4 cup cider vinegar
  • 2 Tbsp water
  • 1 tsp minced garlic
  • 2 tsp sugar
  • 1 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/4 tsp pepper
  • 1 tsp grated Parmesan

Method

  1. Combine all ingredients in a bottle; shake to blend.
  2. Let flavors blend about an hour — even better the next day. Refrigerate leftovers.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup + 1 tsp red wine vinegar
  • 1/4 cup + 1 tsp apple cider vinegar
  • 1/4 cup regular vinegar
  • 1/2 cup vegetable oil
  • 1 tsp salt
  • 3 tsp strained onion/oil juice
  • 1/4 tsp dried mixed Italian spices

Method

  1. For the onion juice (3 tsp): blend fresh onion (Vidalia is good) with just enough vegetable oil (1/2 cup) to purée. Strain; use the strained juice/oil.
  2. Whisk everything together.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups cilantro, minced then measured
  • 5 garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 Tbsp dried oregano
  • 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice (or red wine vinegar)
  • 1/2 cup extra virgin olive oil
  • Sea salt, to taste
  • Optional brine: 1 cup water + 1 Tbsp sea salt

Method

  1. Optional salt brine: boil the water and salt, remove from heat, cool to room temp.
  2. Combine cilantro (2 cups), garlic, pepper flakes (2 tsp), oregano and lemon juice (1/4 cup). Whisk in olive oil (1/2 cup), then brine if using. Season with salt.
  3. Let flavors mingle a few hours. Cilantro fades — best within a few days, up to 1 week.

Ingredients

  • 2 oz cubed Parmesan
  • 2 cloves garlic
  • 2 Tbsp Dijon mustard
  • 1 1/2 tsp white wine vinegar
  • 1 1/2 tsp Worcestershire sauce
  • Pinch kosher salt + black pepper
  • 1 cup silken soft tofu
  • 2 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Starting on lowest speed, chop the Parmesan (2 oz) cubes in the blender, gradually increasing speed. Drop the garlic (2 cloves) down the chute and chop until minced.
  2. Add mustard (2 Tbsp), vinegar (1 1/2 tsp), Worcestershire, salt, pepper and tofu (1 cup); blend until smooth.
  3. With blender running, drizzle olive oil (2 Tbsp) into the vortex until it reaches dressing consistency.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup tahini, well stirred
  • 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice
  • 1 small garlic clove, grated
  • 3/4 tsp ground turmeric
  • 1 tsp maple syrup or honey
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1/3–1/2 cup ice water

Method

  1. Whisk tahini (1/2 cup), lemon juice (1/4 cup), garlic, turmeric, maple and salt (1/2 tsp) — it will seize and thicken.
  2. Whisk in ice water (1/3–1/2 cup) a splash at a time until creamy and pourable, like heavy cream.
  3. Keeps 1 week refrigerated; loosen with water as needed.

Ottolenghi-adjacent all-purpose dressing — pairs with the rice salad, roasted cauliflower and any of the quinoa salads.

Ingredients

  • 1 (28-oz) can whole San Marzano tomatoes, D.O.P. if possible
  • 1/4 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • 7 garlic cloves, peeled and slivered
  • Small dried whole chile, or pinch crushed red pepper flakes
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 large fresh basil sprig, or 1/4 tsp dried oregano, more to taste

Method

  1. Crush tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can) with your hands in a large bowl. Slosh 1 cup water in the can to get the juices; reserve.
  2. Heat the oil in a large skillet (not a deep pot) over medium; add garlic (7 cloves).
  3. As soon as the garlic sizzles (don't let it brown), add tomatoes, tomato water, chile, oregano if using, and salt (1 tsp). Stir.
  4. Lay the basil (1 large sprig) sprig on the surface, let it wilt, then submerge. Simmer until thickened and the surface oil is deep orange, about 15 minutes. Discard basil and chile.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup olive oil
  • 30 cloves garlic (about 2 large heads)
  • 3 (28-oz) cans San Marzano tomatoes
  • 2 Tbsp fennel seeds
  • 1/2 Tbsp red chili flakes, crushed
  • 3 sprigs basil
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp kosher salt

Method

  1. Warm the oil in a heavy saucepan over medium-low. Add garlic (30 cloves) and cook until softened and starting to caramelize golden.
  2. Meanwhile drain the tomatoes (3 (28-oz) cans) (reserve juice) and crush them by hand.
  3. Smash half the garlic cloves in the pot with a fork. Stir in tomatoes, fennel seed and chili flakes (1/2 Tbsp).
  4. Simmer, stirring occasionally, until melded and slightly thickened, about 45 minutes.
  5. Add basil (3 sprigs) and salt (1 1/2 Tbsp); cook 5 minutes. Remove basil, taste and adjust — pasta water will season it further.

Ingredients

  • 5 cloves garlic
  • Coarse salt and black pepper
  • 1/4 cup + 2 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil, divided
  • 1 large onion, diced
  • 3 carrots, peeled and diced
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 1 cup fresh basil leaves
  • 2 Tbsp fresh oregano leaves
  • 1 (28-oz) can crushed tomatoes

Method

  1. Heat oven to 400°F. Wrap garlic (5 cloves) in foil with salt, pepper and 2 Tbsp oil; roast ~25 minutes until brown and tender. Squeeze from skins.
  2. Sauté onion (1 large) and carrots (3) in the remaining oil. Add tomato paste (2 Tbsp), stir well and cook ~10 minutes until it turns orangey.
  3. When soft, add roasted garlic, salt, pepper, basil and oregano. Pour in tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can) plus one can of water.
  4. Boil, then simmer uncovered 1 hour, stirring occasionally. Purée in a food processor until smooth.

Carrots + roasted garlic offset the tomatoes' acidity. Freeze in ice cube trays, then bag — portion control.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp butter
  • 1/2 cup onion, chopped
  • 1/4 cup celery, chopped
  • 1 garlic clove, minced
  • 1 (8-oz) can tomato sauce
  • 1 (6-oz) can tomato paste
  • 2 Tbsp grated Parmesan
  • 1 tsp each dried basil and oregano
  • 1/2 tsp each salt and sugar
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper
  • 1 small bay leaf
  • 1 tsp fennel seed

Method

  1. Melt butter (1 Tbsp) with oil in a large skillet. Sauté onion (1/2 cup), celery (1/4 cup) and garlic until soft and transparent.
  2. Add tomato sauce (1 (8-oz) can) and paste; stir until smooth.
  3. Add everything else and bring to a slow simmer; simmer 30–60 minutes (or not at all, depending on taste and time). Remove bay leaf (1 small) before spreading on dough.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp white miso
  • 1/4 cup rice vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1 Tbsp sugar or honey
  • 1 tsp grated ginger
  • 2 Tbsp toasted sesame oil
  • 1/4 cup neutral oil
  • 1 Tbsp toasted sesame seeds

Method

  1. Shake or blend everything until creamy and emulsified.
  2. Keeps 2 weeks refrigerated. The name is accurate: greens, grain bowls, roasted vegetables, cold noodles, the crispy tofu.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 2 (12-oz) bags cranberries, fresh or frozen (6 cups)
  • 1 1/2 cups honey (one honeybear)
  • Small pinch kosher salt
  • Optional: 1/2 cup citrus juice, grated citrus zest or fresh ginger

Method

  1. Combine cranberries (2 (12-oz) bags), honey and salt in a medium pot (frozen berries can go straight in). Bring to a boil, drop to medium, add 1/2 cup water.
  2. Cook, stirring occasionally, until the berries burst and the pot turns jammy — a few stubborn whole ones are charming.
  3. Off heat, add citrus or ginger if using. Cool completely; it firms as it chills. Keeps 1+ week refrigerated.
  4. Deliberately austere and barely sweet — add more honey (1 1/2 cups) if your table complains.

Also in Alison Roman’s archive. Her version →

Ingredients

  • 2 tablespoons whole grain mustard
  • ¼ cup sherry, white wine, or red wine vinegar
  • Kosher salt and cracked black pepper
  • ¼ cup thinly sliced chives or scallions

Method

  1. Stir the mustard (2 Tbsp) and vinegar (¼ cup) together in a small bowl.
  2. Mix in the chives (¼ cup) and season with salt and pepper.
  3. Toss with greens (escarole, arugula, romaine or kale), taste and adjust vinegar and salt, then finish with a drizzle of olive oil.

Escarole is her preferred green since it holds up to dressing. No storage guidance given. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 pound apples (about 3), cored and chopped
  • ¼–⅓ cup sugar
  • 1 cup water
  • Lemon juice or apple cider vinegar, to finish (optional)
  • Cinnamon, to finish (optional)

Method

  1. Put the chopped apples (1 lb), sugar (¼ cup) and water (1 cup) in a medium pot.
  2. Simmer over medium until the apples collapse completely, mashing with a wooden spoon to the texture you like.
  3. Off the heat, add more sugar (up to ⅓ cup total) if needed, plus a splash of lemon juice or vinegar and a little cinnamon if wanted.

Peeling is optional; skins add texture. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 (14-ounce) cans jellied cranberry sauce
  • 2 tangerines, oranges or blood oranges, peeled and sliced into rounds, seeds removed
  • ½ small red onion, thinly sliced
  • 1 lemon, halved crosswise
  • Flaky sea salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • ½ cup mint or parsley leaves (optional)

Method

  1. Slide the cranberry sauce (2 cans) out and cut into ½-inch rounds.
  2. Lay the rounds on a big platter and scatter over the citrus slices (2) and red onion (½).
  3. Squeeze the lemon (1) over everything, then finish with flaky salt, pepper and herbs (½ cup) if using.

Choose the smooth jellied kind without whole berries; it slices more cleanly. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 small shallot, finely chopped
  • 1 garlic clove, finely grated
  • 2 Tbsp white wine, red wine or sherry vinegar, plus more
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 Tbsp whole-grain or Dijon mustard (or a mix)
  • 1/3 cup olive oil, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp water

Method

  1. Let the shallot (1 small) and garlic (1 clove) sit in the vinegar (2 Tbsp) with salt and pepper a few minutes to mellow.
  2. Mix in the mustard (2 Tbsp) and water (1 Tbsp).
  3. Add the oil (1/3 cup); stir or shake to combine.
  4. Taste and nudge with more of anything.

Whole-grain reads rustic, Dijon creamier. Keeps 1 week in the fridge; good on salads, lentils, roasted vegetables. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Dips & Spreads

Ingredients

  • 1 cup plain full-fat Greek yogurt
  • 3 oz feta cheese
  • 1 medium clove garlic, halved
  • 1 Tbsp fresh lemon juice
  • 2 Tbsp chopped fresh dill
  • 1 1/2 tsp finely grated lemon zest

Method

  1. Put everything in a food processor and blend until smooth.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 lb (250 g) feta
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 medium pepper, seeded and finely chopped
  • 1 small dried red chili, finely chopped
  • 1 pickled green pepper, seeded and finely chopped
  • 2 tsp dried oregano
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • Juice of 1/2 lemon
  • Black olives, for garnish

Method

  1. Mash the feta (1/2 lb) with a fork, gradually adding olive oil (3 Tbsp), the chopped peppers, oregano (2 tsp) and black pepper (1).
  2. Beat and mash about 10 minutes (less in a food processor), mixing in lemon juice occasionally, until soft and creamy.
  3. Refrigerate at least an hour before serving. Garnish with olives.

Ingredients

  • 1 pkg (12 oz) Smart Ground Original
  • 1 1/2 tsp chili powder
  • 1 tsp onion powder
  • 1 (16-oz) can fat-free refried beans
  • 1 (4-oz) can diced green chilies
  • 3/4 cup taco sauce
  • 2 cups shredded reduced-fat cheese blend
  • 1 cup fat-free sour cream
  • 1/3 cup green onions
  • 1 Tbsp olive oil

Method

  1. Crumble Smart Ground into a bowl; mix with chili powder (1 1/2 tsp) and onion powder (1 tsp).
  2. In an oiled 13×9×2 baking dish, layer beans (1 (16-oz) can), the Smart Ground mixture, green chilies (1 (4-oz) can), taco sauce and 1 1/2 cups cheese.
  3. Cover and bake at 350°F for 25–30 minutes until bubbling at the edges. Cool 5 minutes.
  4. Spread sour cream (1 cup) on top; sprinkle with green onions (1/3 cup) and remaining cheese. Serve with tortilla chips.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 clove garlic, peeled
  • 1 cup pitted kalamata olives
  • 2 Tbsp capers
  • 3 Tbsp chopped fresh parsley
  • 2 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • Salt and pepper to taste

Method

  1. Pulse everything in a food processor to a coarse paste; season to taste.

Ingredients

  • 2 ripe avocados
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 Tbsp fresh lime or lemon juice
  • 2 Tbsp to 1/4 cup minced red onion or thinly sliced green onion
  • 1–2 serrano chiles, stems and seeds removed, minced
  • 2 Tbsp cilantro, finely chopped
  • Dash freshly grated black pepper
  • 1/2 ripe tomato, seeded and chopped

Method

  1. Mash avocados (2) with salt and lime juice (1 Tbsp); fold in onion (2 Tbsp), serrano, cilantro (2 Tbsp), pepper and tomato (1/2).
  2. Garnish with red radishes or jicama; serve with tortilla chips.

Ingredients

  • 5 Tbsp chopped fresh cilantro, divided
  • 1/4 cup minced white onion
  • 1 large jalapeño, seeded, minced, divided
  • Kosher salt
  • 6 medium or 4 large avocados (about 3 lb), halved and pitted
  • 1 medium tomato, cored and chopped
  • 3 Tbsp fresh lime juice
  • 1 tsp lime zest

Method

  1. Mash 2 1/2 Tbsp cilantro, the onion (1/4 cup), 1 Tbsp jalapeño and a pinch of salt to a coarse paste in a mortar (or with a fork).
  2. Cube the avocado flesh in the skins, scoop into the mortar, and mash until slightly chunky.
  3. Mix in remaining cilantro (5 Tbsp total) and jalapeño, tomato (1 medium), lime juice (3 Tbsp) and zest. Season with salt.
  4. Do ahead: press plastic wrap on the surface and chill up to 4 hours.

Ingredients

  • 2 plum tomatoes, chopped
  • 1 ripe avocado, peeled, pitted and chopped
  • 1/4 cup finely chopped red onion
  • 1 clove garlic, minced
  • 1 Tbsp snipped fresh parsley
  • 1 Tbsp chopped fresh oregano
  • 1 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp red or white wine vinegar
  • 4 oz crumbled feta

Method

  1. Gently toss everything together; let sit briefly so flavors blend.

Ingredients

  • 8 oz carrots, peeled, 1-inch pieces
  • 3 cloves garlic, peeled, whole
  • 2 Tbsp + 1/2 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 (15-oz) can chickpeas, drained and rinsed
  • 2 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 1/8 tsp cayenne
  • Root vegetable chips, for dipping

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Toss carrots (8 oz), garlic, 2 Tbsp oil and 1/2 tsp salt on a small rimmed sheet; cover with foil and roast until tender, ~15 minutes. Cool.
  2. Pulse carrots, garlic (3 cloves) and chickpeas (1 (15-oz) can) in a food processor. Add lemon juice (2 Tbsp), 1/2 cup oil, cayenne and 1/4 tsp salt; purée until smooth and pale orange.

Organic chickpeas can run dry — add up to 1/4 cup water to loosen.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil
  • 2 medium red onions, coarsely chopped
  • 1 1/2 cups yellow split peas
  • Pinch of turmeric
  • Vegetable stock or water
  • Salt to taste
  • 1 bay leaf
  • 1–3 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 8 dried shiitakes, soaked in warm water
  • 1/3 cup red wine
  • 1 Tbsp red wine vinegar

Method

  1. Heat 2 Tbsp oil over low; soften a quarter of the onions (2 medium), ~8 minutes. Add split peas (1 1/2 cups) and turmeric, toss to coat, add 3 cups stock/water, salt and bay leaf (1).
  2. Boil, then simmer low until the peas have absorbed the liquid and are like chunky mashed potatoes — add liquid as needed, stir so it doesn't stick.
  3. Meanwhile caramelize the remaining onion in 2 Tbsp oil with a little salt. Slice soaked shiitakes (8) 1/4-inch and add. Raise heat, add wine (1/3 cup) and vinegar (1 Tbsp), season, and cook until the juices are thick.
  4. Purée the fava with lemon juice (1–3 Tbsp) and remaining oil until velvety. Serve warm or room temp, mushrooms spooned on top.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 cups whole-milk ricotta
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • Kosher salt
  • 3 Tbsp honey
  • 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 tsp cider vinegar
  • Flaky salt; toasts or crackers

Method

  1. Whip the ricotta (1 1/2 cups) with olive oil and a pinch of salt in a food processor until light and mousse-y, ~1 minute.
  2. Warm the honey (3 Tbsp) with pepper flakes (1/2 tsp) and vinegar until loose and spicy.
  3. Swoosh the ricotta on a plate, drizzle the hot honey over, flaky salt on top. Ten minutes flat.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 1 (8-oz) block feta
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp honey
  • 1 tsp fresh thyme leaves or a pinch of oregano
  • Black pepper
  • Crackers, pita or bread

Method

  1. Set the feta (1 (8-oz) block) in a small baking dish; coat with the oil.
  2. Bake at 400°F until soft through, 15–20 minutes, then broil a minute for color.
  3. Warm the honey (2 Tbsp) and pour it over; thyme and pepper to finish. Serve immediately, while spreadable.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

Scallion oil

  • ⅓ cup olive oil
  • 4 scallions (or green garlic), white and light green parts, thinly sliced
  • 1 teaspoon red-pepper flakes
  • 2 tablespoons fresh cilantro (leaves and tender stems) or chives, plus more for garnish
  • Flaky sea salt
  • Freshly ground black pepper

Base

  • 2 cups labne, full-fat Greek yogurt, or sour cream
  • 2 tablespoons fresh lemon juice

Method

  1. Combine the olive oil (⅓ cup), scallions (4), pepper flakes (1 tsp) and cilantro (2 Tbsp) in a small pot over medium-low, swirling now and then until audibly sizzling and the oil turns bright orange.
  2. Let cool a bit, then season with salt and pepper.
  3. Stir the labne (2 cups) with lemon juice (2 Tbsp) and season with salt and pepper.
  4. Spread in a bowl, swirl in the scallion oil, and top with extra cilantro.

Both parts keep refrigerated in separate airtight containers up to a week; combine just before serving. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 clove garlic
  • 2 scallions, thinly sliced and finely chopped
  • 1 lemon
  • 1 heaping cup (8–10 ounces) sour cream or full-fat Greek yogurt
  • ½–¾ cup (4–6 ounces) blue cheese, crumbled
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground pepper
  • Buttermilk, lemon juice, or water, for thinning

Method

  1. Grate the garlic (1 clove) into a medium bowl and add the scallions (2).
  2. Squeeze in lemon juice (from 1 lemon) and season with salt and pepper.
  3. Stir in the sour cream (1 heaping cup) and taste, adding more lemon, salt or pepper as needed.
  4. Fold in the blue cheese (½–¾ cup) and loosen with buttermilk, lemon juice or water if it needs to be pourable.

Works as a wedge salad dressing, BLT spread or vegetable dip; without the cheese it's a garlicky creamy sauce. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 lb large tomatillos, husks removed
  • Olive oil, for rubbing
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 1/2 yellow onion, quartered (or 1/2 bunch scallions)
  • 1 jalapeño, halved
  • 2 garlic cloves, finely grated
  • 1 cup cilantro, finely chopped
  • 1/4 cup fresh lime juice, plus more

Method

  1. Rub the tomatillos (2 lb), onion (1/2) and half the jalapeño with oil, salt and pepper.
  2. Grill, turning, until army-green and charred, 10–15 minutes, pulling pieces as they finish (scallions need just 5–7).
  3. Cool slightly, then chop coarsely (or blend a big batch) into a bowl.
  4. Mince the raw jalapeño half and stir in with the garlic (2 cloves), cilantro (1 cup) and lime (1/4 cup).
  5. Season with salt and more lime; finish with a good pour of olive oil.

No grill: roast the oiled vegetables at 450°F, 15–20 minutes. Keeps a week refrigerated. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3/4 cup neutral oil (grapeseed or canola)
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 1 large egg yolk
  • 1 tsp Dijon mustard
  • 1–2 garlic cloves, finely grated, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp white vinegar or lemon juice, plus more
  • Kosher salt

Method

  1. Combine both oils (3/4 cup neutral + 1/4 cup olive) in a spouted cup.
  2. Set the yolk (1), Dijon (1 tsp) and garlic (1–2 cloves) in a bowl.
  3. Whisk in the oil a few teaspoons at a time, fully emulsifying between additions; loosen with a splash of the vinegar if it tightens.
  4. Finish with the vinegar or lemon (1 Tbsp total), salt, and extra garlic to taste; thin with water for a drizzling sauce.

Keeps about a week refrigerated. Her original offers anchovy as an add-in – omitted here. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Pickles & Marinated

Ingredients

  • 3 1/2 oz sea salt (about 1/3 cup + 1 Tbsp)
  • 1/2 cup apple cider vinegar
  • 8 cups bottled water
  • 1 1/2 lb kirby cucumbers, washed and dried
  • Few sprigs fresh dill, washed and dried
  • Optional: 1 tsp sugar, 3–4 garlic cloves, 2 Tbsp peppercorns

Method

  1. Bring salt, vinegar (1/2 cup) and water to a boil; simmer 5 minutes. Cool slightly.
  2. Pack cucumbers (1 1/2 lb) upright and tight in a container, coming within 1/2 inch of the top; tuck in the dill (and optional garlic/peppercorns).
  3. Fill to the top with brine; tap on the counter to release air bubbles. Keeps up to a few weeks refrigerated.

Ingredients

  • 2 lb firm, unwaxed kirby pickling cucumbers
  • 2 cloves spring garlic, sliced thin (optional)
  • 1 dill flower, or 5 sprigs fresh dill or 1 tsp dill seed (optional)
  • 1/2 tsp coriander seed (optional)
  • 1/2 jalapeño, seeded and slivered (optional)
  • 2 Tbsp salt

Method

  1. Soak cucumbers 30 minutes in ice water; slice off the blossom end (opposite the stem — or a little off both ends). Cut into spears or chunks if desired.
  2. Pack into clean quart jars with garlic (2 cloves), dill, coriander and jalapeño.
  3. Dissolve salt (2 Tbsp) in 2 cups boiling water; add 2 cups ice (filtered if your water is chlorinated) and stir until cool. Pour over to cover.
  4. Cap loosely, set jars in a bowl (they may leak) and ferment on the counter — the brine bubbles lazily and turns cloudy. Taste after 3 days; go another day or two for more sour, then refrigerate. Keeps a month.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 kg baby eggplants
  • 1 cup vinegar
  • 2 cups water
  • 3 tsp salt
  • Fresh garlic, sliced
  • Chili pepper, sliced
  • Bay leaves and allspice berries, optional
  • Olive oil, to cover

Method

  1. Wash eggplants (1/2 kg), remove stems, cut a cross slit in the top of each.
  2. Bring water, vinegar (1 cup) and salt to a boil with the eggplants; cook until they soften (check with a fork). Cool in the pot.
  3. Fill each slit with a slice of garlic and chili.
  4. Pack into a sterilized jar, pour over the cold liquid, add extra garlic, chili, bay and allspice to taste.
  5. Top with olive oil to cover completely. Seal and leave at room temperature about 1 week before opening; refrigerate after opening.

Ingredients

  • 1 medium head red cabbage
  • 1 cup water
  • 1 cup red wine vinegar
  • 1 heaping Tbsp sugar
  • 1 clove garlic, finely minced
  • 1 tsp salt
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper
  • Pinch red pepper flakes (optional)

Method

  1. Core and shred cabbage (1 medium head) finely (mandoline or sharp knife); place in a large bowl.
  2. Whisk water, vinegar (1 cup) and sugar until sugar (1 heaping Tbsp) mostly dissolves; stir in garlic (1 clove), salt, pepper (1/4 tsp) and flakes.
  3. Pour brine over cabbage, transfer to jars with tight lids, sit on the counter 4–6 hours, then chill.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 lb small purple Italian or white eggplants (about 3), trimmed, sliced lengthwise 1/8-inch
  • 1 1/4 tsp salt
  • 5 Tbsp red-wine vinegar
  • 1 tsp sugar
  • 1 garlic clove, thinly sliced
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped fresh mint
  • 2/3 cup extra-virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Toss eggplant with 1 tsp salt in a colander; drain 30 minutes. Rinse, press out excess liquid, pat dry.
  2. Whisk vinegar (5 Tbsp), sugar and remaining 1/4 tsp salt until dissolved; stir in garlic (1 clove) and mint (1/2 cup).
  3. Fry eggplant in hot oil in a nonstick skillet, ~8 slices at a time, until pale golden, about 1 minute per side; drain on paper towels.
  4. Layer in a shallow dish, spooning the vinegar-mint mixture over each batch. Cover and chill at least 8 hours.

Ingredients

  • 1 (15-oz) can chickpeas, drained and rinsed
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp fresh oregano, chopped
  • 1 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 1 Tbsp lemon zest
  • 1 tsp fresh parsley, chopped
  • 1/4 tsp garlic, minced
  • Sea salt, to taste

Method

  1. Mix everything well in a bowl.
  2. Marinate in the refrigerator 8 hours to overnight.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb kirby or Persian cucumbers
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 2 Tbsp gochugaru (Korean chile flakes)
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 2 tsp sugar
  • 3 garlic cloves, grated
  • 1 tsp grated ginger
  • 2 scallions, sliced
  • 1 tsp toasted sesame oil; sesame seeds

Method

  1. Smack the cucumbers (1 lb) with the flat of a knife until they crack, then break into chunks. Salt (1 tsp) 20 minutes; pour off the liquid.
  2. Toss with everything else.
  3. Eat right away, but it's even better after a night in the fridge. Keeps ~4 days.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 1 lemon
  • 1 orange
  • Peel of 1 grapefruit
  • 1/2 cup olive oil
  • 4 chiles de árbol (or 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes)
  • 1 lb unpitted Castelvetrano olives

Method

  1. Slice half the lemon and half the orange thin; keep the other halves for juicing.
  2. Warm the oil (1/2 cup) with the chiles (4), citrus slices and grapefruit peel until they sizzle, 1–2 minutes.
  3. Add the olives (1 lb); steep on the lowest heat 10–60 minutes, stirring occasionally.
  4. Cool in the pot, then squeeze in the reserved lemon and orange halves. Serve with a pit bowl.

Marinate up to a month ahead, covered and chilled; rewarm gently. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1/3 cup olive oil
  • 2 (15-oz) cans butter, lima or gigante beans, rinsed
  • 1 small garlic clove, grated
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1/2 cup red or white wine vinegar
  • Lemon zest, to finish

Method

  1. Warm the oil (1/3 cup) in a small pot over medium.
  2. Add the beans (2 cans) and garlic (1 clove) with salt and pepper; heat just 1–2 minutes to chase off the canned taste.
  3. Off heat, stir in the vinegar (1/2 cup); sit at least 30 minutes.
  4. Serve with some of the marinade, black pepper and lemon zest over.

Keeps 2 weeks refrigerated – bring to room temp. The board's marinated-bean family grows again. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup olive oil
  • 2 sprigs fresh oregano
  • 1 lemon, thinly sliced, seeded
  • 1 shallot, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 (14-oz) cans artichoke hearts in water, drained, halved
  • 2 Tbsp white wine vinegar
  • 1/2 cup mint or parsley leaves

Method

  1. Sizzle the oregano (2 sprigs), lemon slices (1 lemon) and shallot (1) in the oil (1/2 cup) over medium until they take color, 4–6 minutes; season, off heat.
  2. Pour the hot oil over the artichokes (2 cans) in a bowl.
  3. Add the vinegar (2 Tbsp), season again, and rest at least 30 minutes.
  4. Scatter with mint or parsley (1/2 cup) just before serving.

Marinates up to 2 weeks ahead (add herbs at the end). Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 1/2 lb mixed citrus (grapefruit, tangerines, oranges, lemons, kumquats)
  • 2 1/2 cups demerara sugar
  • 2 1/2 cups granulated sugar
  • 1/4 cup lemon juice
  • 10 cups water

Method

  1. Cut rind and pith off the citrus (2 1/2 lb) as if segmenting; slice the peels ~1/8 inch and the fruit thin, seeding as you go.
  2. Boil just the peels in the water (10 cups) 90+ minutes, until halved and nearly translucent.
  3. Add the fruit; boil until reduced by a third, ~45 minutes.
  4. Stir in both sugars (5 cups total); cook ~1 hour, skimming, stirring constantly near the end.
  5. Test at 220°F or on a chilled plate; add the lemon juice (1/4 cup), boil 1–2 minutes, cool 10, jar.

Keeps in the fridge roughly forever. Pairs with the bostock and the scones. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 4 lb mixed tomatoes
  • A few shallots, spring onions or leeks, in wedges
  • 3–5 garlic cloves, smashed
  • A few sprigs thyme, marjoram or oregano
  • A few whole dried chiles (árbol, guajillo, chipotle)
  • Kosher salt
  • At least 1/2 cup extra-virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Core the tomatoes (4 lb); leave small ones whole, halve mediums, cut big ones into 2-inch pieces. Into a Dutch oven (not cast iron).
  2. Tuck in the alliums, garlic (3–5 cloves), herbs and chiles; salt well.
  3. Pour over the oil (1/2 cup+) so everything can swim.
  4. Roast at 300°F covered 2–2.5 hours, then uncovered ~1 hour more, until jammy.

Keeps a month in oil in the fridge, 6 months frozen – end-of-summer insurance for toast, eggs and beans. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Salads

Ingredients

  • 5 ears of corn, shucked
  • 1/2 cup small-diced red onion (1 small)
  • 3 Tbsp cider vinegar
  • 3 Tbsp good olive oil
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper
  • 1/2 cup julienned fresh basil

Method

  1. Boil the corn (5 ears) in salted water 3 minutes, until the starchiness is just gone. Shock in ice water; cut kernels off close to the cob.
  2. Toss kernels with onion (1/2 cup), vinegar, oil, salt (1/2 tsp) and pepper (1/2 tsp).
  3. Toss in basil (1/2 cup) just before serving; taste for seasoning.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 head red cabbage
  • 1 medium beet (yellow or candy-striped is nice)
  • Juice of 1 lemon, divided
  • 1 Tbsp shio kombu, optional
  • 1 tsp dried hijiki seaweed
  • 1 tsp ume vinegar or red wine vinegar
  • 2 Tbsp sweet white miso paste
  • 3 Tbsp tahini
  • 1 tsp unseasoned rice vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp shiro shoyu or light soy sauce
  • Anchovies (per original dressing)
  • 3 Tbsp neutral oil
  • 1/2 cup kasha
  • 2 Tbsp aonori or finely shredded nori
  • 2 Tbsp toasted white sesame seeds

Method

  1. Core the cabbage (1/2 head) and slice into thin strips (easier to eat than the original 1-inch pieces). Scrub and trim the beet (1 medium), slice 1/8-inch on a mandoline (or julienne), season with half the lemon juice, and add to the cabbage.
  2. Dressing: bloom hijiki in warm water 15 minutes; drain and season with ume vinegar (1 tsp). Whisk with miso, tahini (3 Tbsp), rice vinegar (1 tsp), shiro shoyu, remaining lemon juice and the anchovies — it should be thick, almost mayonnaise-like.
  3. Kasha (1/2 cup) furikake: heat oil until shimmering, fry kasha, stirring constantly, until darkened two shades of brown (watch closely — bitter if burnt). Drain, season while hot with the seaweed (1 tsp), and once cool mix with sesame seeds (2 Tbsp) and shio kombu (1 Tbsp). Salt to taste.
  4. Toss cabbage and beets with 3 Tbsp dressing; adjust. Top with ~4 Tbsp furikake; pass extra at the table (or save for rice).

Ingredients

Marinated feta

  • 4 oz feta (not crumbled), creamy sheep's-milk type
  • 1 garlic clove, smashed and peeled
  • 1 dozen black or mixed peppercorns, coarsely cracked
  • Extra-virgin olive oil, preferably cold-pressed

Salad

  • 2 large hearts romaine, outer leaves removed
  • 2 lemons, halved
  • 1 avocado, halved, pit removed
  • About 2 oz sprouts (alfalfa, clover or rainbow)
  • 2 mini or kirby cucumbers, spears or sliced
  • 2–3 hard-boiled eggs, room temp, peeled and halved
  • Flaky salt, coarse black pepper, olive oil
  • 2 handfuls soft herbs (basil, chives, cilantro, mint)
  • Sourdough toast, optional

Method

  1. Marinate the feta (4 oz) in olive oil with garlic (1 clove) and cracked peppercorns (1) (do ahead; keeps refrigerated).
  2. Arrange romaine (2 large), cucumbers (2), avocado, sprouts and eggs (2–3) on a platter. Squeeze lemon over, season with flaky salt and pepper, drizzle with olive oil.
  3. Top with the marinated feta and its oil, shower with herbs; serve with toast.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice
  • 2 Tbsp Dijon mustard
  • 1 Tbsp minced shallot
  • 1 small garlic clove, finely grated
  • 1/4 tsp kosher salt, plus more
  • Black pepper
  • 2 large bunches Tuscan kale (~1 1/2 lb), stemmed, thinly sliced
  • 12 oz brussels sprouts, trimmed, shredded
  • 1/2 cup extra-virgin olive oil, divided
  • 1/3 cup almonds with skins, coarsely chopped
  • 1 cup finely grated pecorino

Method

  1. Stir lemon juice (1/4 cup), Dijon, shallot, garlic (1 small clove), salt (1/4 tsp total) and a pinch of pepper; set aside to meld.
  2. Mix kale (2 large bunches) and shredded sprouts (12 oz) in a large bowl.
  3. Toast almonds in 1 Tbsp of the oil over medium-high until golden in spots, ~2 minutes; salt lightly.
  4. Slowly whisk remaining oil into the lemon mixture; season. (Dressing, greens and nuts hold separately 8 hours.)
  5. Toss greens with dressing and cheese; garnish with almonds.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup cubed kabocha, butternut or other winter squash
  • Extra-virgin olive oil
  • Salt and pepper
  • 1 bunch lacinato kale, ribs removed, finely sliced (~2 1/2 cups)
  • 1/4 cup almonds, roughly halved
  • 1/4 cup crumbled aged cheddar (Cabot clothbound; or Parmesan)
  • Fresh lemon juice
  • Pecorino for shaving, optional

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Toss squash in oil, salt and pepper; roast on parchment until tender and caramelized, ~40 minutes, tossing every 10–15. Toast almonds (1/4 cup) in the same oven until nutty, ~10 minutes.
  2. Toss kale (1 bunch) with almonds, cheddar and squash. Dress to taste with lemon juice and olive oil (~1 Tbsp lemon : 2 Tbsp oil); season.
  3. Divide between two plates; shave pecorino over if you like.

Ingredients

  • 1 Tbsp coconut oil
  • 3/4 cup cooked, cooled quinoa (cook 1/2 cup quinoa in 3/4 cup broth — dry and slightly undercooked crisps best)
  • 1 head butter lettuce
  • 2 cups arugula
  • 3/4 cup toasted hazelnuts, roughly chopped
  • 1 cup pomegranate seeds

Meyer lemon yogurt dressing

  • 1/4 cup meyer lemon juice
  • 1 clove garlic, grated
  • 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp honey
  • 1 tsp sea salt
  • 1 tsp dried Italian herbs
  • Pinch of pepper
  • 1/3 cup whole-milk Greek yogurt or sour cream
  • 3 Tbsp extra virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Heat coconut oil (1 Tbsp) over high; sauté the quinoa 2–3 minutes until dry and crackling-crisp (add a bit more oil if needed). Cool completely.
  2. Whisk lemon juice (1/4 cup), garlic, vinegar, honey (1 Tbsp), salt (1 tsp), herbs (1 tsp), pepper and yogurt; whisk in oil. (Keeps up to a week.)
  3. Toss greens in dressing; top with crispy quinoa, hazelnuts (3/4 cup) and pomegranate. Serve immediately.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb yellow wax beans, trimmed
  • 1/2 lb runner beans, trimmed
  • 1/2 lb haricots verts or string beans, trimmed
  • 1 red onion, halved and sliced
  • 2 tsp coarse sea salt
  • 2 lemons
  • 2 cups crumbled Greek feta
  • Black pepper + 1 tsp red/pink peppercorns
  • 1/3 cup fresh basil, julienned
  • 1/3 cup flat-leaf parsley, chopped
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced or slivered
  • 1/3 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil

Method

  1. Blanch beans (1/2 lb) in ample salted water 3–4 minutes; shock in ice water and drain.
  2. Grill the whole lemons (2) (barbecue, ribbed skillet or broiler) until lightly charred. Cool slightly; grate the zest, then juice.
  3. Pulse feta (2 cups), 2 Tbsp oil, 2 Tbsp lemon zest, black pepper, half the basil (1/3 cup) and the garlic in a food processor.
  4. Toss beans (1 lb) with the spicy feta, remaining oil and basil, parsley (1/3 cup), onion, peppercorns, salt (2 tsp) and pepper. Sharpen with lemon juice to taste.

Ingredients

  • 1 small head cauliflower or 4 cups florets
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 cup cherry tomatoes, sliced
  • 1/4 cup sliced red onion
  • 1/4 cup kalamata olives
  • 4 oz feta, crumbled
  • 1/4 cup fresh basil, chopped

Dressing

  • 1/4 cup lemon juice (2 lemons) + zest of 1
  • 1/2 tsp each salt and pepper
  • 2 cloves garlic, minced
  • 1/4 cup extra virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Toss florets (1 small head) with 3 Tbsp oil on a baking sheet; roast 45 minutes. Cool.
  2. Combine cauliflower, tomatoes (1 cup), onion (1/4 cup), olives, feta and basil (1/4 cup).
  3. Whisk the dressing, toss well; serve now or chilled.

Ingredients

  • 3 large sweet potatoes, scrubbed, whole
  • 6 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil, divided
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 cup tahini, well-stirred
  • 7 Tbsp fresh lime juice, divided
  • 3/4 cup each coarsely chopped basil and mint
  • 1 habanero, stemmed and seeded
  • 1 Tbsp each raw pistachios and raw sesame seeds
  • 1 tsp whole cumin
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (Mexican if possible)
  • 1/4 tsp crushed red pepper or chile de árbol
  • 1 poblano, seeded, thin strips
  • 1 grapefruit, peeled, sliced into rounds
  • 1 serrano, seeded, sliced into rings

Method

  1. First roast: bake sweet potatoes (3 large) at 350°F until tender, 60–80 minutes; cool. Break into 3-inch pieces, drizzle with 3 Tbsp oil and salt, and roast at 475°F for 20–30 minutes until browned and lightly charred. Cool.
  2. Sauce: blend tahini (1 cup), 1/4 cup lime juice, basil, mint (3/4 cup), habanero, 2 tsp salt and 3/4 cup water until light green and creamy (thin to sour-cream consistency); season.
  3. Topping: toast pistachios and sesame until golden, ~3 minutes; then toast cumin (1 tsp), oregano (1 tsp) and pepper flakes until fragrant, ~2 minutes. Combine.
  4. Toss poblano (1) with 1 Tbsp each lime juice (7 Tbsp total) and oil; same for the sweet potatoes; salt both.
  5. Spread tahini sauce on a platter; top with sweet potatoes, poblano and grapefruit (1). Sprinkle with the seed mixture and serrano (1) rings.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb frozen vegetable dumplings
  • 1 lb ripe tomatoes, in chunks; kosher salt
  • Vinaigrette: 2 Tbsp chile crisp, 2 Tbsp rice vinegar, 1 Tbsp soy sauce, 1 tsp sugar, 1 tsp toasted sesame oil
  • 1 small cucumber, smashed and sliced (optional)
  • Cilantro and basil, torn

Method

  1. Salt the tomatoes in the serving bowl and let them get juicy.
  2. Cook the dumplings (1 lb) your favorite way — steamed, boiled, or pan-fried crisp.
  3. Whisk the vinaigrette; toss the warm dumplings with the tomatoes and dressing.
  4. Cucumber (1 small) and lots of herbs over the top. Eat immediately, slightly warm.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 lb broccoli, cut into very small florets, stems peeled and sliced
  • 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • 1/3 cup olive oil
  • 2 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 1 tsp cumin seeds
  • 1 tsp toasted sesame oil
  • 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes; sesame seeds

Method

  1. Toss the raw broccoli (1 1/2 lb) with salt (1 1/2 tsp) and vinegar (2 Tbsp) in a big bowl.
  2. Warm the olive oil (1/3 cup) with garlic and cumin seeds (1 tsp) until the garlic (2 cloves) just turns golden; pour it, hot, over the broccoli.
  3. Add sesame oil (1 tsp) and flakes; toss well.
  4. Marinate at least 1 hour (better: several) — the broccoli softens and drinks the dressing. Sesame seeds to serve.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup olive oil, divided
  • 2/3 cup coarse breadcrumbs, toasted golden in 2 Tbsp of the oil

Ingredients

  • 4 ears corn, shucked
  • 4 thick slices country bread
  • 1/4 cup olive oil, plus more for grilling
  • 1 lb tomatoes, in chunks
  • 1/2 small red onion, thinly sliced
  • 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • Kosher salt and pepper; 1 cup basil, torn

Method

  1. Grill the corn (4 ears) and oiled bread (4) until charred; cut the kernels off, tear the bread into chunks.

Broccoli charred hard, dressed with garlic and basil.

One of the few recipes still subscriber-only on her site – opens with your login. Paste the text here anytime and it becomes a full card. Open recipe →

Ingredients

  • 1–2 heads bitter greens (radicchio, treviso, castelfranco, or escarole), roughly torn
  • 1 tangerine, orange, blood orange, or grapefruit, sliced about 1/4 inch thick
  • 1/2 small shallot or red onion, thinly sliced
  • 2 tablespoons lemon or acidic orange juice (or 1 tablespoon orange juice if sweeter, plus 1 tablespoon sherry or white wine vinegar)
  • Extra virgin olive oil
  • Kosher salt and fresh ground black pepper

Method

  1. Tear the bitter greens (1–2 heads) onto a wide platter.
  2. Lay the citrus slices (1 fruit) and thin shallot (1/2 small) among and over the greens in layers.
  3. As you build, dress each layer with citrus juice (2 Tbsp), olive oil, salt and pepper.
  4. Taste and adjust acid and salt depending on how sweet the fruit and how bitter the greens are.

Rinse the onion slices in cold water if raw onion feels too sharp; crushed olives, capers or pecorino are optional add-ins. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Honey'd walnuts

  • 1–1 1/2 cups raw walnuts, coarsely chopped
  • 3–4 tablespoons olive oil
  • 2 tablespoons honey
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper

Salad

  • 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice and 2 tablespoons finely grated lemon zest (from about 2 lemons), plus more
  • 1–2 cloves garlic, finely grated
  • 2 bunches lacinato kale, stems removed, torn into bite-sized pieces
  • 1 cup parsley, tender leaves and stems
  • 1 bunch chives, finely chopped
  • 1/2 bunch tarragon or mint, tender leaves and stems only

Method

  1. Toast the walnuts (1–1 1/2 cups) in olive oil (3–4 Tbsp) for about 2 minutes, add the honey (2 Tbsp) and cook until it bubbles and caramelizes and coats the nuts, about 2 more minutes; season with salt and pepper.
  2. Stir the lemon juice (1/4 cup), zest (2 Tbsp) and garlic (1–2 cloves) together in a big bowl, add the torn kale (2 bunches) with salt and pepper, and massage until well coated; taste and adjust.
  3. Add the parsley (1 cup), chives (1 bunch) and tarragon or mint (1/2 bunch) and toss.
  4. Fold in the walnuts so they are spread through the salad, not just on top.

Recipe has an optional dash of fish sauce (1/2 tsp), omitted here. Nuts keep several days sealed at room temperature; the dressed salad base keeps a day in the fridge. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Pecans

  • 2 tablespoons olive oil, plus more
  • 1 cup raw/untoasted pecans or walnuts, coarsely chopped
  • 1 tablespoon fennel seed
  • 2 tablespoons honey

Salad

  • 2 crisp, tart apples (honeycrisp or gold rush), thinly sliced
  • 1 large or 2 medium fennel bulbs, very thinly sliced lengthwise, or 4 celery stalks, thinly sliced
  • 2–3 cups bitter greens (radicchio, castelfranco, endive, or arugula)
  • 1/2 cup herbs (tarragon, dill, or chives)
  • 2 tablespoons sherry vinegar, apple cider vinegar, or fresh lemon juice, plus more
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper

Method

  1. Toast the pecans (1 cup) in oil (2 Tbsp) for about 2 minutes, add fennel seed (1 Tbsp), salt and pepper, and cook 4–5 minutes more until golden, watching closely.
  2. Stir in the honey (2 Tbsp) until it bubbles and lightly caramelizes and coats the nuts; take off the heat.
  3. Toss the apples (2) and fennel (1 large bulb) with the vinegar (2 Tbsp), salt and pepper.
  4. Spread the greens (2–3 cups) and herbs (1/2 cup) on a platter, pile the apple-fennel mixture on top, drizzle with more vinegar, and mix gently; taste.
  5. Scatter the spiced pecans and extra herbs over the top.

Pecans can be made a few days ahead and kept in the fridge; leftovers are good over roasted vegetables or other salads. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 large head escarole, romaine or chicories (like radicchio), torn into large pieces
  • 1 1/2 cups parsley
  • 3/4 cup finely chopped chives
  • 1/4 cup tarragon or dill (optional)
  • 3 tablespoons whole grain mustard
  • 2 tablespoons fresh lemon juice or white wine vinegar, plus more
  • Kosher salt
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Pile the torn escarole (1 large head), parsley (1 1/2 cups), chives (3/4 cup) and tarragon (1/4 cup, if using) into a big bowl.
  2. Whisk the mustard (3 Tbsp) with lemon juice (2 Tbsp), salt and pepper in a small bowl.
  3. Spoon the dressing over the greens and toss, drizzle with olive oil, and adjust salt, pepper and lemon to taste.

Greens and herbs can be combined ahead, chilled or covered with a damp paper towel; leftover wilted greens are good with fried eggs or in soup. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 cup walnuts, almonds, hazelnuts or pecans, coarsely chopped (optional)
  • 2 large sour or tart apples (such as Granny Smith), unpeeled, thinly sliced, core removed if desired
  • 1 bunch celery, leaves and stalks separated, thinly sliced
  • 1/2 medium yellow onion, thinly sliced
  • 2 lemons, zested and juiced
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 cups parsley, tender leaves and stems
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

Ingredients

  • 4 cups spicy greens (arugula, mizuna, or torn mustard greens)
  • Leaves and tender stems from 1 bunch parsley
  • Leaves and tender stems from 1 bunch cilantro
  • 1 bunch chives, coarsely chopped
  • 1 to 2 cups mint or dill leaves (optional)

Method

Ingredients

  • 6 ears yellow corn, husks removed
  • 1–2 bunches scallions
  • 2 tablespoons olive oil, plus more
  • 1/2 cup fresh lime juice, pepperoncini liquid, vinegar, or a mix, plus more
  • 6 pepperoncini peppers, coarsely chopped
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 cup cilantro, tender leaves and stems
  • 4 ounces queso fresco, haloumi, or other firm, mild, salty fresh cheese like feta, crumbled or coarsely chopped
  • 1/2 cup corn nuts (or roasted pumpkin seeds, sunflower seeds or almonds), coarsely chopped

Method

  1. Heat a grill to high. Rub the corn (6 ears) and scallions (1–2 bunches) with oil and salt, and grill the corn 10–15 minutes, turning, until charred outside and tender inside; grill the scallions until the tops char and the bulbs turn golden.
  2. Let the corn cool enough to handle, then stand each ear over a bowl and slice the kernels off close to the cob.
  3. Chop the scallions and add to the corn with salt, pepper and olive oil (2 Tbsp).
  4. Stir in the cilantro (1 cup), pepperoncini (6) and the lime juice or pickling liquid (1/2 cup), then the cheese (4 ounces) and corn nuts (1/2 cup).
  5. Keep tasting and adding lime, salt and pepper until it tastes almost too limey.

No grill: roast the corn and scallions at 450F, turning now and then; slower and paler. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 large hothouse or 6 Persian or Kirby cucumbers, cut into bite-sized pieces
  • 1 small red onion, sliced on the thicker side
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 tablespoon fresh lemon or lime zest
  • 2 tablespoons fresh lemon or lime juice (or white wine or rice wine vinegar), plus more
  • 1/4 cup white sesame seeds, well toasted
  • 2–3 tablespoons toasted sesame oil
  • 1–2 tablespoons sumac, optional
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 1/2 cups cilantro, dill or mint, torn or coarsely chopped

Method

  1. Put the cucumbers (2 large or 6 small) and red onion (1 small) in a big bowl or platter and salt them.
  2. Add the citrus zest (1 Tbsp) and juice (2 Tbsp) and adjust salt and acid until pleasantly salty and bright.
  3. Drizzle with sesame oil (2–3 Tbsp), scatter the toasted sesame seeds (1/4 cup), sumac (1–2 Tbsp, optional), pepper and herbs (1 1/2 cups), and toss gently.

Dress right before eating or give it time to marinate; for a heartier dish serve over tahini dressing or seasoned yogurt, with chickpeas or white beans. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 small head iceberg lettuce, cut into 1 1/2-inch slices (crosswise) or wedges
  • 1/2 small white or yellow onion, or 1 large shallot, thinly sliced into rings
  • 2 tablespoons capers, coarsely chopped
  • 2 tablespoons distilled white vinegar, white wine vinegar, or red wine vinegar (plus more as needed)
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • Olive oil, for drizzling
  • About 1/2 cup parsley, finely chopped
  • A few sprigs oregano or marjoram, leaves removed and finely chopped
  • About 1/2 cup pecorino cheese, finely grated (plus more if desired)

Method

  1. Lay the iceberg (1 small head) on a platter or shallow bowl and top with the onion (1/2 small) and capers (2 Tbsp).
  2. Spoon over the vinegar (2 Tbsp) and season with salt and pepper.
  3. Drizzle with olive oil, scatter the parsley (1/2 cup) and oregano or marjoram, and finish with the pecorino (1/2 cup).

Strictly a make-it-at-the-last-minute salad; the lettuce can be cut a few hours ahead and kept in a zip bag in the fridge. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 oranges, peel and pith removed, thinly sliced
  • 1–1½ lb apples, persimmons, pears and/or Asian pears, thinly sliced
  • 1 lemon, halved
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • Aleppo-style pepper (optional)
  • Sumac (optional)
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Fan the sliced oranges (2) and tree fruit (1–1½ lb) across a wide platter or shallow bowl.
  2. Squeeze the lemon (1, halved) over everything, then season with salt and black pepper.
  3. Dust with Aleppo-style pepper or sumac if you like.
  4. Finish with a drizzle of olive oil right before serving.

Assemble at the last minute so the fruit doesn't brown or dry out; leftovers soften but are good next day over yogurt and granola. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Dressing: 1 cup sour cream or Greek yogurt, 1 cup buttermilk, 2 Tbsp lemon juice or white vinegar, 2 grated garlic cloves, whites of 1 bunch scallions (chopped), salt and pepper
  • 1 pint small ripe tomatoes, halved
  • 1 garlic clove, grated
  • 2 Tbsp white or white wine vinegar
  • 4–6 oz blue cheese
  • 1/2 large head iceberg
  • 2 cups mixed soft herbs (chives, parsley, dill)
  • Olive oil

Method

  1. Stir the dressing together (sour cream 1 cup, buttermilk 1 cup, lemon 2 Tbsp, garlic 2 cloves, scallion whites); season assertively.
  2. Slice the scallion greens into long pieces; ice-water them if you want curls.
  3. Marinate the tomatoes (1 pint) with garlic (1 clove) and vinegar (2 Tbsp), salted, until serving.
  4. Cut the iceberg (1/2 head) into wedges and set them on a swoosh of dressing; spoon the tomato juices over.
  5. Top with tomatoes, blue cheese (4–6 oz), scallion tops, herbs (2 cups) and an oil drizzle.

Dressing keeps 5 days – the garlic gets louder as it sits. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 lemons
  • 1/2 cup raw pistachios (or almonds/walnuts), finely chopped
  • 1 garlic clove, grated
  • 1/4 cup olive oil, plus more
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 4–6 heads Little Gem (1 1/2–2 lb), quartered (or romaine/iceberg)
  • 1/2 cup parsley, chopped
  • 1/2 cup chives, chopped
  • 1/4 cup dill, chopped
  • Flaky salt

Method

  1. Finely chop one whole lemon (1), seeds out; zest and juice the other (1) and set that aside.
  2. Toast the nuts (1/2 cup) in a dry skillet until fragrant, 2–3 minutes; cool in a bowl.
  3. Stir the chopped lemon, zest, garlic (1 clove) and oil (1/4 cup) into the nuts; season.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 4 garlic cloves, finely chopped, divided
  • 1 lb cucumbers, mixed varieties
  • Kosher salt
  • 2 Tbsp white, white wine or red wine vinegar, plus more
  • Zest and juice of 1 lemon
  • 1/2 cup chopped dill
  • 1/2 cup chopped cilantro or parsley
  • 1/2 small red onion or 1 shallot, thinly sliced

Method

  1. Warm the oil (1/4 cup) with half the garlic (2 cloves) until lightly golden, ~2 minutes; set aside.
  2. Slice or smash the cucumbers (1 lb); salt well, sit briefly, pour off the liquid.
  3. Add vinegar (2 Tbsp) and the raw garlic (2 cloves); sit ~30 minutes, then adjust.

  5. Finish with the garlicky oil; toss and tweak.

Grain, Bean & Potato Salads

Ingredients

  • 1 cup coarse bulgur
  • 4 large firm vine-ripened tomatoes, cored and diced
  • 5 scallions, trimmed and chopped
  • 1 cup chopped flat-leaf parsley
  • 1 cup chopped mint
  • 1 garlic clove, minced
  • Grated zest of 1 lemon
  • Salt, pepper, 1/2 tsp cinnamon
  • 1/3 cup Greek extra virgin olive oil

Dressing

  • 2/3 cup Greek yogurt
  • 1 Tbsp Greek pine honey
  • 2 tsp Dijon mustard
  • Salt + pinch of cayenne

Method

  1. Soak bulgur (1 cup) in 1 cup cold water several hours, until absorbed.
  2. Toss with all remaining salad ingredients; refrigerate 1 hour.
  3. Whisk the dressing just before serving; serve alongside or drizzled over.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 cups couscous
  • 1 1/2 cups boiling water
  • 1/3 cup extra virgin olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp ground cumin
  • 300 g chickpeas, rinsed and drained
  • 3 cups flat-leaf parsley, chopped
  • 1 cup mint leaves, chopped
  • 6 green onions, finely chopped
  • 3 tomatoes, deseeded and chopped
  • Juice of 2 lemons
  • Salt and pepper

Method

  1. Pour boiling water (1 1/2 cups) over couscous, stir, cover 3 minutes until absorbed; fluff with a fork.
  2. Heat 1 Tbsp oil; toss cumin (1 Tbsp) and chickpeas 5 minutes until warm and light golden.
  3. Add chickpeas (300 g), parsley (3 cups), mint, onions and tomatoes (3) to the couscous (1 1/2 cups); toss gently.
  4. Shake remaining oil, lemon juice, salt and pepper in a jar; pour over, toss and serve.

Ingredients

  • 150 g fresh flat-leaf parsley (~2 bunches), finely chopped
  • 6 spring onions (~1 bunch), finely chopped
  • 4 large tomatoes, finely chopped
  • Juice of 3 lemons, freshly squeezed
  • 5 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 cup bulgur
  • 1 cup boiled water
  • 1/8 tsp each black pepper and salt

Method

  1. Mix bulgur (1 cup) with the boiled water (1 cup); cover the bowl with a tea towel so no steam escapes, and set aside until cool.
  2. Put parsley (150 g), spring onions and tomatoes (4 large) in a large salad bowl.
  3. Add lemon juice, oil, pepper and salt (1/8 tsp); mix well.
  4. Fold in the cooled bulgur. Keeps 2–3 days refrigerated.

Ingredients

  • 4 cups bulgur
  • 5 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil
  • 1 (14-oz) package firm tofu, drained and cubed
  • 5 scallions, chopped, plus more for garnish
  • 1 small tomato, chopped
  • 1 kirby cucumber, chopped
  • 1 Tbsp fresh lemon juice
  • Kosher salt and pepper

Method

  1. Boil bulgur (4 cups) until tender, ~12 minutes. Drain, return to the pan, cover 10 minutes; fluff and spread on a sheet to cool.
  2. In a cast-iron skillet, heat 2 Tbsp oil over high; cook tofu (1 (14-oz) package) and scallions (5 total), stirring, until golden and crisp in spots, ~5 minutes.
  3. Stir in the bulgur, tomato (1 small), cucumber (1), lemon juice (1 Tbsp) and remaining oil; season. Garnish with more scallions.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups short-grain rice (arborio)
  • Salt and pepper
  • 2 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp tahini
  • 3 Tbsp lemon juice + 1 tsp zest
  • 1/2 cup currants, soaked in hot water and drained
  • 1 cup roasted almonds, roughly chopped
  • 1/2 cup roasted pistachios, roughly chopped
  • 3 Tbsp each snipped chives and chopped mint
  • 2 tsp chopped savory or thyme
  • 1/2 cup chopped parsley

Method

  1. Boil rice (2 cups) like pasta in 8 cups water with 2 Tbsp salt, ~15 minutes to al dente. Drain and spread on a sheet to cool.
  2. Stir oil, tahini (2 Tbsp), lemon juice and zest; season. Fold gently into the rice.
  3. Add currants (1/2 cup), nuts and herbs; toss, adjust lemon/salt, serve at room temperature.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 1 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • 1/4 tsp dried oregano
  • 1 clove garlic, chopped to a paste
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 1/4 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • 1 cup quinoa, rinsed
  • 2 cups red and yellow grape tomatoes, halved
  • 1 cup pitted kalamata olives
  • 2 green onions, thinly sliced
  • 2 pickled cherry peppers, diced
  • 1 small red onion, halved and thinly sliced
  • 1/2 English cucumber, small dice
  • Feta, for sprinkling

Method

  1. Whisk lemon juice (3 Tbsp), vinegar (1 Tbsp), oregano, garlic (1 clove), salt and pepper; slowly whisk in oil. Let sit.
  2. Simmer quinoa (1 cup) in 2 cups water with 1 tsp salt and 1/4 tsp pepper until tender, ~15 minutes. Fluff, cool 5 minutes.
  3. Toss with tomatoes (2 cups), olives, onions (2), cherry peppers (2), cucumber and dressing. Chill 1–8 hours — longer is better. Sprinkle feta before serving.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup black rice (Forbidden or Lundberg Black Japonica)
  • 1 1/4 cups water + salt
  • 1/2 cup fresh or frozen edamame
  • 1/2 cup red lentils, soaked 2–3 hours and drained
  • 1/4 cup broken walnuts
  • 1 Tbsp seasoned rice vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp fresh lime juice
  • Pinch of cayenne (optional)
  • 1 tsp chopped or grated fresh ginger
  • Soy sauce to taste (optional)
  • 1 Tbsp dark sesame or walnut oil
  • 2 Tbsp grapeseed oil
  • 2 Tbsp chopped cilantro

Method

  1. Cook the black rice (1/2 cup) in salted water until tender; cool.

  3. Whisk vinegar (1 Tbsp), lime, cayenne, ginger (1 tsp), soy and the oils; toss with rice, lentils, edamame, walnuts (1/4 cup) and cilantro (2 Tbsp).

Ingredients

  • 2 yams or sweet potatoes, 1-inch dice
  • 1 zucchini, 1-inch dice
  • 1 can chickpeas, rinsed and drained
  • 2 garlic cloves
  • 3 sprigs parsley, leaves finely chopped
  • 2 scallions, thinly sliced on the bias
  • Olive oil

Dressing

  • 1 Tbsp tahini
  • Zest and juice of 1 lemon
  • Salt and pepper
  • 1 Tbsp olive oil

Method

  1. Heat oven to 400°F. Roast sweet potatoes (2) on a lined sheet with oil, salt and pepper 35–40 minutes; add zucchini (1) and roast 10–15 more until soft and caramelized (broil 3 minutes if needed).
  2. Sauté chickpeas (1 can) and whole garlic (2 cloves) cloves in 1 Tbsp oil ~3 minutes until warmed and slightly dry. Remove garlic, chop finely, reserve for dressing.
  3. Combine chickpeas, vegetables, parsley (3 sprigs) and scallions (2).
  4. Whisk tahini (1 Tbsp), lemon zest and juice, oil and half the garlic; thin with water by the teaspoon until smooth and runny. Adjust garlic, salt, pepper. Toss; serve warm or room temp.

  • 2 (15-oz) cans cannellini or butter beans, rinsed
  • 1/3 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • 1 small shallot, thinly sliced
  • 1 garlic clove, grated
  • 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 tsp dried oregano
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 3 oz feta, broken into big shards
  • 1 cup basil leaves, torn

Method

Ingredients

  • 3 lb medium Yukon Gold potatoes
  • Salt
  • 1/2 cup extra virgin olive oil
  • 3 Tbsp white wine vinegar
  • 2 tsp coarse-grained Dijon mustard
  • 1 Tbsp caper liquid from the jar
  • 1 clove garlic, finely chopped
  • Pepper
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped chives or green onions
  • 1/3 cup chopped flat-leaf parsley
  • 3 Tbsp finely chopped tarragon
  • 2 Tbsp capers, drained

Method

  1. Cover potatoes (3 lb) by 2 inches with salted water; boil then simmer until tender, ~15 minutes. Drain, cool.
  2. Whisk oil, vinegar (3 Tbsp), mustard (2 tsp), caper liquid, garlic (1 clove), salt and pepper.
  3. Slice potatoes 1/3-inch thick; toss gently with herbs, capers (2 Tbsp) and dressing. Serve at room temperature.

Ingredients

  • 1 large red onion, in 1/3-inch wedges, layers separated
  • 1/2 cup + 2 Tbsp cider vinegar
  • 2 tsp salt
  • 5 lb medium yellow-fleshed potatoes (Yukon Gold)
  • 2–2 1/2 tsp Old Bay seasoning
  • 1 1/4 tsp sugar
  • 3/4 cup extra-virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Toss onion (1 large) with 2 Tbsp vinegar and 1/2 tsp salt; marinate ~45 minutes until pink and slightly soft.
  2. Simmer potatoes (5 lb) in salted water until just tender, 15–20 minutes.
  3. Whisk 2 tsp Old Bay, sugar, remaining 1 1/2 tsp salt and 1/2 cup vinegar.
  4. Peel warm potatoes if desired, cut into 1/2-inch wedges, toss warm with the vinegar mixture. Add onions and oil; more Old Bay to taste. Serve warm or room temp.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 lb fusilli
  • Kosher salt, good olive oil
  • 1 lb ripe tomatoes, medium dice
  • 3/4 cup good black olives (kalamata), pitted and diced
  • 1 lb good feta, medium dice
  • 6 sun-dried tomatoes in oil, drained and chopped

Dressing

  • 5 sun-dried tomatoes in oil, drained
  • 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar
  • 6 Tbsp good olive oil
  • 1 garlic clove, diced
  • 1 tsp capers, drained
  • 1 tsp kosher salt, 3/4 tsp black pepper

Ingredients

  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 lb ridged or curly pasta (fusilli, gemelli)
  • 2 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil, plus more
  • 2 pints cherry or grape tomatoes (~1 1/4 lb)
  • 1/3 cup tahini
  • 3/4 cup finely grated Parmesan + 1/4 cup chopped or shaved
  • 1/2 cup packed mint leaves, torn
  • 3 scallions, thinly sliced
  • 2 Tbsp toasted sesame seeds

Method

  1. Cook pasta in salted water; reserve 1 cup pasta water, drain.
  2. Char tomatoes (2 pints) in oil over medium-high with salt until wrinkly, 4–6 minutes; move to a big bowl.
  3. Stir tahini (1/3 cup) and grated Parmesan (it'll be thick); season, then stir in 1/2 cup pasta water until smooth.
  4. Add pasta to the tomatoes; stir in enough dressing to coat. Cool 15 minutes (or up to 3 hours at room temp).
  5. To serve: stir in the rest of the dressing plus water until glossy, then mint, scallions (3), sesame and chopped Parmesan. Season and drizzle with oil.

Ingredients

  • 3 large sweet peppers (~1 1/2 lb), mixed colors
  • 4 Tbsp olive oil, plus more for drizzling
  • 1 small red onion, thin strips
  • 1 Tbsp minced thyme leaves
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 2 Tbsp capers
  • 2 Tbsp red wine or sherry vinegar
  • 2 cups halved cherry tomatoes, divided
  • 1 lb casarecce or other tubular pasta
  • 1 cup basil, torn
  • 1/2 cup toasted pine nuts, chopped walnuts or slivered almonds
  • 5 oz fresh mozzarella, torn
  • 1 1/2 oz Parmesan, shaved

Method

  1. Halve, seed and slice peppers (3 large) into thin strips (halve long strips).
  2. Sauté peppers, onion (1 small), thyme, salt and pepper in the oil ~6 minutes — soft with a little crunch. Add capers (2 Tbsp) 1 minute, then vinegar; off heat.
  3. Transfer to a shallow dish with 1 cup of the tomatoes to marinate (keeps several days; bring to room temp).
  4. Cook pasta (1 lb) al dente; salt the remaining tomatoes (2 cups total).
  5. Toss pasta with the peperonata; cool to warm. Fold in raw tomatoes with juices, nuts (1/2 cup) and basil, then mozzarella (5 oz) and Parmesan gently at the end so they stay creamy. Adjust seasoning; drizzle with oil. Room temp.

Ingredients

  • 6 cups packed arugula (6 oz), plus leaves for garnish
  • 2 cups Israeli couscous (12 oz)
  • 1/2 cup extra-virgin olive oil, plus more
  • Pine nuts (toasted, per method)
  • 4 garlic cloves, chopped
  • 1/4 cup grated Parmigiano-Reggiano
  • Salt and pepper
  • 1 1/2 pints red cherry tomatoes, halved
  • 4 yellow or orange tomatoes, 1-inch dice

Method

  1. Blanch arugula (6 cups) 10 seconds in boiling salted water; shock and drain.
  2. Cook couscous (2 cups) in the same water until al dente, ~10 minutes. Drain, spread on a sheet, oil lightly to prevent clumping; cool.
  3. Toast pine nuts over medium ~2 minutes; cool.
  4. Squeeze arugula dry, chop, then process with pine nuts, garlic (4 cloves), cheese and the 1/2 cup oil; season.
  5. Stir pesto into couscous, fold in tomatoes (4), garnish with arugula. Holds refrigerated up to 2 hours.

Ingredients

  • 2 (15-oz) cans beans (kidney, black or mixed), rinsed
  • 1 cup corn kernels (canned or thawed)

Pasta-and-chickpea salad, the cold cousin of pasta e ceci.

One of the few recipes still subscriber-only on her site – opens with your login. Paste the text here anytime and it becomes a full card. Open recipe →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup olive oil
  • 1 large red or yellow onion, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 (15-oz) cans chickpeas, drained and rinsed
  • 4 garlic cloves, smashed
  • Pinch of crushed red pepper flakes
  • 2 cups mixed tender herb leaves and stems (parsley, cilantro, dill, mint; mostly parsley and/or cilantro)
  • 2 Tbsp marjoram or oregano leaves (optional)
  • 2 oz feta, thinly sliced or crumbled, or 1 cup seasoned Greek yogurt or labne (optional)
  • Lemon wedges (optional)

Method

  1. Warm the oil (½ cup) in a big skillet over medium-high, add the onion (1, sliced) with salt and pepper, and cook until soft and just starting to color, 5–8 minutes.
  2. Add the chickpeas (2 cans), garlic (4 cloves) and red pepper flakes (pinch), season again, and keep cooking, shaking the pan now and then, until the chickpeas are golden and crisp at the edges and the onion is deep brown, 12–15 minutes.
  3. Taste and adjust salt, pepper and chile.
  4. Tip into a bowl, pile on the herbs (2 cups, plus marjoram if using), and serve with feta (2 oz) or yogurt (1 cup) and lemon wedges.

Can be cooked a few hours ahead and held at room temp, covered; re-crisp in a skillet before serving. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 lb small waxy potatoes (gold creamers, fingerlings or new potatoes)
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 lemon, seeds removed, finely chopped (whole, peel and all)
  • 1 large bunch scallions or spring onions, thinly sliced
  • ½ bunch celery (about 6 stalks), thinly sliced on the diagonal, leaves saved
  • 2 Tbsp white wine vinegar or apple cider vinegar (or celery vinegar), plus more
  • 3 Tbsp whole grain mustard, plus more
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 cup celery or parsley leaves, coarsely chopped
  • 1 cup dill, coarsely chopped

Method

  1. Simmer the potatoes (2 lb) whole in well-salted water until tender, 10–15 minutes (quarter any larger than a golf ball); drain and let cool a bit.
  2. In a large bowl, stir together the chopped lemon (1), scallions (1 bunch), celery (about 6 stalks), vinegar (2 Tbsp) and mustard (3 Tbsp); season hard with salt and pepper so it tastes nearly too salty and sharp.
  3. Crush the warm-ish potatoes by hand or with a tool into the bowl.
  4. Add the olive oil (2 Tbsp) and fold, smashing the potatoes further into the dressing; adjust salt, pepper, vinegar and mustard.
  5. Fold in the celery or parsley leaves (1 cup) and dill (1 cup) just before serving.

Gets better as it sits; good for making ahead. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup (52 g) olive oil, plus more
  • ½ cup (60 g) walnut pieces or almonds, coarsely chopped
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 bunches scallions
  • Crushed red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 1 lb (2 medium) zucchini, thinly sliced
  • 3–4 oz (45–60 g) capers, drained
  • 8 oz rigatoni or other tubular pasta
  • 2–3 garlic cloves, finely grated
  • 1–2 lemons, juiced (or 2 Tbsp/28 g vinegar)
  • 2–3 oz (60–90 g) Parmesan, coarsely chopped
  • 1 cup fresh herbs (parsley, cilantro, dill and/or mint)

Method

  1. Toast the nuts (½ cup) in the oil (¼ cup) over medium heat until deep golden, 2–3 minutes; scoop out and salt them.
  2. Slice a third of the scallions thinly and reserve raw; chop the rest coarsely and cook in the same oil with salt and pepper over medium-high until browned and soft, 8–10 minutes. Add chile flakes if using, then half the zucchini (½ lb) and cook until translucent, 10–15 minutes.
  3. Add the remaining zucchini (½ lb) and capers (3–4 oz) and cook until just wilted but still a bit firm, 5–8 minutes.
  4. Meanwhile boil the pasta (8 oz) in salted water a little past al dente, drain, and rinse with cold water.
  5. Scrape the zucchini mixture into a big bowl; add garlic (2–3 cloves), lemon juice (1–2 lemons) and the raw scallions, season, then toss in the pasta.
  6. Mix in half of the Parmesan (2–3 oz total), nuts and herbs (1 cup total); pile into a serving bowl and top with the rest.

Best at room temperature and improves as it sits. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 12–16 oz short pasta (cavatappi, shells, fusilli)
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 garlic clove, grated
  • 2 lemons, zested and juiced
  • 1 bunch scallions, thinly sliced
  • 1/3 cup olive oil
  • Black pepper
  • 10–12 oz snap peas, halved lengthwise
  • 1–2 cups peas (blanched fresh or thawed frozen)
  • 4 oz Parmesan or pecorino, coarsely chopped
  • 1–2 cups herbs (parsley, basil, tarragon)
  • 1–2 cups pea shoots

Method

  1. Boil the pasta (12–16 oz) to al dente or a touch past; drain and rinse cool.
  2. In your serving bowl: lemon zest and juice (2), garlic (1 clove), scallions (1 bunch), oil (1/3 cup), salt and pepper.
  3. Toss the pasta through the dressing.
  4. Fold in snap peas (10–12 oz), peas (1–2 cups), the chopped cheese (4 oz), herbs and pea shoots.
  5. Correct with more lemon, salt or oil – it drinks seasoning as it sits.

Better the next day. The chopped (not grated) cheese is the move – salty little jackpots. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 cups pearled farro (or barley, freekeh, wheat berries)
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 6 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 1 Tbsp fennel seeds
  • 1 large fennel bulb, thinly sliced, fronds reserved
  • 1 lemon, thinly sliced, seeded
  • Kosher salt, black pepper, pinch red pepper flakes
  • 1 cup basil leaves, torn

Method

  1. Cook the farro (2 cups) per the package; drain.
  2. Fry the garlic (6 cloves) and fennel seeds (1 Tbsp) in the oil (1/4 cup) over medium until the garlic just browns, 3–4 minutes.
  3. Add the sliced fennel (1 bulb) and half the lemon slices with salt and pepper; cook 8–10 minutes until caramelized.
  4. Toss in the farro with a pinch of flakes; coat for a few minutes.
  5. Off heat, add the remaining lemon slices; serve topped with basil (1 cup) and fennel fronds.

Improves as it sits; the plain farro keeps 5 days ahead. Add ricotta salata or toasted nuts at will. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Sides

Ingredients

  • 1 lb green beans, trimmed
  • 1–1 1/2 lb mixed mushrooms (oyster, maitake, baby bella), halved or quartered
  • 2 small red or yellow onions, in 1/2-inch wedges
  • 1/3 cup olive oil, plus more
  • 2 Tbsp soy sauce or tamari
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 Tbsp fresh oregano or marjoram leaves (plus parsley/chives to finish)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Toss beans (1 lb), mushrooms, onions (2 small), oil and soy on a rimmed sheet; season.
  2. Roast ~45 minutes, tossing once or twice (more oil if needed), until mushrooms are deeply browned and beans tender and browned in spots.
  3. Toss with the herbs, more oil and pepper.

Trim beans up to 2 days ahead. Roast a few hours ahead and rewarm, or serve room temp. Leftovers are best cold, or over greens, or refreshed in a hot skillet.
