Ingredients

  • 2 1/2 lb mixed tender greens (spinach, sorrel, dandelion), trimmed, washed
  • 1/4 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil, plus more to finish
  • 2 large onions, halved and sliced
  • 2 leeks, washed and finely chopped
  • 1 1/4 cups snipped wild fennel leaves or mint
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 tsp sweet paprika
  • 1 cup plum tomatoes, peeled and finely chopped
  • 1 1/4 cups snipped fresh dill
  • Salt, pepper, 1 tsp cayenne
  • 1–2 Tbsp tomato paste

Method

  1. Blanch greens until wilted; drain completely.
  2. Soften onions (2 large) and leek in the oil over medium, 5–7 minutes. Add garlic (2 cloves), tomato paste (1–2 Tbsp), cayenne and paprika (2 tsp); stir ~3 minutes.
  3. Add greens, dill (1 1/4 cups), fennel/mint (1 1/4 cups) and tomatoes. Simmer uncovered ~20 minutes until soft and fairly dry.
  4. Adjust salt and cayenne; finish with fresh olive oil (1/4 cup total). Serve hot or warm with country bread.

Ingredients

  • 2 lb carrots, in 2-inch lengths, quartered lengthwise
  • 6 Tbsp water
  • 3 Tbsp fresh lemon juice
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp ground cumin
  • 2 garlic cloves, pressed
  • 2 Tbsp minced fresh cilantro
  • Salt and pepper

Method

  1. Boil carrots (2 lb) with the water and salt, covered, until crisp-tender, ~7 minutes; drain any excess.
  2. Mix in lemon juice (3 Tbsp), oil, cumin and garlic (2 cloves); season. Cool, then add cilantro (2 Tbsp).

Ingredients

  • 1 large head cauliflower (~2 1/2 lb)
  • 5 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil, divided
  • Kosher salt, black pepper
  • 1 oz Parmesan
  • 2 Tbsp capers, drained, divided
  • 1 lemon

Method

  1. Rack in lowest position; oven 450°F. Trim the cauliflower (1 large head) flat, stand it up and cut the whole head into 1/2-inch slices, letting pieces fall.
  2. On a rimmed sheet, coat with 3 Tbsp oil, salt and pepper; arrange on flat cut sides. Roast on the bottom rack until deeply browned underneath, 25–30 minutes.
  3. Meanwhile grate the Parmesan (1 oz) (reserve half for garnish). Chop 1 Tbsp of capers, toss all capers (2 Tbsp total) with remaining 2 Tbsp oil and the juice of the lemon (1).
  4. Flip the pieces, sprinkle with the non-reserved Parm, and roast until the second side browns, 10–12 minutes.
  5. Plate, drizzle with the lemon-caper dressing, season, top with reserved Parm.

Ingredients

  • 1 Tbsp vegetable oil
  • 1 head cauliflower
  • 1 1/2 cups plain Greek yogurt
  • 1 lime, zested and juiced
  • 2 Tbsp chile powder
  • 1 Tbsp cumin
  • 1 Tbsp garlic powder
  • 1 tsp curry powder
  • 2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 tsp black pepper

Method

  1. Heat oven to 400°F; lightly oil a small baking sheet.
  2. Trim the base and leaves. Mix yogurt (1 1/2 cups) with lime zest and juice and all the spices.
  3. Dunk the cauliflower (1 head) and smear the marinade evenly over it (extra keeps 3 days for meat, fish or other veg).
  4. Roast 30–40 minutes until the crust is dry and lightly browned. Rest 10 minutes; cut into wedges and serve with a big green salad.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup + 1 tsp olive oil, separated
  • 1/3 cup white wine (or white vinegar)
  • 1/3 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1/4 cup each seedless green and red grapes
  • 3 slices halloumi
  • 1 Tbsp julienned mint
  • Pomegranate syrup

Method

  1. Combine 1/4 cup oil, wine and pepper flakes (1/3 tsp). Halve the grapes (1/4 cup).
  2. Brush halloumi (3 slices) with 1 tsp oil; griddle over medium-high 2–3 minutes per side until marked and warm.
  3. Sauté grapes with the oil mixture and mint (1 Tbsp) ~5 minutes until warm and fragrant.
  4. Plate the halloumi, top with grapes and the reduction; finish with pomegranate syrup.

Ingredients

  • 2 lb carrots, peeled, halved lengthwise
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil, plus more to finish
  • 1 Tbsp za'atar, plus more for dusting
  • 1 tsp honey
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 cup labneh (or thick Greek yogurt whisked with a pinch of salt)
  • 1 small garlic clove, grated
  • 2 Tbsp chopped pistachios
  • Squeeze of lemon; mint or cilantro leaves

Method

  1. Heat oven to 450°F. Toss carrots (2 lb) with oil, za'atar, honey (1 tsp), salt and pepper; roast cut-side down 20–25 minutes until deeply browned and tender.
  2. Stir garlic (1 small clove) into the labneh (1 cup); swoosh over a platter.
  3. Pile hot carrots on top; scatter pistachios (2 Tbsp) and herbs, squeeze lemon over, dust with more za'atar and a thread of oil.

Same platter logic as the sweet potato–tahini salad: cold creamy base, hot vegetables, crunch on top.

Ingredients

  • 2 large eggplants, halved lengthwise, flesh scored in a diamond pattern
  • 1/3 cup olive oil
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1/3 cup tahini
  • 3 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 1 small garlic clove, grated
  • 3–4 Tbsp ice water
  • Seeds of 1/2 pomegranate
  • 2 Tbsp toasted pine nuts or chopped walnuts
  • Handful of mint and parsley leaves
  • Pinch of Aleppo pepper or red pepper flakes

Method

  1. Heat oven to 450°F. Brush the scored eggplant faces generously with oil, season, and roast cut-side up 35–45 minutes until deeply bronzed and completely soft.
  2. Whisk tahini (1/3 cup), lemon, garlic, salt and ice water (3–4 Tbsp) to a pourable cream.
  3. Spoon tahini over the warm halves; shower with pomegranate, nuts, herbs and Aleppo. Serve warm or room temperature.

Ingredients

  • 2 medium zucchini, sliced 1/4-inch
  • 2 medium eggplants, sliced 1/4-inch
  • 1 1/2 lb Yukon Gold potatoes, sliced 1/4-inch
  • 2 red onions, thinly sliced
  • 4 ripe tomatoes, grated (skins discarded), or 1 (28-oz) can crushed
  • 4 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 2/3 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp dried Greek oregano
  • 1/2 cup chopped parsley or dill
  • Kosher salt and black pepper

Method

  1. Heat oven to 375°F. Toss everything together in a large baking dish with the oil, tomatoes (4) and herbs; season generously and spread evenly.
  2. Roast uncovered 60–75 minutes, stirring once halfway, until the vegetables are collapsed, the juices thick and the top edges caramelized.
  3. Rest at least 20 minutes; serve warm or room temp with feta and bread. Better the next day.

Sits naturally next to the gigantes and spinach-bean casserole for a Greek table.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 lb rutabagas, peeled, 1/2-inch pieces
  • 2 large carrots, peeled, thinly sliced
  • 1 large garlic clove, peeled
  • 1 bay leaf
  • 1 1/2 lb russet potatoes, peeled, 1-inch pieces
  • 3 Tbsp butter
  • 2 Tbsp chopped fresh parsley

Method

  1. Boil rutabagas (1 1/2 lb), carrots, garlic and bay in salted water until almost tender, ~20 minutes. Add potatoes (1 1/2 lb); cook until everything is very tender, ~15 more. Drain; discard bay.
  2. Stir over medium heat ~2 minutes to dry. Add butter (3 Tbsp); mash until almost smooth. Mix in 1 Tbsp parsley, season.
  3. Holds 2 hours at room temp; rewarm over low, stirring. Sprinkle with remaining parsley (2 Tbsp).

Ingredients

  • 6 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 4 cups finely sliced onions
  • 5 lb Yukon Gold potatoes, peeled
  • Salt and pepper, to taste

Method

  1. Heat oven to 400°F. Slowly sweat the onions (4 cups) in butter in a large pot until tender and translucent, no browning.
  2. Slice potatoes (5 lb) 1–2 mm thick; mix with the onions until coated. Pepper lightly; salt until you just taste it over the onion sweetness.
  3. Line a 10-inch cast-iron skillet or heavy baking dish with parchment; heap in the potatoes. Cover with parchment, wrap in foil, bake on a sheet pan 1 3/4–2 hours (uncover for the last 10). A skewer should meet no resistance.
  4. Cool 45 minutes, press the top flat, then refrigerate 5–6 hours or overnight.
  5. To serve: 400°F oven. Either invert, trim, cut into 12 slices and bake ~10 minutes until golden and hot — or heat in the pan 20 minutes and slice at the table.

Ingredients

  • Kosher salt
  • 1/2 tsp (4 g) baking soda
  • 4 lb russet or Yukon Gold potatoes, peeled, cut in quarters/sixths/eighths by size
  • 5 Tbsp (75 ml) extra-virgin olive oil, duck fat or beef fat
  • Small handful rosemary leaves, finely chopped
  • 3 medium cloves garlic, minced
  • Black pepper
  • Small handful fresh parsley, minced

Method

  1. Oven 450°F (400°F convection). Boil 2 quarts water with 2 Tbsp salt and the baking soda (1/2 tsp); add potatoes (4 lb), return to boil, simmer until a knife meets little resistance, ~10 minutes.
  2. Meanwhile heat the fat with rosemary, garlic (3 medium cloves) and pepper over medium, stirring, until the garlic just turns golden, ~3 minutes. Strain into a large bowl; reserve the garlic-rosemary solids.
  3. Drain potatoes, rest 30 seconds to steam off, then toss roughly in the infused oil with salt and pepper until a mashed-potato paste coats the chunks.
  4. Spread out on a large rimmed sheet. Roast 20 minutes without moving, then release with a thin spatula, turn, and keep roasting until deep brown and crisp all over, 30–40 minutes more, turning a few times.
  5. Toss with the reserved garlic-rosemary mixture and parsley; season and serve immediately.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 lb small yellow potatoes
  • Kosher salt
  • 4 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1/2 tsp cumin seeds or coriander seeds, lightly crushed
  • 1 cup Greek yogurt
  • 1/2 cup soft herbs (dill, cilantro, chives), chopped
  • 1 small garlic clove, grated
  • 1 Tbsp lemon juice
  • Flaky salt, black pepper, pinch of Aleppo or red pepper flakes

Method

  1. Boil potatoes (1 1/2 lb) in well-salted water until completely tender, 15–20 minutes; drain and cool slightly.
  2. Heat oven to 450°F. On an oiled sheet, smash each potato flat with a mug; drizzle with oil, scatter the crushed seeds (1/2 tsp), season.
  3. Roast 25–35 minutes without turning, until the edges are shattering-crisp.
  4. Stir yogurt (1 cup), herbs, garlic and lemon; spread on a platter, pile potatoes on top, finish with flaky salt, pepper and chile.

Ingredients

  • 4 lb russet potatoes, peeled
  • 1 Tbsp kosher salt, plus more
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 2 onions, thinly sliced
  • 1 Tbsp distilled white vinegar
  • Black pepper, flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Parboil whole potatoes (4 lb) in salted water until a paring knife enters with some resistance, 15–20 minutes. Drain; chill uncovered on a sheet at least 30 minutes.
  2. Racks at highest and lowest positions; oven 425°F. Line two 8-inch cake pans with parchment; brush with 2 Tbsp oil. Toss onions (2), vinegar (1 Tbsp) and 1 Tbsp salt; soften 5–10 minutes (massage to speed it up).
  3. Grate the potatoes (box grater, processor or julienne). Heat the pans on the lower rack 5 minutes.
  4. Toss potatoes with the onions; season. Divide between the hot pans, pressing down firmly. Bake on the lower rack until very brown at the edges, 45–60 minutes. Cool slightly.
  5. Broil the tops until golden, then invert onto a rack, peel off parchment, brush with oil, sprinkle flaky salt (1 Tbsp total) and broil the second side deep golden.

Parboil a day ahead. The finished rösti holds 3 hours (or 1 hour in a 250°F oven); reheat at 300°F for 10 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 6 1/4 lb Yukon Gold potatoes
  • 1/2 cup + 2 Tbsp butter
  • 2 1/2 cups Parmesan cheese
  • 1 1/4 cups chopped fresh chives
  • 1 3/4 cups + 2 Tbsp cream cheese
  • 5/8 medium head garlic, peeled and minced
  • Salt and pepper to taste

Method

  1. Boil potatoes (6 1/4 lb) in salted water until tender but still firm; drain and dry over low heat 1–2 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 2 1/2 lb potatoes (~6 large), ideally russet + Yukon Gold mix, or all Yukon
  • 4 Tbsp butter, more for dotting
  • 1/3 cup whole milk
  • Salt

Method

  1. Peel and quarter potatoes (hold in cold water up to 4 hours). Boil in 1 gallon water + 2 Tbsp salt until soft, 15–20 minutes — better over than under.
  2. Heat butter (4 Tbsp) and milk (1/3 cup) together until steaming.
  3. Drain well, shake dry in the hot pan a minute, then mash with a ricer or masher until smooth. Mix in half the butter mixture, then more to taste with salt.
  4. Fluffy: stop there. Creamy: keep stirring, pressing against the pot until dense and thick. Whipped: stand mixer 30 seconds, then all the butter in short medium bursts — stop the instant it's light (it turns gluey fast).
  5. Keeps warm 30+ minutes covered and dotted with butter; longer over an inch of simmering water.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb dried black beans, soaked overnight
  • 4 cloves garlic, minced
  • 1 large onion, diced
  • 1/2 tsp ground cumin
  • Salt and black pepper
  • 1 Tbsp unsalted butter

Method

  1. Drain beans (1 lb); cover with 2 inches cold water. Add garlic (4 cloves), onion, cumin (1/2 tsp), salt and pepper. Simmer until tender and the liquid is a thick sauce, 1 1/2–2 hours.
  2. Melt butter (1 Tbsp) in a skillet, pour in the beans, mash (leave some texture) and cook over medium 3–4 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 12 oz very ripe tomatoes, cored (or canned — see note)
  • 1 medium white onion
  • 3 medium jalapeños
  • 2 cups long grain white rice
  • 1/3 cup canola oil
  • 4 minced garlic cloves
  • 2 cups chicken broth (or veg broth)
  • 1 Tbsp tomato paste (omit if using canned tomatoes)
  • 1 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 cup fresh cilantro, minced
  • 1 lime

Method

  1. Oven 350°F, rack in middle. Purée tomato and onion (1 medium); reserve exactly 2 cups (top up with salsa if short).
  2. Seed and mince 2 jalapeños; mince the third (seeds in) separately.
  3. Rinse rice (2 cups) in a fine strainer until the water runs clear, ~1 1/2 minutes; shake dry. Skipping this = sticky rice.
  4. Heat oil in an ovensafe 12-inch straight-sided pan or Dutch oven over low-medium ~2 minutes (grains should sizzle). Fry rice, stirring, until light golden and translucent, 6–8 minutes.
  5. Medium heat: add garlic (4 cloves) and the 2 seeded jalapeños, stir 1 1/2 minutes. Stir in broth (2 cups), purée, tomato paste (1 Tbsp) and salt; bring to a boil.
  6. Cover, bake 30–35 minutes until tender, stirring well at 15.
  7. Stir in cilantro (1/2 cup) and the reserved jalapeño to taste; pass lime (1) wedges.

Freezes well; reheat covered in the microwave, stirring every 2–3 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 1 large onion, finely chopped
  • 2 garlic cloves, finely chopped
  • 3 scallions, trimmed, chopped
  • 1 medium fennel bulb, trimmed and chopped
  • 1 cup long- or medium-grain Greek rice
  • 1 1/2 lb fresh spinach, stemmed, coarsely chopped
  • 1/2 bunch fresh dill, snipped
  • Salt and pepper
  • ~2/3 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil
  • 1–2 fresh lemons
  • Feta or Greek yogurt, for serving

Method

  1. Heat 1/2 cup oil in a wide pot; sauté onion (1 large), scallions (3) and fennel over medium-low until soft, ~10 minutes. Stir in garlic (2 cloves), then the rice (1 cup) to coat.
  2. Add spinach (1 1/2 lb) in batches, stirring each down. Season.
  3. Add 2 cups water or vegetable stock; cook covered over low until the rice is very tender, ~45 minutes, adding liquid if needed.
  4. Stir in dill (1/2 bunch) for the last 5 minutes. Off heat, stir in remaining oil, lemon juice, salt and pepper. Serve with feta or yogurt.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup coarse bulgur (~200 g)
  • 2 medium tomatoes, roughly chopped (or 1 400-g tin)
  • 1 medium onion, finely chopped
  • 1 green or red pepper, deseeded, finely chopped
  • 1 1/2 cups hot water or stock
  • 1 tsp red pepper paste or tomato paste
  • Salt and pepper; olive oil

Method

  1. Sauté onion (1 medium) and pepper (1) in a little oil over low until the onion sweats. Add tomato; soften a couple of minutes.
  2. Stir in the paste (1 tsp), then the bulgur (1 cup); stir 2 minutes.
  3. High heat: add liquid, salt and pepper; bring to a boil. Lid on, medium-low, simmer 8–10 minutes until absorbed.
  4. Rest covered 5+ minutes; stir and serve.

Ingredients

  • 2 tsp olive oil
  • 1/2 onion, chopped
  • 1 cup quinoa
  • 2 cups water
  • 2 Tbsp vegetarian chicken-flavored bouillon granules
  • 1 tsp black pepper
  • 1 tsp thyme
  • 1 carrot, chopped
  • 1 tomato, chopped
  • 1 cup baby spinach

Method

  1. Soften onion (1/2) in oil over medium, ~5 minutes. Lower heat, add quinoa (1 cup), toast 2 minutes, stirring.

  3. Stir in carrots; cover and simmer until the water is absorbed, ~10 minutes.
  4. Off heat, stir in tomato (1) and spinach (1 cup) until wilted, ~2 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 4 lb russet potatoes, peeled, sliced 1/8-inch (mandoline)
  • 2 cups heavy cream

  • 3 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 Tbsp thyme leaves
  • Kosher salt and pepper; butter for the dish

Method

Ingredients

  • 2 eggs
  • 4 Tbsp melted butter, plus more for the dish
  • 1/4 cup sugar
  • 1/2 cup flour + 1/2 cup cornmeal

Method

Ingredients

Ingredients

  • 2 lb spinach, stemmed and washed well (or 1 lb baby spinach)
  • 2 Tbsp extra virgin olive oil
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 can diced tomatoes in juice (or 1 1/4 lb fresh, peeled, seeded, chopped)
  • 1 cup vegetable stock, garlic broth or water
  • 2–3 Tbsp lemon juice, to taste
  • 3 Tbsp long grain or basmati rice, well rinsed (or 1/2 cup cooked brown rice)
  • Salt, 2 tsp paprika, 1/2 tsp cinnamon
  • 3/4–1 tsp sugar, to taste

Method

  1. Warm the garlic (2 cloves) in the oil until fragrant; add tomatoes, paprika, cinnamon, sugar (3/4–1 tsp) and salt and cook down 5–8 minutes to a saucy base.
  2. Pile in the spinach (2 lb) in batches, turning it through until wilted.
  3. Stir in the rice (3 Tbsp) and stock; cover and simmer gently until the rice is tender and the mixture is juicy but not soupy, ~20 minutes (if using cooked rice, just 5).
  4. Off heat, add lemon juice (2–3 Tbsp) to taste. Serve warm or room temp — yogurt alongside is right at home here.

Same family as the tsigarelli and spanakorizo already on the board. Original →

Ingredients

  • 4 lb russet potatoes (5–7), peeled
  • 1 large yellow onion
  • 6 large eggs
  • 10 Tbsp melted chicken fat — use vegetable oil to keep it vegetarian
  • Kosher salt and plenty of black pepper
  • 1/3 cup finely chopped chives; flaky salt

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Grate the potatoes (4 lb) and onion (box grater or processor) into a colander.
  2. Squeeze the shreds absolutely as dry as you can, handful by handful (a kitchen towel helps); dry potatoes = crisp kugel.
  3. Mix with the eggs (6 large), 6 Tbsp of the fat, salt and lots of pepper.
  4. Heat 2 Tbsp fat in a 9–10-inch cast-iron skillet over medium-high; add the mixture loosely — don't pack it, keep it airy.
  5. Cook on the stovetop, rotating the pan, until golden at the edges and up the sides, 10–12 minutes.
  6. Drizzle the last 2 Tbsp fat over the top and bake until deeply golden, wispy-crisp at the edges and fully tender inside, 45–50 minutes.
  7. More pepper, chives and flaky salt; slice warm.

Ingredients

  • 1 large loaf crusty sourdough or ciabatta (~1 1/4 lb), torn into 1 1/2–2-inch pieces and staled overnight (or dried at 300°F, 20–30 min)
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 1 bunch lacinato kale, stemmed, torn
  • 8 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 6 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 2 large leeks and 6 celery stalks, thinly sliced (celery leaves reserved)
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 cup dry white wine (or broth + 1 Tbsp white wine vinegar)
  • 2 Tbsp finely chopped sage, oregano or thyme
  • 2 1/2 cups vegetable broth
  • 3 large eggs
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped parsley and/or chives

Method

  1. Heat oven to 375°F. Wilt the kale (1 bunch) in the olive oil with salt and pepper; move it to your big mixing bowl.
  2. In the same skillet, melt the butter (8 Tbsp); cook celery (2 large stalks) and leeks with salt and pepper until completely soft, 8–10 minutes. Garlic (6 cloves) for a minute or two, then the wine — reduce by three-quarters. Stir in the sage (2 Tbsp); off heat.
  3. Whisk broth (2 1/2 cups) and eggs in the big bowl, add the vegetables, then the bread and kale; toss in stages so the bread drinks the liquid without being crushed.
  4. Transfer loosely to a 9×13 (don't pack it). Dot with butter, especially the corners; flaky salt and pepper over.
  5. Cover with foil; bake 25–30 minutes until sizzling and cooked through. Then uncover at 425°F until impossibly golden and crisp on top, ~20 minutes. Celery leaves to finish.
  6. James's spec: the original's 2 lb sausage is omitted — a pound of hard-browned mushrooms is the best stand-in if you want the bulk.

Ingredients

  • 1 large head cauliflower, in florets
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 3/4 cup pitted olives (Castelvetrano or kalamata), halved
  • 3/4 cup coarsely grated Parmesan
  • Zest of 1 lemon; parsley; red pepper flakes

Method

  1. Roast the oiled, seasoned florets at 425°F for 20 minutes until browning at the edges.
  2. Toss, scatter the olives (3/4 cup) around, then the Parmesan (3/4 cup) directly over and onto the pan.
  3. Roast 10–12 more minutes until the cheese fuses into crisp frico bits.
  4. Scrape it all up; finish with lemon zest, parsley and flakes.

Ingredients

  • 1 large head cauliflower, in bite-size florets
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 1 tsp ground cumin
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 3 oz feta, crumbled
  • 1/3 cup toasted almonds, roughly chopped
  • 1/3 cup green or kalamata olives, chopped
  • Juice of 1/2 lemon; parsley or dill

Method

  1. Toss the florets with oil, cumin (1 tsp), salt and pepper; roast at 425°F until deeply browned, 25–30 minutes.
  2. While hot, toss with feta (3 oz), almonds (1/3 cup), olives (1/3 cup) and lemon juice — the feta softens against the hot florets.
  3. Herbs over; serve warm or room temp.

Ingredients

  • 5 lb squash (butternut, acorn or kabocha), peeled, seeded, sliced ¼ inch thick
  • 4 oz Parmesan, grated (about 1 cup)
  • 8 garlic cloves, very well smashed, chopped or thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt
  • Lots of freshly ground black pepper
  • Crushed red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 4–6 sprigs sage or 2–3 Tbsp thyme, rosemary or oregano leaves
  • 1 cup heavy cream
  • 2 Tbsp butter or olive oil, plus more olive oil

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F (220°C, no fan). Build layers of squash (5 lb), Parmesan (about ¾ of 4 oz), garlic (8 cloves) and sage (4–6 sprigs) in a 1.5–2 qt dish, seasoning each layer with salt, pepper and chile flakes.
  2. Pour the cream (1 cup) over the top, scatter on the remaining Parmesan, more pepper and herbs, and dot with butter or oil (2 Tbsp).
  3. Cover with foil and bake until the squash is tender, 30–35 minutes.
  4. Uncover and bake until the top is browned and the cream caramelizes at the edges, another 30–35 minutes; rest briefly and serve warm.

Assemble through the covered stage hours ahead, then bake uncovered when ready; leftovers are great cold or topped with a fried egg. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Bread and fat

  • 1 large crusty loaf, ideally sourdough or ciabatta (about 1¼ lb)
  • ¼ cup olive oil
  • 8 Tbsp unsalted butter, plus more

Vegetables

  • 6 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 2 large leeks (white and light green), chopped, or 2 yellow onions, thinly sliced
  • 6–8 celery stalks, thinly sliced, leaves saved
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper

Liquid and seasoning

  • ½ cup dry white wine (or 1 Tbsp vinegar plus ½ cup water)
  • ½ tsp red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 2½ cups low-sodium vegetable broth
  • 3 large eggs
  • ½ cup finely chopped parsley or celery leaves
  • ¼ cup finely chopped chives
  • 2 Tbsp finely chopped marjoram, oregano or thyme

Method

  1. Tear the loaf (about 1¼ lb) into 1½–2 inch pieces and leave out 12–24 hours to dry, or crisp them in a 300°F oven.
  2. Heat the oven to 375°F. Melt olive oil (¼ cup) with 4 Tbsp of the butter in a large skillet and soften garlic (6 cloves) and leeks (2) with salt and pepper until bright green and tender, 8–10 minutes; add celery (6–8 stalks) and cook 4–6 minutes more.
  3. Pour in the wine (½ cup) and boil down by about three-quarters, 3–4 minutes; add chile flakes if using and take off the heat.
  4. Whisk the broth (2½ cups) with the eggs (3); in a big bowl combine bread, vegetable mixture, parsley (½ cup), chives (¼ cup) and marjoram (2 Tbsp), then pour on the egg mixture in stages, tossing gently until absorbed.
  5. Transfer loosely to a 2½–3 qt dish, dot with extra butter, cover with foil and bake 25–30 minutes.
  6. Uncover, raise the oven to 425°F, and bake until deep golden and crisp, 20–25 minutes; scatter with celery leaves.

Original allows chicken, turkey or vegetable broth; vegetable broth used here. Can be assembled 1–2 days ahead; reheat leftovers in a skillet in the oven to re-crisp. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3 lb small waxy potatoes (golf-ball size)
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 bunch scallions or 1 large leek, thinly sliced
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 1½ cups heavy cream
  • Olive oil, for drizzling
  • 1½ cups mixed herbs (dill, parsley, chervil, chives, tarragon)
  • Flaky salt, for finishing

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F. Boil the potatoes (3 lb) in salted water until fork-tender, 15–20 minutes; drain and cool.
  2. Press each potato lightly with the flat of a knife to crush, then layer in a 1.5 qt dish with about three-quarters of the scallions (1 bunch).
  3. Pour over the cream (1½ cups), season with salt and pepper, and drizzle with oil.
  4. Bake until the cream browns at the edges and the potatoes crisp, 15–20 minutes; finish with flaky salt, the remaining scallions and the herbs (1½ cups).

Potatoes can be boiled and crushed hours ahead; leftovers are good cold or reheated at 425°F. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3 lb winter squash (about 2 small; acorn, butternut, honeynut or kabocha), quartered lengthwise, seeds removed
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 Tbsp brown sugar, divided
  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 8 garlic cloves, well smashed
  • ½ bunch sage (or thyme, marjoram or oregano)

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 275°F. Set the squash (3 lb) in a roasting pan, coat with olive oil (2 Tbsp), season with salt and pepper, and sprinkle the cut sides with some of the brown sugar (2 Tbsp total); turn skin side up.
  2. Tuck garlic (8 cloves) and sage (½ bunch) around the squash and lay butter (4 Tbsp) on top.
  3. Roast, untouched, until the squash is meltingly soft and collapsing, about 2½–3 hours; the garlic turns jammy and the sage crisps.
  4. Serve in or out of the skins, spooning over the garlic, sage and browned butter-sugar from the pan.

Rewarm gently ahead of time; reheat leftovers in a skillet to bring back the crisp bits. Seeds can be roasted with oil and salt at 425°F for 20–25 min. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 8 garlic cloves
  • 1 lemon, seeds removed, thinly sliced, plus 1 lemon, halved (for juicing/chopping)
  • ¼ cup olive oil
  • 1 medium shallot, thinly sliced lengthwise
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 tsp crushed red pepper flakes, plus more
  • 2 lb green beans or haricots verts, stems removed
  • ¼ cup coarsely chopped parsley

Method

  1. Finely chop 2 of the garlic cloves and mix with a finely chopped half lemon in a small bowl; slice the remaining garlic (6 cloves) and lemon (1) thinly.
  2. Warm the oil (¼ cup) in a large pot or skillet and cook the shallot (1) and sliced garlic with salt and pepper until tender and browned, 10–12 minutes; add the lemon slices and chile flakes (1 tsp) and cook until the lemon browns, 3–5 minutes.
  3. Stir in the beans (2 lb) to coat, season, pour in about 1 cup water, and cook, stirring, until they turn deep olive green and soft, 8–10 minutes.
  4. Off the heat, fold in the raw garlic-lemon mixture and let sit at least 30 minutes; top with parsley (¼ cup) and a squeeze of lemon.

Can be made a day ahead; best eaten cold or room temperature, not reheated. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 6 scallions, thinly sliced, plus more for serving
  • ½ cup dill, coarsely chopped, plus more for serving
  • 2 garlic cloves, finely grated or chopped
  • 3 Tbsp white wine vinegar or white distilled vinegar, plus more
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1½–2 lb zucchini, quartered lengthwise
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Stir together scallions (6), dill (½ cup), garlic (2 cloves) and vinegar (3 Tbsp) with salt and pepper, adding more vinegar until saucy; set aside.
  2. Heat a grill to medium-high. Rub the zucchini (1½–2 lb) with oil and season with salt and pepper.
  3. Grill cut side down until charred, 6–8 minutes, then turn to char the other sides, 10–15 minutes total.
  4. Pile onto a platter, spoon the scallion-dill mixture over, drizzle with oil, and top with extra scallions and dill.

Serve right away so it doesn't get soggy; the topping can be made hours ahead. Can also be roasted at 500°F or seared in a skillet. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2–2½ lb small to medium carrots (tops saved if available)
  • ¼ cup olive oil or 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 1 tsp freshly ground black pepper
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 cup coarsely chopped dill (about 1 small bunch), plus more for serving

Method

  1. Cut the carrots (2–2½ lb) into 2–3 inch lengths, splitting thick ones lengthwise, or slice into ½ inch coins.
  2. Heat the oil or butter in a large skillet over medium-high, add the pepper (1 tsp) and let it sizzle 30–45 seconds.
  3. Add the carrots, season with salt, and cook, stirring now and then, until just tender with some bite, 6–8 minutes.
  4. Off the heat, toss with dill (1 cup) and chopped carrot tops if you have them; top with more dill.

Original also lists chicken or duck fat; olive oil or butter used here. Best fresh; carrot tops can be swapped for parsley. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ lb green beans, stems trimmed
  • Kosher salt
  • ¼ cup soy sauce or tamari (use a little extra in place of the fish sauce)
  • 2 Tbsp fresh lime or lemon juice, plus more
  • 6 scallions, thinly sliced
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • Olive oil, toasted sesame oil and/or chili oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Blanch the beans (½ lb) in heavily salted water until bright green but still crunchy, about 90 seconds; taste one to check.
  2. Drain into a bowl; halve any thick beans lengthwise if you like so they take up dressing.
  3. While still warm, toss with soy sauce (¼ cup), citrus juice (2 Tbsp) and over half the scallions (6 total); season with salt and pepper and balance so it's equally tart and salty.
  4. Drizzle with oil and top with the remaining scallions.

Original includes 2 tsp fish sauce, omitted here (optional seasoning). Improves over time and keeps up to 2 days, though the color fades. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup plus 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 medium leeks (about 1½ lb), white and green parts thinly sliced, rinsed
  • 1 lb kale (curly or Tuscan) or Swiss chard (1 large or 2 medium bunches), thick stems removed, leaves torn
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • Crushed red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 1½ cups heavy cream
  • 1 cup finely grated Parmesan, pecorino, white cheddar or Gruyère
  • 1½ cups fresh coarse bread crumbs or 1 cup panko

Method

  1. Cook the leeks (2) in ¼ cup of the oil in a big skillet over medium-high with salt and pepper until bright green and nearly melting, 10–15 minutes.
  2. Add the kale (1 lb) by handfuls, seasoning as you go, until all wilted, 5–8 minutes.
  3. Add chile flakes if using and the cream (1½ cups), scraping up browned bits; take off the heat, stir in the cheese (1 cup), and transfer to a 2 qt baking dish.
  4. Heat the oven to 425°F. Toss the bread crumbs (1½ cups) with the remaining oil (2 Tbsp), salt and pepper, scatter over the top, and bake until golden and bubbling, 15–20 minutes.

The leek-greens mixture and crumbs can be made a day ahead and chilled; good cold or reheated at 350°F. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 lb squash (honeynut, butternut, kabocha or acorn), quartered, seeds removed if desired, unpeeled
  • 6 Tbsp olive oil, divided
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 6 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 1 cup (4 oz) walnut halves or pieces (or pecans, hazelnuts or almonds), coarsely chopped
  • 4 oz dates (about 9), pitted and torn in half
  • 2 Tbsp thyme, marjoram or oregano leaves
  • Flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F. Put the squash (2 lb) cut side down on a rimmed sheet, coat with 4 Tbsp of the oil, season, and roast without turning until soft and deeply caramelized underneath, 45–50 minutes.
  2. Melt the butter (6 Tbsp) in a small pot over medium until foamy, then add the remaining oil (2 Tbsp) and the nuts (1 cup).
  3. Stir constantly until the nuts toast and the butter browns, 4–6 minutes; off the heat, stir in the dates (4 oz) and season.
  4. Set the squash on a platter, spoon the nut mixture over, and top with herbs (2 Tbsp), flaky salt and pepper.

Works with sweet potatoes too. The brown butter mixture can be made hours ahead and gently rewarmed; squash can be roasted ahead and rewarmed. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup olive oil
  • 3 large shallots, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 lb green beans, trimmed
  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 1 large bunch kale or Swiss chard, thick stems removed, leaves torn into bite-size pieces
  • 2 Tbsp apple cider vinegar, white wine vinegar or fresh lemon juice, plus more
  • Flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Cook the shallots (3) in the oil (½ cup) in a large skillet over medium until light brown and crisping, 5–6 minutes; lift onto paper towels and season.
  2. Pour half the oil into a small bowl to save. Return the pan to medium-high, add the beans (2 lb) with salt and pepper, and char without stirring 3–4 minutes, then toss and cook until tender, 3–4 more minutes.
  3. Add the butter (4 Tbsp), season, then toss in the greens (1 bunch) until wilted, 2–3 minutes.
  4. Off the heat, add the vinegar or lemon (2 Tbsp), adjust seasoning, and move to a platter; drizzle with the reserved shallot oil and top with the fried shallots and flaky salt.

Ingredients

  • 1 (3- to 4-pound) winter squash (kabocha, red kuri, or butternut)
  • ⅓ cup olive oil, plus more for drizzling
  • ¼ cup maple syrup, plus more for drizzling
  • 1 tablespoon hot smoked paprika
  • 2 teaspoons red-pepper flakes
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • ¾ cup hazelnuts, coarsely chopped
  • Flaky sea salt
  • 1 lemon

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F. Split the squash (1, 3–4 lb) lengthwise, scoop out the seeds if you like, and cut into wedges about 1½ inches thick.
  2. On a rimmed sheet pan, coat the wedges with olive oil (⅓ cup), maple syrup (¼ cup), smoked paprika (1 Tbsp) and pepper flakes (2 tsp); season with kosher salt and pepper.
  3. Roast 40–45 minutes, flipping once, until browned at the edges and soft throughout.
  4. While it roasts, toast the hazelnuts (¾ cup) in a dry skillet over medium heat, stirring often, until golden, 4–6 minutes.
  5. Pile the squash on a platter, drizzle with extra oil and maple syrup, and scatter the hazelnuts, flaky salt and pepper. Zest the lemon (1) over the top and serve with the halves for squeezing.

Skin can stay on; works with sweet potatoes or carrots too. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup unsalted butter or olive oil (original also allows chicken fat; omitted here)
  • ¼ cup olive oil
  • Pinch of red-pepper flakes (optional)
  • 2 bunches carrots (about 1 pound), tops removed, thinly sliced into rounds
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 garlic clove, finely chopped or grated

Method

  1. Warm the fat (¼ cup butter or olive oil) in a large skillet over medium-high; if using butter, let it foam and start to brown, 2–3 minutes.
  2. Stir in the olive oil (¼ cup) and a pinch of pepper flakes so they bloom in the fat.
  3. Add the sliced carrots (2 bunches, ~1 lb), season with salt and pepper, and toss now and then until just tender, 3–4 minutes.
  4. Off the heat, toss in the garlic (1 clove) and move to a bowl.

Ingredients

  • 3 pounds russet potatoes (about 4–6), peeled
  • 1 pound onions (about 2 medium or 1 large), peeled
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • ¼ cup fine plain dried breadcrumbs, panko, or matzo meal (or 2 tablespoons potato starch)
  • 1–2 large eggs
  • Neutral oil (canola or grapeseed)

Optional toppings

  • Sour cream
  • Apple sauce
  • Chives

Method

  1. Grate the potatoes (3 lb) and onions (1 lb) on the big holes of a box grater, salt them, and leave 5–10 minutes.
  2. Squeeze the mixture in handfuls over a colander, repeating until as dry as you can get it.
  3. Stir in the breadcrumbs or matzo meal (¼ cup) and 1 egg, then season with salt and pepper.
  4. Heat a thin layer of neutral oil in a large skillet over medium-high. Fry a small test spoonful; if it crumbles, mix in the second egg (1).
  5. Spoon in loose mounds without packing and fry about 2 minutes per side until deep golden; drain on paper towels and repeat with the rest.
  6. Serve with sour cream, apple sauce and chives.

Original also suggests schmaltz and fish roe, both omitted. Keep warm on a wire rack (no paper towel) in a 375°F oven, but don't hold too long. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 large loaf crusty bread (sourdough or ciabatta, about 1¼ pounds)
  • ½ cup olive oil
  • 8 tablespoons unsalted butter
  • 8 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 2 medium yellow onions, thinly sliced (or 2 large leeks)
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 4 cups chopped celery (reserve leaves for garnish)
  • 1 cup dry white wine
  • 2½ cups low-sodium vegetable broth
  • 3 large eggs
  • 1 cup finely chopped parsley or celery leaves
  • ½ cup finely chopped chives
  • 2 tablespoons finely chopped marjoram, oregano, or thyme (optional)

Method

  1. Tear the bread (1 loaf) into 1½–2-inch chunks and let dry out 12–24 hours, or toast at 300°F.
  2. Heat oven to 375°F. Cook the garlic (8 cloves) and onions (2) in olive oil (½ cup) and butter (8 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until soft, 10–12 minutes.
  3. Add celery (4 cups), cook 4–6 minutes, then pour in the wine (1 cup) and reduce by half, 3–4 minutes.
  4. Whisk the broth (2½ cups) with eggs (3), pour over the bread, and toss until absorbed. Fold in the vegetables, parsley (1 cup), chives (½ cup) and herbs (2 Tbsp).
  5. Spread loosely in a 2½–3-quart dish, cover with foil and bake 40–45 minutes.
  6. Uncover, raise heat to 425°F and bake 20–25 minutes more until crisp and golden; top with celery leaves.

Original allows chicken or turkey broth; vegetable broth used here. Bread can be torn 1–2 days ahead, vegetables prepped the day before, first bake done hours ahead and crisped before serving. Leftovers re-crisp well in a buttered skillet. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 head broccoli (florets and stems sliced into long strips)
  • Kosher salt
  • ½ lemon
  • 1 clove garlic, finely grated
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • Flaky salt
  • ½ bag frozen peas (optional)

Method

  1. Boil a pot of well-salted water and drop in the broccoli (1 head) for about 30 seconds until vivid green.
  2. If using peas (½ bag), add them for the last 30 seconds, then drain onto a plate.
  3. Squeeze the lemon (½) over the top and grate in the garlic (1 clove), tossing to coat.
  4. Adjust with flaky salt and pepper until tangy, salty and garlicky.

Meant as a palate-cleansing side next to rich pasta. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 bunches small carrots (~1 lb), unpeeled, tops trimmed
  • 3/4 cup walnuts, coarsely chopped
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1/4 tsp each ground cinnamon and coriander
  • 1/2 tsp crushed red pepper flakes
  • 1 Tbsp fennel seed
  • 2 Tbsp butter, in tiny pieces
  • 1 garlic clove, grated

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Cut some carrots (1 lb total) into coins, halve the rest lengthwise – mixed shapes roast better.
  2. Pile carrots and walnuts (3/4 cup) on a sheet with the oil (2 Tbsp), spices (1/4 tsp each cinnamon and coriander, 1/2 tsp flakes, 1 Tbsp fennel seed), salt and pepper; toss and dot with the butter (2 Tbsp).

Ingredients

  • 5 lb Yukon gold potatoes, peeled, large chunks
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 cup whole milk or buttermilk, plus more
  • 1 cup heavy cream
  • Black pepper
  • 1 cup sour cream, divided
  • 1 cup finely chopped chives, divided
  • A few Tbsp butter (optional)

Method

  1. Boil the potatoes (5 lb) in well-salted water until completely soft, ~40 minutes.
  2. Warm the milk (1 cup) and cream (1 cup) with salt and pepper to a bare simmer.
  3. Drain, return to the pot, and mash to your preferred texture – lumps allowed.
  4. Stir in the hot dairy; season. (Pause here up to 2 days, chilled.)
  5. To serve: fold in three-quarters of the sour cream (3/4 cup) and chives (3/4 cup), plus butter if using; top with the rest of each, pepper, flaky salt.

They tighten overnight – loosen with 1/2 cup+ milk when reheating. Leftovers fry into pancakes. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 medium onion, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 lb mushrooms (cremini, button, oyster or maitake), quartered
  • 1 lb green beans, trimmed, halved
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • 2 Tbsp flour
  • 1 3/4 cups whole milk
  • 2 Tbsp soy sauce or tamari
  • 1 garlic clove, grated
  • French's fried onions, to taste

Method

  1. Brown the sliced onion (1) in the oil (2 Tbsp) with salt and pepper over medium-high, 10–12 minutes.
  2. Add mushrooms (1 lb) and cook until shrunken and browned, another 10–12 minutes – let the onions go very dark.
  3. Add the green beans (1 lb); cook until bright and tender, 8–10 minutes.
  4. Melt in the butter (2 Tbsp), sprinkle over the flour (2 Tbsp) and stir 2–3 minutes; add the milk (1 3/4 cups) gradually, thickening between pours. Season with soy (2 Tbsp) and garlic (1 clove) and simmer to a gravy.
  5. Heat oven to 425°F; blanket with fried onions and bake until golden and bubbling, 20–25 minutes.

Assemble a day ahead without the topping; top and bake to serve. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 cups coarsely ground polenta
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 5 Tbsp butter, divided
  • 3–4 ears corn, kernels cut (~2 cups)
  • 1/4–1/2 cup Parmesan or pecorino, plus more
  • 1/4 tsp hot paprika
  • Pinch red pepper flakes

Method

  1. Boil 8 cups salted water; whisk in the polenta (2 cups) and cook over low, stirring now and then, 15–20 minutes until tender.
  2. Off heat, beat in butter (3 Tbsp) and cheese (1/4 cup); season.
  3. Sizzle the corn (3–4 ears) in butter (2 Tbsp) with salt, paprika (1/4 tsp) and flakes, 2–3 minutes – just past raw.
  4. Pile the corn over the polenta; more pepper, flakes and cheese over the top.

Both parts hold a few hours; loosen the polenta with water to reheat. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 lb small potatoes
  • Kosher salt; splash white vinegar (optional)
  • 1/4–1/2 lb snap or snow peas, halved lengthwise
  • 1 large spring onion or 2 scallions, thinly sliced
  • 1–2 lemons, zested and juiced
  • Black pepper; olive oil; dill (optional)

Method

  1. Boil the potatoes (1 lb) in well-salted water (a splash of vinegar is nice) until a knife slides through; drain and cool a few minutes.
  2. Halve lengthwise and lay on a platter with the raw peas (1/4–1/2 lb) and spring onion.
  3. Salt, then zest the lemons (1–2) over everything, squeeze on the juice, pour over good olive oil, crack pepper. Dill if you have it.

Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 4 large leeks, dark greens removed, halved lengthwise
  • 1/3 cup olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp harissa paste
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1/2 lemon, seeded, very finely chopped
  • Flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Heat oven to 450°F. With the leeks (4) cut-side down, slit them lengthwise every 1/4 inch, stopping at the root, so they fan open.
  2. Whisk the oil (1/3 cup) with harissa (2 Tbsp); rub it into all the layers, season, and lay flat in a 9×13 or on a sheet.
  3. Roast mostly undisturbed until the tips are browned and crisp, 20–25 minutes.
  4. Platter; top with the chopped lemon (1/2 – flesh, peel and all) and flaky salt.

Holds hours at room temp; fine unheated. Cousin of the NYT miso leeks two sections over. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2–3 large artichokes (1 1/2–2 lb)
  • 1 cup white wine
  • 1 cup water
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 4 Tbsp butter, in 1/2-inch pieces
  • 4 garlic cloves, smashed
  • Pinch red pepper flakes
  • Kosher salt, black pepper
  • Lemony aioli, for serving (hers is on this board)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Trim the stems, saw off the top 1 1/2 inches, pull the toughest leaves, snip thorns.
  2. Halve lengthwise and scoop out the fuzzy choke; set cut-side down in a 9×13.
  3. Add wine (1 cup), water (1 cup) and oil (1/4 cup); scatter the butter (4 Tbsp), garlic (4 cloves) and flakes; season, cover tight with foil, roast 45–55 minutes to tender.
  4. Uncover; roast 15–20 more until the liquid is mostly gone and the tops brown.
  5. Serve with the garlicky pan oil – and the aioli card – for dipping.

Roast hours ahead; room temperature is fine. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 lb small waxy potatoes
  • 1/2 cup butter
  • Kosher salt and cracked black pepper
  • 1 cup chives, finely chopped
  • Flaky salt

Method

  1. Simmer the potatoes (2 lb) in well-salted water until tender, 10–15 minutes; drain and cool slightly.
  2. Warm the butter (1/2 cup) with lots of cracked pepper until fragrant – a splash of water keeps it from browning.
  3. Halve the potatoes, toss with the pepper butter, then the chives (1 cup) and flaky salt.

Her version offers anchovies in the butter – omitted here. Scales freely. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3/4 cup coarse breadcrumbs or panko (optional)
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 cup heavy cream
  • 4 garlic cloves, smashed
  • 1/8 tsp grated nutmeg (optional)
  • 2 large bunches spinach, kale, chard or broccoli rabe (~16 cups), coarsely chopped
  • 1/2 cup crème fraîche (optional)

Method

  1. Toast the crumbs (3/4 cup) in oil with salt and pepper until crisp, 3–5 minutes; set aside.
  2. Simmer the cream (1 cup) with garlic (4 cloves) and nutmeg until reduced by half and properly thick, 15–20 minutes.
  3. Wilt the greens (16 cups) in oil (2 Tbsp) a handful at a time, cooking until totally tender and dry, ~10–13 minutes total.
  4. Stir in the reduced cream and crème fraîche (1/2 cup); heat through. Crumbs over the top.

Reduce the cream further than feels right – the greens give water back. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Pasta & Bakes

Ingredients

  • 1/2 box pasta (4 servings)
  • 1 cup reserved pasta water
  • 3 Tbsp butter
  • 1 Tbsp pink peppercorns
  • 1/2 cup grated Parmesan, plus more to serve

Method

  1. Cook pasta (1/2 box) al dente (~9 minutes); reserve 1 cup pasta water and drain.
  2. Melt half the butter (3 Tbsp) with the pink peppercorns (1 Tbsp) in a sauce pan; after ~5 minutes slowly add the reserved water until a sauce forms.
  3. Add the pasta, then the remaining butter and Parmesan (1/2 cup total); toss. Top with more cheese.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/4 lb fresh fettuccine
  • 1 cup pasta cooking water, divided
  • 4 oz buffalo butter
  • 2 oz butter
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 cup finely grated Parmigiano Reggiano
  • 1/2 cup roughly grated Parmigiano Reggiano

Method

  1. Boil salted water; cook fresh pasta to al dente (1–2 minutes).
  2. In a large sauté pan on low, warm 1/2 cup pasta water; slowly stir in both butters to emulsify.
  3. Transfer pasta with tongs into the sauce; add remaining pasta water and black pepper, tossing on low.
  4. Off heat, rain in the finely grated cheese in a slow stream while tossing. Bowl it; top with the coarse cheese and more pepper.

Ingredients

  • Salt
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 2 medium leeks, white and light green, thinly sliced (~2 cups)
  • 1 1/2 lb mushrooms (cremini, shiitake, oyster), stemmed and sliced
  • 1 lb short pasta (ziti, cavatappi)
  • 2 Tbsp white miso
  • 3/4 cup grated Parmesan, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp sherry or red wine vinegar, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp chopped parsley

Method

  1. Boil salted water. Soften leeks (2 medium) in shimmering oil with salt, ~5 minutes.
  2. Add mushrooms, salt lightly, and cook, stirring every 2 minutes, until browned, ~10 minutes.
  3. Cook pasta al dente; reserve 1 cup pasta water, drain.
  4. Dissolve miso (2 Tbsp) in the reserved water; add to the skillet with pasta, Parmesan (3/4 cup total) and vinegar (1 Tbsp total), stirring hard over medium-high until a cheesy sauce coats the noodles, 1–2 minutes.
  5. Adjust vinegar; garnish with parsley (1 Tbsp), more Parmesan and oil.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 6 large shallots, very thinly sliced
  • 5 garlic cloves, 4 thinly sliced, 1 grated
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 Tbsp white miso (the umami the anchovies would bring)
  • 1 (4.5-oz) tube or 1/2 cup tomato paste
  • 10 oz bucatini or spaghetti
  • Butter, 1 Tbsp
  • 1 cup parsley, finely chopped

Method

  1. Cook shallots (6 large) in the oil over medium, stirring, until deeply golden and jammy, 15–20 minutes. Add sliced garlic (5 cloves), salt, pepper and flakes (1 tsp); 2 minutes more.
  2. Stir in miso (1 Tbsp), then tomato paste (1 (4.5-oz)); fry, stirring, until it goes brick-dark and caramelized at the edges, ~2 minutes. (This shallot-tomato concentrate keeps a week — the original's whole point.)
  3. Cook pasta al dente; reserve 1 cup water. Toss pasta with half the sauce, butter and splashes of pasta water until glossy.
  4. Off heat, add parsley (1 cup) and the grated garlic; season. Serve with the extra sauce dolloped on top.

Ingredients

  • 1 (17.6-oz) package shelf-stable potato gnocchi
  • 1 lb mixed mushrooms, torn into bite-size pieces
  • 1 bunch scallions, cut in 1-inch lengths
  • 4 garlic cloves, smashed
  • 5 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp butter, cut small
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • Grated Parmesan and lemon wedges, to serve

Method

  1. Heat oven to 450°F. On a rimmed sheet, toss gnocchi (1 (17.6-oz) package) (straight from the package, broken apart), mushrooms (1 lb), scallions and garlic (4 cloves) with oil, soy, salt and pepper.
  2. Dot with butter (2 Tbsp). Roast 20–25 minutes, tossing once halfway, until gnocchi are plump and crisp-edged and mushrooms are browned.
  3. Shower with Parmesan and a squeeze of lemon.

Weeknight sibling of the frizzled green beans — same roast-everything logic.

Ingredients

  • 2 lb ricotta, whipped smooth in a processor or mixer
  • 1 lb mascarpone
  • 3 cups Pecorino Romano, divided
  • 2 Tbsp freshly ground black pepper, plus more
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 1/2 lb dried linguine, fettuccine or spaghetti
  • 1 Tbsp butter

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; boil generously salted water.
  2. Mix ricotta (2 lb), mascarpone (1 lb) and 2 cups pecorino; stir in pepper (2 Tbsp total) and salt (1 tsp).
  3. Cook pasta just barely al dente (~6 minutes — it finishes in the oven). Drain; stir into the cheese.
  4. Butter a 9×13 dish, add the pasta, top with remaining cheese. Bake ~35 minutes until crisp and golden on top and sides.
  5. More pepper, cut into squares, serve.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 1 large yellow onion, finely chopped
  • 4 cloves garlic, finely chopped
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 1 (28-oz) can whole peeled tomatoes in juice
  • 1 (28-oz) can tomato purée or sauce
  • 3/4 tsp red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 16 oz ricotta
  • 1/2 cup heavy cream
  • 1/2 cup finely grated Parmesan or pecorino, plus more
  • 1 lb ziti, rigatoni or penne
  • 1 lb fresh mozzarella, 1/2-inch pieces

Method

  1. Sauce: soften onion (1 large) and garlic in oil over medium with salt and pepper, 8–10 minutes, no browning. Add tomato paste (2 Tbsp); cook to brick-red, ~2 minutes. Hand-crush the whole tomatoes in with juice (1 (28-oz) can), add purée, season, add flakes (3/4 tsp). Simmer until thickened and melded, 20–30 minutes.
  2. Filling: mix ricotta (16 oz), cream (1/2 cup) and 1/2 cup Parmesan; season.
  3. Oven 425°F. Cook pasta 2 minutes shy of al dente; reserve 1 cup pasta water, drain and rinse cool.
  4. Stir the reserved pasta water into the finished sauce. Toss pasta with 2 cups sauce so it gets inside the tubes.
  5. In a 3-quart dish: a little sauce, then a third of the pasta, a third of the remaining sauce, half the ricotta, a third of the mozzarella (1 lb). Repeat. Final layer: last pasta and sauce, dot remaining mozzarella, shave Parmesan over.
  6. Bake on a lined sheet pan until golden, bubbling at the edges, 30–40 minutes. Cool slightly; serve with a big leafy salad.

Bakes 1 day ahead; rewarm to serve.

Also on NYT Cooking – ★★★★ (7,787 ratings), by Alison Roman. View there →

Ingredients

Sauce

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 large yellow onion, finely chopped
  • 4 cloves garlic, finely chopped
  • 6 anchovy fillets, optional
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste, optional
  • 1 (28-oz) can whole peeled tomatoes
  • 1 (28-oz) can crushed tomatoes

Assembly

  • 1 1/2 lb fresh mozzarella, grated or shredded
  • 16 oz (2 cups) whole-milk ricotta
  • 1 cup coarsely grated Parmesan, plus more
  • 1/3 cup heavy cream
  • 1 garlic clove, finely grated
  • 1 lb dried lasagna noodles (not no-boil)
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Soften onion (1 large), garlic and anchovies in oil with salt and pepper, 8–10 minutes. Tomato paste (2 Tbsp) in; cook to brick red, ~2 minutes.
  2. Hand-crush the whole tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can) in, add crushed tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can) plus half a can of water. Simmer 35–45 minutes until as thick as jarred sauce — looser and it won't cut clean.
  3. Oven 425°F; salted water on to boil. Set aside 1 cup mozzarella; mix the rest with ricotta (16 oz), Parmesan (1 cup total), cream and grated garlic (4 cloves); season.
  4. Boil noodles (1 lb) until just softened, 4–5 minutes; drain, separate, oil lightly.
  5. In a 3-quart dish: thin layer of sauce, noodles (minimal overlap), ~1 1/4 cups sauce, a quarter of the cheese mixture. Repeat three more times, ending with noodles then the last of the sauce. Top with the reserved mozzarella (1 1/2 lb) and more Parmesan.
  6. Cover loosely with foil on a lined sheet pan; bake 25–30 minutes until tender and bubbling. Uncover and bake until golden with frilly crisp corners, 35–45 more. Cool slightly.

Sauce keeps 1 week. Baked lasagna keeps 3 days (1 month frozen); reheat covered at 375°F, 25–45 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 2 large eggplants, sliced lengthwise 3/4-inch (8 slices)
  • 5 Tbsp olive oil, divided, plus more for the dish
  • Coarse salt and pepper
  • 1 cup thinly sliced cremini mushrooms
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 Tbsp chopped fresh thyme
  • 1 (15-oz) container whole-milk ricotta
  • 3 large eggs
  • 1 cup grated Parmesan, divided
  • 2 Tbsp chopped fresh oregano
  • 2 cups Nona's Marinara (see Dressings & Sauces)

Method

  1. Oven 400°F. Brush eggplant with 3 Tbsp oil, season, roast in one layer until soft and golden, turning halfway, ~25 minutes.
  2. Sauté mushrooms (1 cup) in 2 Tbsp oil ~7 minutes; add garlic (2 cloves) and thyme (1 Tbsp) 2 minutes. Cool.
  3. Mix ricotta (1 (15-oz) container), eggs (3 large), 1/2 cup Parmesan, oregano, mushrooms, 2 tsp salt, 1/4 tsp pepper.
  4. In an oiled 8-inch dish: half the marinara, 4 eggplant slices, the ricotta mixture, 4 more slices, remaining marinara, remaining 1/2 cup Parmesan.
  5. Bake at 350°F until golden brown, 30 minutes.

Ingredients

Tomato sauce

  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 cups chopped onions
  • 5 garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 tsp dried marjoram
  • 5 cups undrained canned whole tomatoes (3 14.5-oz cans)
  • 1/3 cup chopped kalamata olives
  • 2 tsp salt, 1/2 tsp black pepper
  • 3 Tbsp chopped fresh dill (1 tsp dried)

Filling

  • 1 large eggplant (~1 1/2 lb), 1/2-inch rounds
  • Olive oil, for brushing
  • 3 eggs, beaten
  • 2 cups cottage cheese
  • 1 tsp ground fennel
  • 3 cups grated feta
  • 1/2 lb uncooked lasagna noodles (12)

Method

  1. Sauce: sauté onions (2 cups) in oil ~5 minutes; add garlic (5 cloves) and marjoram (2 tsp) until translucent. Add tomatoes (5 cups), cover, simmer; add olives (1/3 cup), salt, pepper. Stir in dill (3 Tbsp) just before assembling.
  2. Oven 400°F. Brush eggplant (1 large) rounds with oil on an oiled sheet; bake uncovered ~15 minutes. Reduce oven to 350°F.
  3. Mix eggs (3), cottage cheese (2 cups), fennel and 1 cup of the feta.
  4. In an oiled 8×10×3 dish: 2 cups sauce, a layer of noodles (1/2 lb), 1 cup sauce, all the eggplant, 1 cup feta, noodles, 1 cup sauce, all the cottage cheese mixture, noodles, the rest of the sauce, the last cup of feta (3 cups).
  5. Cover with foil; bake 45–60 minutes. Rest uncovered 10–15 minutes before serving.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb cavatappi (or pasta of choice)
  • 1/2 cup butter, plus 2 Tbsp for crumbs
  • 1/2 cup flour
  • 4 cups milk
  • 6 cups freshly grated white cheddar
  • 1 tsp salt, up to 1 tsp pepper
  • 1/2 cup panko breadcrumbs

Method

  1. Boil pasta in salted water per package.
  2. Melt 1/2 cup butter over medium; whisk in flour (1/2 cup) and cook 2–3 minutes. Season. Slowly whisk in milk (4 cups); heat to a low boil until properly thick — don't stop early. Off heat.
  3. Grease a 9×13; add the hot pasta, sprinkle all the cheddar (6 cups) over, pour the hot sauce on top and let it sit until the cheese melts. Stir together.
  4. Toast panko in 2 Tbsp butter, stirring, 3–5 minutes until golden; scatter over.
  5. Bake at 325°F for 12–15 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 1 box egg noodles
  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter + 1/2 Tbsp for the pan
  • 4 Tbsp flour
  • 1/2 white onion, chopped
  • 3 cups milk
  • 3–4 eggs
  • 2 cups cheddar, grated
  • 1 cup Parmesan, grated
  • 1 cup cottage cheese
  • Dash of Tabasco
  • Salt, pepper, breadcrumbs

Method

  1. Cook noodles (1 box); make a roux with butter and flour (4 Tbsp), soften the onion (1/2) in it, then whisk in milk (3 cups) to a thickened sauce.
  2. Off heat, stir in cheddar (2 cups), Parmesan (1 cup), cottage cheese (1 cup), Tabasco, salt and pepper; fold in noodles, then the beaten eggs (3–4).
  3. Pour into the buttered pan, top with breadcrumbs, and bake at 350°F until set and golden, ~40 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups whole-milk ricotta
  • 2 large eggs
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 3/4 cup all-purpose flour
  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 1 large leek, white and light green, thinly sliced
  • 1 bunch asparagus, trimmed, 2-inch pieces
  • 2 cups shelled fresh or frozen peas
  • 3 cups pea shoots or leaves, thick stems removed
  • 1/4 cup parsley or mint leaves
  • Parmesan or pecorino, to serve

Method

  1. Boil a large pot of salted water. Mix ricotta (2 cups) and eggs; season, then gently mix in flour (3/4 cup) — don't overmix or the dumplings toughen.
  2. Heat butter (4 Tbsp) and oil over medium-high; cook leeks with salt and pepper until soft and coloring, 5–8 minutes.
  3. Add asparagus (1 bunch) and peas (2 cups); toss until asparagus is just tender and bright, 2–4 minutes. Off heat.
  4. Drop the ricotta mixture into the boiling water by heaping spoonfuls (they swell; work in batches). Once they float, boil 4–5 more minutes — cut one open: pillowy, not doughy.
  5. Transfer dumplings to the skillet with a slotted spoon; toss over medium to coat. Add pea shoots just until wilted.
  6. Serve topped with herbs, grated cheese and olive oil (2 Tbsp total).

Ingredients

  • 1 large head cauliflower, in bite-size florets
  • Kosher salt
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • 2 tsp Dijon or 1/2 tsp mustard powder
  • 3/4 cup heavy cream
  • 8 oz sharp cheddar, grated, divided
  • Black pepper; pinch of cayenne

Method

  1. Blanch the florets in salted water 4 minutes — just shy of tender. Drain very well.
  2. In the empty pot, warm butter (2 Tbsp), mustard and cream (3/4 cup); melt in most of the cheddar (8 oz total) off the heat.
  3. Fold in the cauliflower (1 large head), season, and tip into a baking dish; top with the reserved cheese.
  4. Broil until brown-blistered, 3–5 minutes. Tastes like the casserole, skips the macaroni.

Ingredients

  • 10 oz cremini mushrooms
  • 1/2 cup walnuts
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 onion + 1 carrot, finely chopped
  • 3 garlic cloves, minced
  • 3 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1/2 cup red or white wine
  • 1 (28-oz) can crushed tomatoes
  • Parmesan rind (optional) + grated Parmesan
  • 12 oz tagliatelle or rigatoni; 2 Tbsp butter

Method

Ingredients

  • 8 oz elbow macaroni or shells
  • 2 3/4 cups water
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 small head broccoli, in very small florets
  • 3/4 cup whole milk or evaporated milk
  • 8 oz sharp cheddar, grated

Method

  1. Boil the pasta in the salted water in a wide pot, stirring so it doesn't stick, 5 minutes.

  3. Off heat, stir in milk (3/4 cup), cheddar, Dijon and plenty of pepper until saucy — the starch does the work.
  4. Rest 2 minutes to thicken; adjust salt.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

Tomato sauce

  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 large or 4 small onions (12–16 ounces), diced
  • 8–10 cloves garlic, thinly sliced, plus 1 clove garlic, grated
  • 1/2–1 teaspoon crushed red pepper flakes, optional
  • 1 (28-ounce) can whole peeled tomatoes, packed in juice
  • 1 (28-ounce) can crushed tomatoes

Assembly

  • 1 cup (8 ounces) ricotta
  • 2 tablespoons heavy cream
  • 1 pound large pasta shells
  • 1/2 pound salted mozzarella, roughly torn or grated
  • Parmesan, for grating

Method

  1. Build the sauce: sweat the onions (2 large) and sliced garlic (8–10 cloves) in oil (1/4 cup) with salt and pepper until soft, then add the hand-crushed whole tomatoes (28-ounce can, with juice), crushed tomatoes (28-ounce can), a half can of swirled water, and red pepper flakes if wanted (1/2–1 tsp). Simmer low at least 30 minutes, up to 1.5 hours, until thick.
  2. Mix the ricotta (1 cup), cream (2 Tbsp) and the grated garlic (1 clove) with salt and pepper; set aside.
  3. Heat the oven to 425F. Boil the shells (1 pound) in salted water until just shy of al dente, drain, and toss with about 2 cups of the sauce.
  4. Layer in a 3-quart dish: a little sauce on the bottom, then a third of the shells, sauce, dollops of the ricotta mix, torn mozzarella (1/2 pound total across layers) and grated Parmesan. Repeat twice more, finishing with pepper.
  5. Set the dish on a foil-lined sheet pan, cover loosely with foil, bake 20 minutes, then uncover and bake about 20 more until browned and bubbling. Rest at least 5 minutes before serving.

Sauce keeps 5 days refrigerated or 2 months frozen; the assembled unbaked dish keeps several days in the fridge or 2 months frozen (bake from frozen at 325F covered 60 min, then uncovered at 425F for 20 min). Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 12–16 ounces / 300–450g pasta (long noodle shape preferred)
  • Kosher salt
  • 6 tablespoons / 85g unsalted butter
  • 1/2 teaspoon crushed red pepper flakes, plus more
  • Freshly ground black pepper (from a pepper mill)
  • 1 lemon, zested and juiced
  • 1/2 cup / 50g finely grated parmesan cheese, plus more

Method

  1. Boil the pasta (12–16 ounces) in well-salted water until al dente; save 2 cups of the cooking water, then drain.
  2. Brown the butter (6 Tbsp) in a large skillet over medium heat, whisking up the browned bits, until foamy and nutty, about 3–4 minutes.
  3. Stir in the red pepper flakes (1/2 tsp), plenty of black pepper and the lemon zest (1 lemon), then add the pasta, parmesan (1/2 cup) and 1/2 cup pasta water. Salt, raise to medium-high, and toss until the liquid reduces.
  4. Add pasta water in two more 1/2-cup additions, tossing each time until the sauce turns glossy and starchy and the pasta is fully tender.
  5. Off the heat, add half the lemon juice (from 1 lemon), taste, adjust with salt, pepper, flakes and the rest of the juice, and finish bowls with more pepper and parmesan.

Best made and eaten right away; no make-ahead guidance given. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 8 ounces rigatoni, ziti or campanelle
  • 6 tablespoons olive oil
  • 3/4 cup fresh coarse or panko bread crumbs
  • 1/2 cup finely grated pecorino cheese, plus more for serving
  • 1 large shallot, finely chopped
  • 1 medium head cauliflower (about 2 pounds), outer leaves and stem removed, sliced about 1/2-inch thick
  • 1 cup heavy cream
  • 1 tablespoon finely grated lemon zest, plus more for serving
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped chives
  • Red-pepper flakes (optional)

Method

  1. Cook the pasta (8 ounces) in salted water until al dente, saving about 1 cup of the cooking water before draining.
  2. Toast the bread crumbs (3/4 cup) in half the oil (3 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until golden, 4–6 minutes, then toss with pecorino (1/4 cup) until clumpy; tip into a bowl.
  3. In the wiped skillet, cook the shallot (1) and sliced cauliflower (about 2 pounds) in the remaining oil (3 Tbsp) with salt and pepper for 12–15 minutes until soft and caramelizing.
  4. Pour in the cream (1 cup) with the lemon zest (1 Tbsp) and simmer 2–4 minutes until thickened.
  5. Add the pasta, the remaining pecorino (1/4 cup) and about 3/4 cup pasta water; toss until thick and glossy, 4–6 minutes. Serve topped with the crumbs, chives (1/2 cup), more zest, chile flakes and cheese.

The cauliflower cream can be made ahead and set aside in the skillet until the pasta is ready. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 8 ounces pasta (spaghetti or bucatini)
  • Kosher salt
  • 1/4 cup olive oil, plus more for drizzling
  • 1/2 cup walnut pieces (optional)
  • 2 large shallots, 1 medium onion, or 1 large leek (white and light green parts), thinly sliced
  • 1 lemon, thinly sliced, seeds removed
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 tablespoons capers
  • 1 1/4 pounds zucchini (about 2 medium), thinly sliced
  • 2 ounces feta, crumbled (about 3/4 cup)
  • 1 cup parsley or dill leaves (or a mix), coarsely chopped
  • 2 tablespoons fresh oregano leaves

Method

  1. Cook the pasta (8 ounces) in salted water until al dente, drain, rinse with cool water and toss with a little olive oil so it does not stick.
  2. Toast the walnuts (1/2 cup, optional) in the oil (1/4 cup) until golden, 3–4 minutes; lift out, salt, and keep aside.
  3. In the same oil, cook the shallots (2 large) and lemon slices (1) with salt and pepper until soft and caramelized, 5–8 minutes, then stir in the capers (2 Tbsp) and let them sizzle briefly.
  4. Add the zucchini (1 1/4 pounds) with salt and pepper and cook 10–15 minutes until the moisture has cooked off and the edges brown.
  5. Off the heat, toss in the pasta, then pile into a bowl and top with the walnuts, feta (2 ounces), herbs (1 cup parsley or dill, 2 Tbsp oregano) and a drizzle of oil.

Best at room temperature; for a saucier result add more olive oil rather than pasta water. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 6 oz shell or elbow pasta
  • Kosher salt
  • 2 oz cream cheese
  • 1/2 cup grated Parmesan
  • Black pepper (optional)

Method

  1. Boil the pasta (6 oz) in well-salted water until tender; save a cup of the water and drain.
  2. Return pasta to the pot with cream cheese (2 oz), Parmesan (1/2 cup) and half the reserved water (1/2 cup).
  3. Stir over medium 3–5 minutes until the cheeses melt into a coating sauce; splash in more water as needed.
  4. Pepper if you like. Reheats well with a splash of water.

Quickest mac on the board – the 9×13 baked one is four cards up when you have an hour. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2–2 lb globe eggplant (~2), sliced 1/4–1/2 inch
  • 1/2 cup + 4 Tbsp olive oil, divided
  • 1 cup panko
  • 1/4 cup Parmesan or pecorino, plus more
  • 1/2 cup chopped parsley, chives and/or oregano
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 4 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 8 oz sungold or cherry tomatoes
  • 1–2 Tbsp tomato paste, harissa or gochujang
  • Red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 12–16 oz short tubular pasta (rigatoni, paccheri)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F. Season the eggplant slices (1 1/2–2 lb) on sheet pans, drizzle with the 1/2 cup oil, and roast 30–45 minutes, turning halfway, until deeply browned.
  2. Toast the panko (1 cup) in oil (2 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until golden, 5–7 minutes; stir in the cheese (1/4 cup) for a minute more, then toss with a few tablespoons of the herbs.
  3. In the same skillet: garlic (4 cloves) in oil (2 Tbsp) 3–5 minutes, then tomatoes (8 oz), paste (1–2 Tbsp) and flakes until the tomatoes burst and the paste goes brick-red.
  4. Fold the eggplant into the skillet. Boil the pasta (12–16 oz) just shy of al dente; reserve 2 cups water.
  5. Toss pasta into the skillet with 1 1/2 cups pasta water over medium, 3–5 minutes, until thick and creamy.
  6. Top with the Parmesan crumbs, remaining herbs and more cheese.

Pasta keeps 3 days – she endorses it cold. Crumbs keep a day at room temp. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 12 oz rigatoni or other pasta
  • Kosher salt
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • 5 garlic cloves (4 sliced, 1 grated)
  • 1 Tbsp fennel seed
  • Red pepper flakes, to taste
  • 2 lb tiny tomatoes, halved
  • 2 Tbsp white distilled vinegar
  • Pecorino or Parmesan; black pepper

Method

  1. Get salted pasta water going while the sauce builds.
  2. Fry the sliced garlic (4 cloves), fennel seed (1 Tbsp) and flakes in the oil (1/4 cup) and butter (2 Tbsp) over medium-high until golden, 3–4 minutes.
  3. High heat: add the tomatoes (2 lb), salt and pepper; cook 5–7 minutes until split and jammy but still holding shape.
  4. Splash in the vinegar (2 Tbsp); off heat.
  5. Boil the pasta (12 oz) to al dente, move it into the tomatoes, and toss with pasta water until glossy.
  6. Bowl with grated cheese, the raw grated garlic (1 clove) and cracked pepper.

Fennel seed + chile + garlic reads like sausage with no meat in sight – same trick as the caramelized shallot pasta. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Mains

Ingredients

  • 1 bag mushrooms + dash soy sauce + olive oil
  • 1 bag spinach + 1/3 cup green onions
  • 12 egg whites and 6 yolks
  • 1/2 cup milk, salt, pepper
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped dill and parsley
  • 6 sun-dried tomatoes, roughly chopped
  • 1/2 cup crumbled feta
  • 1/4 cup grated Parmesan or pecorino

Method

  1. Sauté mushrooms with oil and a dash of soy until caramelized and dry.
  2. Sauté spinach with the green onions until wilted; squeeze dry.
  3. Whisk eggs with milk (1/2 cup), salt and pepper. Add vegetables, herbs, tomatoes (6), feta and Parmesan.
  4. Cook on the stove over medium until the edges pull from the pan (5–7 minutes), then bake 25–35 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb cilantro (~3 large bunches)
  • 1 lb Italian parsley (~3 large bunches)
  • 1 large bunch fresh dill
  • 4 crisp romaine-heart leaves
  • 2 large leeks, trimmed
  • 3 Tbsp + 1/4 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • Fine sea salt and black pepper
  • 1 Tbsp dried fenugreek + 2 tsp dried dill
  • 1/4 cup barberries, rinsed and dried
  • 1 tsp ground turmeric
  • 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 6–7 large eggs
  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • To serve: radishes, Persian pickles or cornichons, feta, warm flatbread, mast-o khiar

Method

  1. Trim herbs to leaves and tender stems; wash and spin very dry with the romaine. Finely dice leeks (2 large) (green and white); wash and drain.
  2. Heat 3 Tbsp oil in a 10-inch cast-iron or nonstick pan over medium-high; cook leeks with a good pinch of salt until translucent and soft, not browned, ~20 minutes.
  3. Very finely chop the herbs and romaine (roll handfuls into a tight ball and rock the knife through — smaller pieces, greener kuku). Combine with fenugreek and dried dill (1 large bunch).
  4. Add the herb mixture and another pinch of salt to the leeks; cook, stirring, until dry and very dark green, ~5 minutes. Spread back into the bowl and cool.
  5. Stir in barberries (1/4 cup), turmeric (1 tsp), baking powder, 2 tsp salt, 1/2 tsp pepper — it should taste a little salty. Add eggs (6–7 large) one at a time, using as few as will barely bind: loose-porridge consistency.
  6. Melt butter (4 Tbsp) over medium-high; add the 1/4 cup oil. Test with a spoonful — it should sizzle — then add the rest, smoothing the top. Cook, rotating a quarter-turn every 3–4 minutes, until set with a very dark brown bottom, 15–20 minutes. (Dark ≈ almost burned ≈ correct.)
  7. Tip off the oil into a bowl, invert the kuku onto a flat platter, return the oil to the pan, slide the kuku back in and cook the second side ~5 minutes.
  8. Drain on paper towels; serve warm, cold or room temp with radishes, pickles, feta, flatbread and mast-o khiar.

Ingredients

  • 5 large eggs + 5 egg whites
  • 1/3 cup water
  • 1/2 tsp salt, 1/4 tsp pepper
  • 2/3 cup black beans (low-sodium)
  • 1/2 cup chopped tomato
  • 3 oz pepper jack, grated
  • Cilantro and salsa, to serve

Method

  1. Oven 375°F; spray a 9-inch pie dish.
  2. Whisk eggs, whites, water, salt (1/2 tsp) and pepper; pour into the dish.
  3. Scatter beans (2/3 cup), tomato (1/2 cup) and cheese evenly over. Bake until set in the center and starting to brown, 30–35 minutes.
  4. Cool 10 minutes; garnish with cilantro, cut into 6 wedges, serve with salsa.

Ingredients

Paneer

  • 24 oz paneer, 1-inch cubes
  • 2 tsp ground turmeric
  • 1 Tbsp salt
  • 1 tsp red pepper/chili powder
  • 2 1/2 Tbsp vegetable oil (for marinade)

Saag

  • 4 tomatoes, roughly chopped
  • 6–8 garlic cloves, chopped
  • 1 1/2-inch ginger, chopped (+1 Tbsp minced)
  • 1 Tbsp garam masala
  • 1 tsp red chili powder
  • 2 tsp cumin powder
  • 3 (16-oz) packages frozen chopped spinach, thawed and dried
  • 2 small/medium white onions, finely chopped
  • 1 serrano, finely chopped (optional)
  • 1 Tbsp ground coriander
  • 1 cup milk or coconut milk, divided

Method

  1. Whisk turmeric (2 tsp), chili powder (1 tsp), salt and oil; marinate the paneer (24 oz) while you prep.
  2. Brown the paneer in a nonstick skillet over medium, a couple of minutes per side; set aside.
  3. In a separate hot pan or Dutch oven, sauté onion, ginger (1) and garlic until the raw smell is gone and lightly browned. Add tomatoes (4); cook until soft and mushy, 3–4 minutes.
  4. Add spinach (3 (16-oz) packages) with cumin, chili powder (1 tsp), salt and garam masala (1 Tbsp); cook 2 minutes.
  5. Cool a little, add 1/2 cup milk/coconut milk (1 cup total) and blend (hand blender fine — as smooth or chunky as you like).
  6. Return purée to the pan over medium with another 1/2 cup milk. Add the paneer, adjust consistency with water, cover and simmer 10 minutes on low.
  7. Optional finish: squeeze of lemon and/or a drizzle of honey.

Ingredients

Tofu

  • 6 oz extra-firm tofu, cubed
  • 2 tsp oil
  • 1/8 tsp each ground pepper, paprika, red chili flakes
  • Salt

Spinach curry

  • 1 lb baby spinach
  • 2 tomatoes, roughly chopped
  • 3–4 garlic cloves + 1 inch ginger, roughly chopped
  • 1/2 tsp garam masala
  • 1/8 tsp red chili powder
  • 3/4 tsp cumin powder
  • 1/2 cup coconut milk (or milk/cream)
  • 2 1/2 tsp oil, salt
  • 1–2 tsp sugar or honey (optional)
  • 1 tsp lemon juice (optional)

Method

  1. Sauté tofu (6 oz) in 2 tsp oil with paprika, salt, pepper (1/8 tsp) and flakes until light golden, 5–6 minutes.
  2. Wilt the spinach (1 lb) in 1 tsp oil; set aside. Sauté ginger and garlic in 1 1/2 tsp oil, add tomatoes until mushy, 3–4 minutes.
  3. Add spinach, cumin, chili powder (1/8 tsp), salt and garam masala (1/2 tsp); cook 2 minutes. Cool slightly, blend with 1/4 cup coconut milk.
  4. Return purée to the pan with another 1/4 cup coconut milk. Add tofu, adjust with water, add sugar/honey (1–2 tsp) if using; simmer covered 10 minutes on medium-low.
  5. Lemon to finish; serve with rice or Indian bread.

Ingredients

Marinade

  • 1/2 cup plain whole-milk yogurt
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 Tbsp lime juice
  • 6 fat garlic cloves
  • 1 Tbsp fresh ginger, sliced
  • 1 Tbsp each ground coriander and cumin
  • 2 tsp garam masala
  • 2 tsp paprika (not smoked)
  • 1/2 tsp turmeric, 1/4 tsp cayenne
  • 2 tsp kosher salt, 1 tsp pepper

Everything else

  • Tofu "filets" ~1 inch thick (or chicken thighs, per original)
  • 1 sheet-pan layer of vegetables, 1/2-inch (carrots, squash, sweet potato, onion, cauliflower, zucchini, peppers, potatoes, eggplant, parsnips…)
  • Olive oil, salt, pepper
  • Garnish: cilantro, scallions, cilantro-mint chutney; basmati rice

Method

  1. Oven 425°F. Blend all marinade ingredients smooth; set 1/4 cup aside for basting.
  2. Coat the tofu in the rest; set aside.
  3. Toss the vegetables (1) (cut to an even 1/2 inch) with oil, salt (2 tsp) and pepper on a lined sheet pan; spread out.
  4. Lay the marinated tofu over the veg, spooning a little marinade on the vegetables — go light.
  5. Bake 25 minutes in the middle of the oven; baste with the reserved marinade, then 10–12 more until the veg are tender.
  6. Garnish; serve over basmati with cucumber raita and naan.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp canola oil
  • 1 small onion, chopped
  • 1 serrano chile, chopped
  • 1/4 cup finely chopped garlic
  • 1/4 cup finely chopped peeled fresh ginger
  • 4 tsp ground coriander
  • 4 tsp garam masala
  • 2 medium tomatoes, chopped
  • 1 (14-oz) package firm tofu, drained and cubed
  • Chopped cilantro; steamed basmati rice

Method

  1. Heat the oil in a large nonstick skillet. Cook onion (1 small), serrano, garlic (1/4 cup), ginger (1/4 cup), coriander, garam masala (4 tsp) and tomatoes over moderate heat, stirring, until fragrant and saucy, ~8 minutes.
  2. Stir in tofu (1 (14-oz) package) and 1 cup water; bring to a simmer.
