Ingredients

  • 14 oz extra-firm tofu
  • 1 Tbsp peanut or safflower oil
  • 1-inch ginger, peeled and minced
  • 2 shallots or 1 small onion, minced
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 Thai chile or 2 serranos, seeded, thinly sliced
  • 2 Tbsp chopped cilantro stems
  • 8 oz cremini mushrooms, quartered
  • 1/2 tsp sea salt, more to taste
  • 3 Tbsp red or green curry paste
  • 1 cup unsweetened coconut milk
  • 2 tsp fish sauce or tamari
  • Zest and juice of 1 lime
  • 1 cup snow peas
  • Basil/cilantro leaves; rice, to serve

Method

  1. Press tofu (14 oz) 20 minutes between paper towels under a baking sheet; cut into 1-inch cubes. (Optional: bake at 375°F for 20 minutes with a drizzle of tamari (2 tsp) and sriracha for firmer cubes.)
  2. Sauté ginger, shallots, garlic (2 cloves), chile and cilantro stems (2 Tbsp) in oil over medium-high until fragrant, ~5 minutes.
  3. Add mushrooms (8 oz) and salt; cook until golden, ~5 minutes. Stir in curry paste (3 Tbsp); 2 minutes.
  4. Add coconut milk (1 cup), scraping up the fond; stir in fish sauce/tamari and lime zest + juice.
  5. Add tofu and snow peas (1 cup); simmer until slightly thickened and peas are tender, 7–10 minutes, stirring. Adjust seasoning.
  6. Serve over rice with torn basil/cilantro.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 leek, thinly sliced
  • 1 small fennel bulb, thinly sliced (optional but good)
  • 2 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 1 tsp ground cumin + 1/2 tsp ground coriander
  • 10 oz baby spinach (or chard, stemmed and sliced)
  • 1/2 cup frozen peas
  • 1/4 cup cream or coconut milk
  • Kosher salt, black pepper, pinch of Aleppo
  • 4–6 eggs
  • Crumbled feta, dill, lemon; bread or pita to serve

Method

  1. Soften leek (1) and fennel in oil over medium, ~8 minutes. Add garlic (2 cloves) and spices, 1 minute.
  2. Wilt in the spinach in batches; add peas (1/2 cup), cream, salt, pepper and Aleppo, and simmer 2–3 minutes to a loose green bed.
  3. Make wells; crack in the eggs (4–6). Cover and cook over low until whites are set, yolks runny, 5–7 minutes.
  4. Finish with feta, dill and lemon; eat from the pan with bread.

Cousin of the frittata and tsigarelli — same greens, breakfast-for-dinner format.

Ingredients

  • 1 small cauliflower, in medium florets
  • 1 tsp kosher salt, plus more
  • 1 red onion — a few rings reserved, the rest finely chopped
  • 5 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1/2 tsp finely chopped rosemary
  • 7 eggs
  • 1/2 cup basil, chopped
  • 1 cup flour, sifted
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 1/3 tsp ground turmeric
  • 5 oz coarsely grated Parmesan or aged cheddar
  • Black pepper
  • Butter, plus sesame + nigella (or poppy) seeds, for the pan

Method

  1. Simmer florets in salted water until just tender, ~6 minutes; drain gently.
  2. Oven 400°F. Soften the chopped onion with rosemary (1/2 tsp) in the oil, ~10 minutes; cool.
  3. Whisk eggs (7) with basil (1/2 cup); whisk in flour (1 cup), baking powder (1 1/2 tsp), turmeric (1/3 tsp), cheese, 1 tsp salt and plenty of pepper, then the cooled onion. Fold in the cauliflower (1 small) gently, keeping florets whole.
  4. Butter a lined 9-inch springform; dust sides and base with the mixed seeds. Pour in the batter, arrange the reserved (1) onion rings on top.
  5. Bake 45 minutes until golden and set. Rest at least 20 minutes — serve just warm or room temp, with a sharp green salad.

Ingredients

  • 1 Tbsp + 1 tsp canola oil, divided
  • 2 large cloves garlic, minced
  • 4 scallions, greens included, thinly sliced
  • 1 Tbsp minced ginger
  • 4 cups leftover cooked brown rice
  • 3/4 cup finely diced red pepper
  • 3/4 cup cooked shelled edamame
  • 1/2 cup corn, fresh or thawed
  • 6 oz firm tofu, 1/4-inch cubes
  • 2 eggs, beaten
  • 3 Tbsp low-sodium soy sauce

Method

  1. Heat 1 Tbsp oil in a wok until very hot; soften garlic (2 large cloves), scallions (4) and ginger (1 Tbsp) 2–3 minutes.
  2. Add rice (4 cups), pepper, edamame (3/4 cup), corn and tofu (6 oz); stir until heated through, ~5 minutes.
  3. Make a 3-inch well, add 1 tsp oil, then the eggs (2); scramble nearly through, then stir into the rice.
  4. Add soy sauce (3 Tbsp), toss well, serve hot.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1 Tbsp unseasoned rice vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp honey
  • 1 tsp sesame oil
  • 1/4 tsp dried crushed red pepper
  • 1 (12-oz) package extra-firm tofu, drained, 3/4-inch cubes, patted dry
  • 1/4 cup water + 1 tsp cornstarch
  • 2 Tbsp vegetable oil, divided
  • 6 oz fresh shiitakes, stemmed, caps quartered
  • 8 oz sugar snap peas, trimmed
  • 4 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 Tbsp minced peeled fresh ginger
  • 4 green onions, sliced on the diagonal

Method

  1. Whisk soy, vinegar (1 Tbsp), honey, sesame oil and red pepper (1/4 tsp); marinate tofu (1 (12-oz) package) 30 minutes. Drain, then whisk the water and cornstarch into the marinade.

Ingredients

  • 2 containers firm or extra-firm tofu, pressed
  • 1/3 cup soy sauce
  • 3/4 cup water

  • 3 cloves garlic, minced

Ingredients

  • 1 (14–16 oz) container extra-firm tofu
  • 3/4 cup Sriracha
  • 1 Tbsp roasted sesame seeds, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1 Tbsp white miso paste
  • 3 chopped scallions, plus more
  • 2 garlic cloves

Method

  1. Cut tofu (1 (14–16 oz)) into 1-inch blocks; drain well on paper towels.

Ingredients

  • 1 (16-oz) pkg extra-firm tofu
  • 1/3 cup soy sauce

  • 1 Tbsp minced garlic
  • 1 Tbsp paprika
  • 2 tsp black pepper
  • 1 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1 tsp garlic powder

Method

Ingredients

  • 10 medium flour tortillas
  • Vegan cheese shreds, green onion, cilantro, 1 avocado — for topping

Filling

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, divided
  • 1 small onion, chopped
  • 4 cloves garlic, minced
  • 2 cups (1 can) black beans, drained
  • 3 tsp chili powder, divided
  • Salt
  • 1 block extra-firm tofu, crumbled
  • 2 cups kale, destemmed, thin-sliced
  • 1/3 cup chopped bell pepper
  • 1/2 cup salsa
  • 1/3 cup vegan cheddar shreds

Sauce

  • 1/3 of the bean mixture
  • 1 cup salsa

  • 1 tsp chili powder

Method

  1. Sauté onion (1 small), garlic (4 cloves), beans (2 cups) and 2 tsp chili powder in 1 Tbsp oil until the onion is translucent; set aside.

  3. Bake until melted and golden, ~15 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 small onion, thinly sliced
  • 3 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 1/4 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 bunch lacinato kale, stemmed, torn
  • 2 (15-oz) cans white beans, rinsed
  • 3/4 cup vegetable broth or bean liquid
  • 1/3 cup heavy cream (or more broth + a knob of butter)
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1/2 cup grated Parmesan or Gruyère
  • 1/2 cup panko tossed with 1 Tbsp olive oil
  • Lemon wedges

Method

  1. In an ovenproof skillet, soften onion (1 small) in oil, ~5 minutes; add garlic (3 cloves) and flakes (1/4 tsp) 1 minute.
  2. Wilt in the kale (1 bunch) with a pinch of salt. Add beans (2 (15-oz) cans), broth and cream; simmer 5 minutes, mashing a few beans to thicken. Season.
  3. Top with cheese, then the oiled panko. Broil until deep golden and bubbling, 2–4 minutes.
  4. Lemon over the top; serve from the skillet with crusty bread.

The crispy-halloumi-tomato-bean skillet's winter cousin.

Ingredients

  • Olive oil, as needed
  • 1 lb cherry or grape tomatoes, halved lengthwise
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced or pressed
  • 1 Tbsp finely chopped parsley, plus more
  • 1 tsp honey, plus more
  • 1/2 tsp dried oregano or thyme
  • Salt and black pepper
  • 1 (15-oz) can cannellini, butter or navy beans, drained
  • 1 (8-oz) block halloumi, 1/4-inch slices
  • 1/2 lemon
  • Crusty bread, optional

Method

  1. Broiler on high, rack 3–4 inches from heat.
  2. In a large ovenproof pan over medium: 2 Tbsp oil, tomatoes, garlic (2 cloves), parsley (1 Tbsp total), honey and oregano; season and cook, stirring, until the tomatoes (1 lb) soften and release juices, ~10 minutes.
  3. Stir in beans; heat through ~3 minutes. Season; off heat.
  4. Arrange halloumi (1 (8-oz) block) on top; broil until golden and crispy, ~5 minutes.
  5. Drizzle with olive oil, squeeze the lemon (1/2) over, a light thread of honey (1 tsp total), parsley. Serve immediately with bread.

Ingredients

Checca sauce

  • 1 pint cherry tomatoes, halved
  • 3 scallions (white/pale green), coarsely chopped
  • 3 cloves garlic, chopped
  • 8 fresh basil leaves
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil
  • Pinch salt and pepper

Lentil loaf

  • 3/4 cup lentils (~5 oz)
  • 3 cups reduced-sodium vegetable broth
  • 1 cup short-grain brown rice, rinsed
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped white onion
  • 1/2 cup shredded carrots
  • 1 celery rib, sliced
  • 1/2 cup frozen corn
  • 2 Tbsp butter, divided
  • 10 oz baby spinach (~4 cups)
  • 1 1/2 cups cubed mozzarella, divided
  • 2 eggs, lightly beaten
  • 1/4 cup + 2 Tbsp grated Parmesan
  • 1/3 cup chopped fresh basil
  • 1/2 tsp salt, 1 tsp pepper
  • 1 tomato, sliced

Method

  1. Checca: pulse tomatoes (1 pint), scallions, garlic (3 cloves), basil (1/3 cup) and oil until coarsely chopped (not puréed); season.
  2. Bring lentils (3/4 cup) just to a boil in cold water; drain and rinse. Boil the broth (3 cups), add rice, cover and simmer low 10 minutes. Stir in lentils, onion (1/2 cup), carrot and celery; cover and cook until tender and absorbed, ~30 minutes. Off heat, scatter corn (1/2 cup) on top, stand 5 minutes; fluff, stand 5 more.
  3. Oven 350°F. Butter a 10×4 1/2×3 loaf pan (1 Tbsp). Wilt spinach (10 oz) ~3 minutes; squeeze dry and chop.
  4. Gently mix the lentil-rice (1 cup) mixture, spinach, 1 cup mozzarella, eggs (2), 1/4 cup Parmesan, basil, salt (1/2 tsp), pepper and half the checca. Spoon into the pan.
  5. Top with tomato (1) slices, remaining 1/2 cup mozzarella and 2 Tbsp Parmesan; dot with the last 1 Tbsp butter.
  6. Bake uncovered until heated through and golden, ~30 minutes. Rest 15 minutes; slice 2 inches thick, serve with remaining checca.

Ingredients

  • 3 cups textured vegetable protein (TVP)
  • 2 1/2 cups boiling vegetable broth
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped onion
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 medium-large carrot, finely chopped or shredded
  • 1 celery stalk, finely chopped or shredded
  • 3/4 cup whole wheat flour
  • 1 tsp salt, 1/4 tsp pepper
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder (or 2 cloves, pressed)
  • 1/2 tsp each dry oregano and marjoram
  • 1 tsp dry basil
  • 1/4 cup ketchup
  • 6 oz tomato paste

Method

  1. Mix TVP and boiling broth (2 1/2 cups); stand 10 minutes.
  2. Sauté onion (1/2 cup) in oil until clear; add to the TVP with carrot (1), celery, flour, seasonings and ketchup (1/4 cup).
  3. Pack tightly into an oiled bread pan, smooth the top, spread tomato paste (6 oz) over.
  4. Bake at 350°F for 45 minutes. Loosen the edges; cut into 16 slices.

Ingredients

  • 2 1/4 lb fresh spinach (or 1 1/2 lb frozen chopped)
  • 1 lb yigandes beans (or large limas)
  • 1 leek, white only, finely chopped
  • 1 bunch green onions, finely chopped
  • 1 bunch fresh dill, thick stems removed, chopped
  • 1/2 lb feta, crumbled
  • 3 ripe tomatoes, peeled, seeded, chopped (or 14.5-oz can, drained)
  • 1/2 cup olive oil
  • 3/4 cup dried bread crumbs
  • 2 tsp sea salt, 1/4 tsp pepper

Method

  1. Soak beans (1 lb) overnight; drain. Cover with 3× cold water, boil, then slow-boil 1 hour. Drain.
  2. Clean and coarsely chop the spinach (2 1/4 lb); squeeze handfuls dry, toss with the salt (2 tsp) in a colander and drain. (Frozen: just thaw and squeeze.)
  3. Oven 340°F. Oil an 11×14 pan with 2 1/2-inch sides.
  4. Toss spinach with onions (1 bunch), leeks, dill (1 bunch) and half the feta (1/2 lb). Layer half in the pan, all the beans, then the rest of the spinach mixture. Top with remaining feta, then tomatoes (3), pepper, the oil, and an even dusting of breadcrumbs.
  5. Bake ~1 1/2 hours — check until just a little oil is left at the bottom. Rest 20–30 minutes; serve warm or room temp.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb dried Greek giant beans
  • 1/2 cup extra-virgin Greek olive oil
  • 2 red onions, finely chopped
  • 3 cups plum tomatoes, peeled, seeded, chopped
  • 2 cups water
  • 3 Tbsp honey
  • 1 bunch fresh dill, chopped
  • 1/4 cup red wine vinegar
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste
  • Salt, pepper
  • 2–3 Tbsp crumbled feta, garnish

Method

  1. Soak beans (1 lb) overnight; drain. Boil in fresh water, skim, then simmer 1–1 1/2 hours until tender but firm. Drain, reserving the water.
  2. Caramelize the onions (2) lightly in 2 Tbsp of the oil, ~15 minutes. Oven 375°F.
  3. In a large baking pan: beans, 6 Tbsp oil, onions, tomatoes (3 cups), a little bean water and the honey (3 Tbsp); mix.
  4. Cover with foil, bake 1–1 1/2 hours until tender in a thick creamy sauce, topping up water so they don't burn.
  5. Fifteen minutes before done: stir in dill (1 bunch), vinegar (1/4 cup), tomato paste, salt and pepper. Serve hot, warm or room temp; feta (2–3 Tbsp) over.

Ingredients

  • 1 kg (2 lb) long skinny eggplant
  • 1/2 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil
  • 2 large red onions, chopped
  • 2 cloves garlic, chopped
  • 2 tomatoes, grated, skins discarded (or 1 Tbsp tomato paste in 1 cup water)
  • 1/2 cup chopped parsley
  • 2 bay leaves
  • 1 1/2 cups or more water or vegetable stock
  • 250 g (1 1/2 cups) sour trahana or bulgur
  • Black pepper

Method

  1. Stem eggplants, cut into 1-inch-thick slices; soak 30–60 minutes in salted water. Squeeze out the liquid.
  2. Sauté onions (2 large) and garlic in the oil until softening; add eggplant (1 kg) and color on both sides.
  3. Mix in tomatoes (2) (or diluted paste), parsley (1/2 cup) and bay; cover 5 minutes to draw the liquid, then simmer, adding a little liquid if it sticks.
  4. When the eggplant is half done, stir in trahana/bulgur (250 g) and 1 cup liquid; cover and simmer until absorbed and tender, adding more as needed. Finish with pepper.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups strained Greek yogurt
  • 2 large eggs, lightly beaten
  • 1 1/2–2 cups water
  • 2 Tbsp unsalted butter, melted
  • 2 1/2 cups crumbled feta
  • 2–2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1/4 cup milk
  • 3 Tbsp extra virgin olive oil

Method

  1. Oven 325°F. Mix yogurt (2 cups), eggs and 1 1/2 cups water; add melted butter (2 Tbsp). Add 2 cups flour, mix well, stir in 1 1/2 cups of the feta — thick batter; adjust flour (2–2 1/2 cups)/water as needed.
  2. Pour into a buttered ~30–45 cm pan; spread evenly.
  3. Mix remaining feta (2 1/2 cups) with milk and oil; pour over the surface, everywhere.
  4. Bake on the middle rack until set and golden, ~1 hour 10 minutes. Cool slightly, cut.

Ingredients

Crust

  • 1 small head broccoli (2–3 cups riced)
  • 2 eggs
  • 1/4 cup Parmesan + 1/4 cup mozzarella
  • 1/4 tsp each salt and pepper
  • 1/2 tsp Italian seasoning, optional

Topping

  • 1/4 cup pizza sauce
  • 1 cup cheese
  • Veggies, optional

Method

  1. Oven 400°F; line a sheet with parchment (recommended) or grease.
  2. Rice the broccoli (1 small head) in a food processor or on a grater. Cover and microwave 1–2 minutes; cool a minute, then wring dry in a clean towel.
  3. Mix broccoli, eggs (2), cheeses and seasoning (1/2 tsp); shape into a 1/2-inch-thick crust on the sheet.
  4. Bake 10–12 minutes until lightly browned. Add sauce, cheese (1 cup) and toppings; bake 10–12 more until melted. Cool 5 minutes before cutting.

Ingredients

  • 1 small-medium head cauliflower (2–3 cups once processed)
  • 1/4 tsp kosher salt
  • 1/2 tsp each dried basil and oregano (crushed finer)
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder
  • Optional: crushed red pepper
  • 1/4 cup shredded Parmesan + 1/4 cup mozzarella
  • 1 egg (larger head: 1 egg + 1 white)
  • Optional: 1 Tbsp almond meal (if only ~2 cups cauliflower)
  • Sauce, cheese and toppings of choice

Method

  1. Pizza stone (or sheet) in the oven; heat to 450°F. Oil a piece of parchment on a cutting board.
  2. Pulse florets ~30 seconds to powdery "snow" (2–3 cups). Microwave covered 4 minutes; dump onto a tea towel and cool.
  3. Wring the cooled cauliflower (1) hard — squeeze out as much water as possible. This is what makes it chewy, not crumbly.
  4. Mix with salt (1/4 tsp), herbs, garlic powder (1/2 tsp), pepper flakes, cheeses, almond meal if using, and the egg. Press into a tight, evenly thick crust on the oiled parchment.
  5. Slide parchment onto the hot stone; bake 8–11 minutes until golden. Top with sauce, cheese, toppings; bake 5–7 more until bubbly.
  6. Cool a minute or two; slice.

Ingredients

  • 1 Quorn Turk'y Roast, thawed
  • 2 cloves garlic, minced
  • 4 Tbsp apple cider vinegar
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil, plus extra
  • 1 bunch each thyme, rosemary, sage
  • 1/2 tsp pepper
  • 3 Tbsp butter (or Earth Balance)
  • 1 large potato, 1-inch chunks
  • 1/2 onion, chopped
  • 3 medium carrots, peeled and chopped

Method

  1. Cut 1-inch-deep slits all over the roast (1). Muddle oil, vinegar (4 Tbsp), garlic, pepper (1/2 tsp) and 1 tsp crushed leaves of each herb; spoon into the slits, then bag with the rest and refrigerate 8–12 hours, turning.
  2. Oven 350°F. Set the roast in a small roasting pan surrounded by the vegetables (sweet potato (1 large) or squash welcome). Drizzle remaining marinade over; salt and oil the veg (not the roast — it's salty enough).
  3. Dot the roast with butter (3 Tbsp), arrange remaining herb sprigs around, cover tightly with foil.
  4. Roast 1 hour; uncover and broil ~5 minutes to brown.

Ingredients

  • 12 oz paneer, 3/4-inch cubes
  • 3 Tbsp ghee or neutral oil
  • 1 tsp cumin seeds
  • 1 onion, thinly sliced
  • 1 Tbsp ginger-garlic, minced
  • 1 cup basmati rice, rinsed until clear
  • 1 1/2 cups water
  • 1 cup frozen peas
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • Cilantro, lime, yogurt to serve

Method

  1. Brown the paneer (12 oz) in ghee on two sides; remove.
  2. Bloom cumin seeds (1 tsp) in the same pot; soften the onion (1) until golden, then ginger-garlic (1 Tbsp) 1 minute.
  3. Stir in rice (1 cup) to coat. Add water and salt (1 tsp); cover and simmer on low 12 minutes.
  4. Scatter peas (1 cup) and paneer on top, cover, 3 more minutes; rest 10 off heat. Fluff with cilantro and lime; yogurt alongside.

Ingredients

  • 1 small green cabbage, in 8 wedges through the core
  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 cups marinara (see Dressings & Sauces)
  • 8 oz low-moisture mozzarella, grated
  • 1/2 cup grated Parmesan
  • Basil and red pepper flakes

Method

  1. Roast the wedges, oiled and seasoned, at 450°F until charred at the edges and tender at the core, 25–30 minutes.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 1 (10–12 oz) package super-firm tofu
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp neutral oil
  • 4 scallions, sliced on an angle, dark greens reserved
  • 1–2 Thai chiles or 1 small serrano, thinly sliced (optional)
  • 3 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 1 Tbsp roasted black or white sesame seeds
  • Cooked rice and cilantro, to serve

Sauce

  • 3 Tbsp tamari or soy sauce
  • 1 Tbsp Chinese black vinegar
  • 1 tsp sugar or maple syrup
  • 1 Tbsp gochugaru (Korean chile flakes)
  • 1 Tbsp toasted sesame oil

Method

  1. Wrap the tofu (1 (10–12 oz)) in a thin towel and press gently with your palms to shed some water — don't crush it. Grate it on the large holes of a box grater (thinly slice any pieces that break off).
  2. Heat the oil in a large nonstick skillet over medium-high. Sizzle the scallions (4), chiles and garlic, stirring often, until the garlic (3 cloves) is lightly golden, ~2 minutes.
  3. Add the grated tofu and toss to coat. Leave it 2 minutes undisturbed, then keep cooking, stirring every 2 minutes, until golden in spots, 10–14 minutes total.
  4. Whisk the sauce. Pour it in — it bubbles hard — and stir to coat everything, 1 minute. Off heat, add the sesame seeds (1 Tbsp).
  5. Serve over rice with the scallion greens and cilantro. Keeps 4–5 days refrigerated.

Ingredients

  • 2 (15-oz) cans chickpeas, rinsed and patted dry
  • 1 pint cherry tomatoes
  • 1 small red onion, in wedges
  • 1/4 cup olive oil, plus more
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika or cumin
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 1 (8-oz) block feta
  • Lemon; mint or parsley; pita to serve

Method

  2. Nestle the feta (1 (8-oz) block) block in the middle; brush it with oil.
  3. Roast at 400°F until the tomatoes burst and the feta is soft and golden-edged, 25–30 minutes.
  4. Break the feta into everything, squeeze lemon over, shower with herbs; scoop with pita.

Ingredients

  • 1 (14-oz) block extra-firm tofu
  • 2 Tbsp cornstarch
  • Kosher salt
  • Neutral oil, for shallow frying
  • Dipping sauce: 2 Tbsp soy, 1 Tbsp rice vinegar, 1 tsp sugar, 1 sliced scallion, sesame seeds

Method

  1. Press the tofu (1 (14-oz) block) well (or freeze and thaw for extra chew — that's where the long time goes). Tear into craggy, bite-size chunks; rough edges fry crispiest.

  3. Shallow-fry in 1/4 inch of hot oil, turning, until deeply golden on all sides, 8–10 minutes. Drain.

  • 1 (14-oz) can coconut milk

Ingredients

  • 1 (14-oz) block extra-firm tofu, in 1/2-inch slabs, pressed dry
  • 1 cup soft herbs (parsley, cilantro, basil, chives)
  • 1 small garlic clove
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil + 1 Tbsp lemon juice
  • 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 2 Tbsp neutral oil, for searing

Method

  1. Blitz (or finely chop) the herbs with garlic (1 small clove), olive oil, lemon, flakes (1/2 tsp), salt and pepper into a loose salsa verde.

  3. Marinate in reverse: spoon the herb sauce over the hot tofu and rest 5 minutes so it drinks it in while staying crisp.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 lb broccoli, in long florets

  • 1 Tbsp toasted sesame oil

Ingredients

  • 6 dried shiitakes, soaked in 1 cup hot water, finely chopped (water reserved)
  • 3 Tbsp neutral oil
  • 2 Tbsp doubanjiang (fermented chili-bean paste)
  • 3 garlic cloves + 1 Tbsp ginger, minced
  • 2 scallions, sliced, whites and greens separated
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce + pinch of sugar
  • 1 (14-oz) block soft or silken tofu, 1-inch cubes
  • 1 Tbsp cornstarch + 2 Tbsp water
  • 1/2 tsp ground Sichuan peppercorns; chile oil
  • Rice, to serve

Method

  1. Fry the chopped shiitakes (6) in oil until browned — they're the 'mince'.
  2. Add doubanjiang (2 Tbsp), garlic, ginger and scallion whites; fry until the oil stains red, ~1 minute.
  3. Add the mushroom water, soy and sugar; slide in the tofu (1 (14-oz) block) and simmer gently 5 minutes without stirring hard.
  4. Thicken with the cornstarch slurry; finish with Sichuan pepper, chile oil and scallion greens over rice.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 12 large green or savoy cabbage leaves
  • 2 cups cooked rice
  • 1 cup cooked lentils or finely chopped sautéed mushrooms
  • 1 onion, finely chopped and softened
  • 1/4 cup each chopped dill and parsley
  • 2 Tbsp currants + 2 Tbsp pine nuts (optional)
  • Kosher salt and pepper
  • 2 cups tomato sauce thinned with 1/2 cup water
  • Juice of 1/2 lemon; olive oil

Method

  1. Blanch the leaves 2 minutes until pliable; shave down the thick ribs.
  2. Mix rice (2 cups), lentils, onion (1), herbs, currants, pine nuts, salt and pepper.
  3. Roll a few spoonfuls in each leaf, sides tucked; pack seam-down in a pot.
  4. Pour the tomato sauce and lemon over, weight with a plate, and simmer covered 40 minutes. Rest before serving; drizzle with oil.

Ingredients

  • 1 large head cabbage
  • 1 1/2 cups cooked rice
  • 10 oz mushrooms, finely chopped and browned
  • 1/2 cup walnuts, toasted and chopped
  • 1 onion, chopped and softened
  • 1 egg (or 2 Tbsp tomato paste to bind)
  • 1 tsp paprika; salt and pepper
  • Sauce: 1 (28-oz) can crushed tomatoes, 2 Tbsp brown sugar, 2 Tbsp red wine vinegar

Method

  1. Core the cabbage (1 large head) and blanch, peeling off 12 leaves as they soften; trim the ribs.
  2. Mix rice (1 1/2 cups), mushrooms, walnuts (1/2 cup), onion, egg and seasonings; roll into the leaves.
  3. Stir the sauce together; spread a third in a baking dish, add the rolls seam-down, pour the rest over.
  4. Cover and bake at 350°F about 1 hour, uncovering for the last 15, until saucy and tender throughout.
  5. Vegetarian take on the classic sweet-and-sour version.

Ingredients

  • 3 large leeks, halved lengthwise, washed well
  • 2 Tbsp butter + 1 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp white miso whisked into 1/2 cup hot water
  • 1 (15-oz) can white beans, rinsed
  • Black pepper; squeeze of lemon
  • Chopped dill or chives; crusty bread

Method

  1. Brown the leeks (3 large) cut-side down in the butter and oil, 4–5 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 1 (14-oz) block extra-firm tofu, pressed, 1-inch cubes
  • 1 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 1 tsp toasted sesame oil
  • 1 Tbsp cornstarch
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder
  • Dip: soy, rice vinegar, scallion, sesame seeds

Method

  1. Toss the cubes with soy and sesame oil (1 tsp), then with cornstarch (1 Tbsp) and garlic powder (1/2 tsp) to coat.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 2 (14-oz) blocks extra-firm tofu, sliced lengthwise 1-inch thick
  • 2 Tbsp brown sugar
  • 2 Tbsp white miso
  • 2 Tbsp soy sauce
  • 2 Tbsp rice vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp vegetable oil
  • 1/4–1/2 tsp ground cayenne

Method

  1. Press the slices between towels under a weighted board, 15–45 minutes.
  2. Stir sugar (2 Tbsp), miso, soy, vinegar (2 Tbsp), oil and cayenne (1/4–1/2 tsp) smooth in a large bowl. Tear the tofu (2 (14-oz) blocks) into ragged ~1-inch pieces (torn edges char better than cut ones) and coat gently. Cook now, or marinate up to 24 hours.
  3. Broiler high, rack ~6 inches down, foil-lined sheet. Lift the tofu out (keep the marinade) and spread it out.
  4. Broil, flipping occasionally and rotating for hot spots, until browned and crisp, 10–15 minutes.
  5. Drizzle the reserved marinade over. Nobu's miso (2 Tbsp)–black cod logic: the sugared miso chars into a crust that keeps the inside custardy. Rice + a broiled green vegetable and dinner's done.

Corrected 2026-07-10 to the real Slagle method – torn pieces, wet marinade, double batch. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 1 (14-oz) block extra-firm tofu, cubed, patted dry
  • 2 Tbsp cornstarch
  • 3 Tbsp neutral oil
  • 8 oz sugar snap peas, trimmed
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • 1 tsp coarsely ground black pepper — be generous
  • Zest and juice of 1 lemon
  • 2 tsp soy sauce + 1 tsp honey

Method

  1. Toss tofu (1 (14-oz) block) in cornstarch and salt; sear in oil until golden all over. Remove.
  2. Flash the snap peas (8 oz) in the pan 2 minutes — bright and snappy.
  3. Lower the heat; melt butter (2 Tbsp) with the pepper until fragrant, then lemon zest, juice, soy and honey.
  4. Return the tofu, toss until everything is glossy.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/4 cups brown jasmine rice, rinsed
  • 1 Tbsp coconut or olive oil
  • 1 small white onion, chopped
  • 1 Tbsp finely grated fresh ginger
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 bell peppers (mixed colors), thin 2-inch strips
  • 3 carrots, sliced diagonally 1/4-inch
  • 2 Tbsp Thai red curry paste
  • 1 (14-oz) can coconut milk + 1/2 cup water
  • 1 1/2 cups packed thinly sliced lacinato kale
  • 1 1/2 tsp coconut or brown sugar
  • 1 Tbsp tamari or soy sauce
  • 2 tsp rice vinegar or lime juice
  • Basil/cilantro, red pepper flakes, sriracha to serve

Method

  1. Boil the rice (1 1/4 cups) like pasta 30 minutes; drain, return to the pot, cover and rest 10. Salt and fluff before serving.
  2. Soften the onion (1 small) with a pinch of salt in the oil, ~5 minutes; ginger (1 Tbsp) and garlic 30 seconds.
  3. Add peppers (2) and carrots; cook until fork-tender, 3–5 minutes. Fry in the curry paste (2 Tbsp) 2 minutes.
  4. Add coconut milk, water, kale (1 1/2 cups) and sugar; gently simmer 5–10 minutes until everything is how you like it.
  5. Off heat: tamari and vinegar; adjust salt/punch/acidity. Bowls of rice, curry over, garnishes on top.

Companion to the Thai tofu curry already on the board — this one is the all-vegetable version.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup neutral oil
  • 1/2 cup each finely chopped onion, celery and green bell pepper (the trinity)
  • 8 oz cremini mushrooms, finely chopped
  • 1 Tbsp minced garlic
  • 2 cups long-grain rice, rinsed
  • 2 tsp Cajun seasoning
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper; pinch of cayenne
  • 3 cups mushroom broth
  • 1 tsp soy sauce or Worcestershire (our upgrade – pushes it toward the livery register)
  • 1 (15-oz) can black-eyed peas, rinsed
  • 1/2 cup sliced scallions
  • Hot sauce, to serve

Method

  1. Heat the oil (1/4 cup) in a deep skillet or Dutch oven. Brown the mushrooms (8 oz) hard – darker than feels polite; that char is where the "dirty," almost-liver depth lives.
  2. Add the trinity (1/2 cup each onion, celery, green pepper) and cook until soft, ~5 minutes; garlic (1 Tbsp) for 1 more.
  3. Stir in the rice (2 cups) with the Cajun seasoning (2 tsp), black pepper and cayenne; toast a minute.
  4. Add the mushroom broth (3 cups) and soy sauce (1 tsp); bring to a boil, then cover and simmer low until the rice absorbs the liquid, ~15 minutes.
  5. Fold in the black-eyed peas (1 can) and warm through 3 minutes. Fluff with the scallions (1/2 cup); hot sauce at the table.

The vegetarian answer to Louisiana dirty rice – mushrooms and black-eyed peas standing in for the chicken livers that traditionally make it "dirty." Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Soft omelette over melted, buttery zucchini.

One of the few recipes still subscriber-only on her site – opens with your login. Paste the text here anytime and it becomes a full card. Open recipe →

Ingredients

  • 4 Tbsp unsalted butter
  • 4 Tbsp olive oil
  • ½ small head cabbage (about 1½ lb.), cut into 1" wedges
  • Kosher salt
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 large shallot, thinly sliced
  • 2 (15 oz.) cans large white beans (butter, gigante or cannellini), drained and rinsed
  • 8 oz. tomatoes, halved or chopped depending on size
  • 1 Tbsp white wine, sherry or white distilled vinegar
  • 1 cup water
  • Parmesan, ricotta or feta, for serving (optional)

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F.
  2. Brown the cabbage (½ head) in butter (4 Tbsp) and olive oil (4 Tbsp) in a big skillet, about 8–10 minutes per side.
  3. Add the shallot (1) and cook 5–7 minutes until tender.
  4. Move cabbage and shallot to a baking dish with beans (2 cans), tomatoes (8 oz.), vinegar (1 Tbsp) and water (1 cup); season and nestle the vegetables on top.
  5. Bake 50–60 minutes until the liquid reduces and the top caramelizes; serve with cheese if wanted.

Can be made 2 days ahead and refrigerated; reheat covered at 425°F for 20–30 minutes or rewarm on the stovetop. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 lb. dried chickpeas
  • 2 large shallots or 1 large onion, sliced ¼" thick
  • 1 head garlic, unpeeled, halved crosswise
  • 2 tsp crushed red pepper flakes
  • 1–2 bay leaves or a few sprigs thyme, oregano or rosemary
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper (about 1½ Tbsp salt)
  • ¼ cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • About 10 cups water
  • A few spoonfuls Calabrian chili (chopped, crushed or paste)
  • 1 lemon, halved

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 275°F.
  2. Put chickpeas (1 lb.), shallots or onion, garlic (1 head), red pepper flakes (2 tsp), bay leaves and salt (about 1½ Tbsp) in a large ovenproof pot with olive oil (¼ cup).
  3. Add water (about 10 cups, roughly 2" above the chickpeas), cover and bake undisturbed 10–12 hours.
  4. Scoop chickpeas and broth into a bowl and top with Calabrian chili and a squeeze of lemon.

No soaking needed; keeps 5 days refrigerated, rewarm in its broth. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Eggplant

  • 1 large globe eggplant (about 2 lb.), sliced ½"–¾" thick
  • ½ cup olive oil, divided
  • Kosher salt and black pepper

Tomato sauce

  • 1 small onion, thinly sliced
  • 4 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • Crushed red pepper flakes (optional)
  • 4 anchovy fillets (optional; omit for vegetarian)
  • 1 (28 oz.) can whole San Marzano tomatoes, crushed

Assembly and topping

  • ¾ cup panko bread crumbs
  • ⅓ cup grated Parmesan
  • 2–3 Tbsp capers, coarsely chopped
  • 2 Tbsp fresh oregano or marjoram (or ½ dried)
  • ⅓ cup chopped parsley, divided
  • 8 oz. fresh mozzarella, sliced or torn

Method

  1. Roast the eggplant slices (2 lb.) at 450°F with half the olive oil (¼ cup), salt and pepper until deeply browned and soft, 25–30 minutes.
  2. Brown the onion (1) and garlic (4 cloves) in the remaining oil (¼ cup), add red pepper flakes, then the crushed tomatoes (28 oz.) and simmer 15–30 minutes.
  3. Toast the panko (¾ cup) in a little oil until golden, 5–7 minutes, then mix with Parmesan (⅓ cup), capers (2–3 Tbsp), oregano and half the parsley (⅓ cup total).
  4. Layer sauce, eggplant, crumb mixture and mozzarella (8 oz.) in a baking dish; repeat the layers.
  5. Bake at 450°F until browned and bubbling, 15–20 minutes, and finish with the rest of the parsley.

Anchovies are optional, so skip them for veg. Components keep 2–3 days ahead; assemble just before baking; reheat leftovers at 400°F for about 25–30 minutes. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup olive oil, plus more as needed
  • 4 medium leeks, white and light green parts, thinly sliced
  • 1 lb. mixed mushrooms (maitake, oyster, cremini or chanterelle), torn into bite-size pieces (about 5 cups)
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1¾ cups pearled or semi-pearled farro or barley
  • 4 cups vegetable broth
  • 2 cups water
  • ½ cup finely chopped chives (about 1 bunch)
  • 1 cup fresh dill leaves, coarsely chopped
  • 1 Tbsp finely grated lemon zest, plus lemon wedges
  • Sour cream, for serving

Method

  1. In a Dutch oven, cook half the leeks (2) and half the mushrooms (½ lb.) in olive oil (about 2 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until browned and crisp, 12–15 minutes; remove to a bowl.
  2. Add more oil and repeat with the remaining leeks and mushrooms.
  3. Toast the farro (1¾ cups) in the empty pot about 5 minutes, then add broth (4 cups) and water (2 cups), season, and simmer 20–25 minutes until tender.
  4. Stir in half the chives (¼ cup).
  5. Toss dill (1 cup), the remaining chives (¼ cup) and lemon zest (1 Tbsp); serve the farro with sour cream, crispy vegetables, the herb mix and lemon wedges.

Source allows vegetable or chicken broth; vegetable broth used here. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more for drizzling
  • ½ medium onion, thinly sliced or chopped
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 large bunch kale (preferably Lacinato/Tuscan)
  • Crushed red pepper flakes
  • 8–10 large eggs, whisked to blend
  • Parmesan or white cheddar (optional)
  • Fresh herbs and lemon juice, for topping (optional)

Method

  1. Strip the kale (1 bunch) from its stems; slice the stems thin and tear the leaves.
  2. Cook the onion (½) in hot olive oil (2 Tbsp) with salt until soft with charred edges, 4–6 minutes.
  3. Add kale with salt and pepper, let it char undisturbed, then toss until wilted; add red pepper flakes and a drizzle of oil.
  4. Pour in the seasoned eggs (8–10), stir briefly, lower the heat to medium-high and let the bottom brown and the edges puff.
  5. Finish by baking at 450°F for about a minute or flipping the whole frittata.

Great made ahead and eaten all week, especially with garlicky yogurt. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Slow-cooked potato-onion tortilla; her aioli card is on the board too.

One of the few recipes still subscriber-only on her site – opens with your login. Paste the text here anytime and it becomes a full card. Open recipe →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup olive oil
  • 2 small or 1 large onion, quartered through the root
  • 1–2 heads garlic, halved crosswise
  • 1 lemon, halved crosswise
  • 1 small bunch dried herbs (oregano, marjoram, thyme or rosemary)
  • A few dried chiles (or 1 fresh chile, halved lengthwise)
  • 1 lb. dried beans (about 2 cups)
  • 6 cups water
  • Kosher salt

Method

  1. Warm olive oil (¼ cup) in a large pot and brown the onions (1–2), garlic (1–2 heads) and lemon (1) for a few minutes.
  2. Add the herbs and chile (a few).
  3. Add the beans (1 lb.) and water (6 cups) with salt and bring to a boil.
  4. Drop to a bare simmer and cook uncovered until tender, 50–120 minutes depending on the beans, topping up the water as needed.

Old beans are the only real failure; keeps well as a pot to eat from for weeks. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp coconut oil or olive oil, plus more for drizzling (ghee or melted butter also work)
  • 4 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • ½ lemon, thinly sliced into half-moons, seeds removed
  • 3 scallions, thinly sliced
  • ¼ cup finely chopped cilantro stems
  • ¼ tsp turmeric (optional but recommended)
  • 1 cup dried red or yellow lentils
  • 4 cups water (or broth)
  • Kosher salt and black pepper

Method

  1. Fry garlic (4 cloves) and half the lemon slices (¼ lemon) in oil (3 Tbsp) until the garlic is golden and the lemon is browned at the edges, 3–4 minutes.
  2. Stir in most of the scallions (3, save some), cilantro stems (¼ cup, save some) and salt, then toast the turmeric (¼ tsp) for about a minute.
  3. Add lentils (1 cup) and water (4 cups) and simmer until they collapse into a creamy daal, thinning with water if needed.
  4. Serve topped with the raw lemon, remaining scallions and cilantro, a drizzle of oil and pepper.

Use red or yellow lentils, not green or black; water or vegetable broth. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp neutral oil
  • 1 large onion, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 4 lightly salted matzo boards
  • 6 large eggs
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • Chives, scallions and/or dill (optional)
  • Sour cream or Greek yogurt (optional)

Method

  1. Brown the onion (1 large) in the oil (2 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until deeply frizzled, 8–10 minutes.
  2. Whisk the eggs (6) with salt and pepper.
  3. Break the matzo (4 boards) into quarters; soak in hot water 60–90 seconds until pliable, then squeeze dry.
  4. Fold the matzo into the eggs; rest a minute or two.
  5. Add to the onions with the butter (2 Tbsp); cook over medium just until the eggs set, ~1 minute. Flaky salt, herbs, sour cream.

Surprisingly good cold the next day – her words, confirmed widely. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Soups, Stews & Chili

Ingredients

  • 3/4 cup quinoa
  • 8 cups water, plus more
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 garlic clove, finely chopped
  • 1 jalapeño, seeded, finely chopped
  • 1/2 lb Yukon Gold potatoes, peeled, small dice
  • 1 tsp ground cumin
  • 1 tsp kosher salt, plus more
  • 1 bunch scallions, thinly sliced (white/light green)
  • 3 cups thinly sliced spinach
  • 4 oz feta, small dice (~1 cup)
  • 1/3 cup chopped cilantro
  • Black pepper; chopped egg to top

Method

  1. Rinse quinoa (3/4 cup) until the water runs clear. Boil in the 8 cups water, then simmer until the casings pop, 10–15 minutes. Strain over a bowl; top up the cooking liquid to 6 cups.
  2. Heat oil until shimmering; bloom garlic (1 clove) and jalapeño 30 seconds. Add potatoes (1/2 lb), cumin and salt (1 tsp total); cook ~3 minutes without browning the garlic.
  3. Add the reserved liquid and half the scallions (1 bunch); simmer until potatoes are tender, ~12 minutes.
  4. Add quinoa, spinach (3 cups) and remaining scallions; simmer 3 minutes. Off heat, stir in feta (4 oz) and cilantro (1/3 cup); season. Top with chopped egg.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp coconut or sunflower oil
  • 1 medium onion, finely diced
  • 1 Tbsp medium curry powder
  • 1/4 tsp chilli flakes
  • 2 garlic cloves, crushed
  • 4 cm fresh ginger, finely chopped
  • 150 g red lentils, rinsed
  • 400 g tinned chopped tomatoes
  • 25 g coriander stalks, 2 cm pieces + 5 g leaves
  • Salt and pepper
  • 400 g tin coconut milk
  • Lime wedges

Method

  1. Fry the onion (1 medium) in oil over medium-high 8 minutes until caramelized. Add curry powder (1 Tbsp), chilli, garlic (2 cloves) and ginger (4); 2 minutes more, stirring.
  2. Add lentils (150 g), then tomatoes (400 g), coriander stalks, 600 ml water, 1 tsp salt and plenty of pepper.
  3. Whisk the coconut milk (400 g) smooth; reserve 4 Tbsp, add the rest. Boil, then simmer gently 25 minutes until the lentils are soft but hold shape.
  4. Serve drizzled with the reserved coconut milk, coriander leaves on top, lime wedges alongside.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup olive oil
  • 4 garlic cloves, chopped
  • 1 yellow onion, sliced
  • 1 (2-inch) piece ginger, finely chopped
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 1/2 tsp ground turmeric, plus more
  • 1 tsp red pepper flakes, plus more
  • 2 (15-oz) cans chickpeas, drained and rinsed
  • 2 (15-oz) cans full-fat coconut milk
  • 2 cups vegetable stock
  • 1 bunch Swiss chard, kale or collards, stemmed and torn
  • 1 cup mint or cilantro leaves
  • Yogurt, toasted pita, lime — to serve

Method

  1. Soften garlic (4 cloves), onion (1) and ginger in the oil over medium with salt and pepper, 3–5 minutes.
  2. Add turmeric (1 1/2 tsp total), pepper flakes (1 tsp total) and chickpeas (2 (15-oz) cans); season and fry, stirring, until the chickpeas start to brown and crisp, 8–10 minutes. Mash a few against the pot.
  3. Add coconut milk (2 (15-oz) cans) and stock; season. Simmer, stirring occasionally and smashing more chickpeas as you go, until thickened, 30–35 minutes.
  4. Fold in the greens until wilted, ~3 minutes. Adjust salt.
  5. Bowl it with mint/cilantro, a spoon of yogurt, lime, and pita for dragging.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb dried chickpeas, soaked overnight (or 4 cans, rinsed)
  • 2/3 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil, divided
  • 2 red onions, finely chopped
  • 2 carrots, diced
  • 2 bay leaves + 1 sprig rosemary
  • 8 cups water or vegetable stock
  • Juice of 2 lemons, plus wedges
  • 2 Tbsp flour (optional, for body)
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • Chopped parsley; crusty bread and olives to serve

Method

  1. Soften onions (2) and carrots in 1/3 cup oil, ~10 minutes. Add drained chickpeas (1 lb), bay and rosemary; stir to coat.
  2. Add water/stock (8 cups); simmer partly covered until completely tender — 1–1 1/2 hours dried, 25 minutes canned. Salt in the last 20 minutes.
  3. Whisk lemon juice with the flour (if using) and a ladle of hot broth; stir back in and simmer 5 minutes until slightly silky. Or skip the flour and just add lemon.
  4. Finish with the remaining oil, pepper and parsley. Serve with lemon wedges, bread, olives.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp arame
  • 4 1/2 cups filtered water, divided
  • 1/3 cup quinoa
  • 1 (1-inch) piece kombu
  • 1/2 onion, 3/4-inch pieces
  • 2 tsp extra-virgin olive oil
  • 2 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 1/2 lb firm tofu, 6 slices, each quartered
  • 1/2 carrot, halved lengthwise, 1/4-inch slices
  • 5 fresh shiitakes, stemmed, caps sliced
  • 2–3 Tbsp white (shiro) miso
  • 1 cup very thinly sliced bok choy or napa cabbage
  • 1 tsp tamari, to taste
  • 1 scallion, thinly sliced; 1/2 tsp dulse flakes

Method

  1. Soak arame (2 Tbsp) in 1 cup of the water.
  2. Wash quinoa (1/3 cup) in 3 changes of water; simmer uncovered with kombu (1 (1-inch) piece) in 1 cup water until just tender, ~20 minutes. Drain; discard kombu.
  3. About 10 minutes before the quinoa is done: brown the onion (1/2) in oil ~5 minutes; garlic (2 cloves) 30 seconds.
  4. Add tofu (1/2 lb), carrot, shiitakes (5) and remaining 2 1/2 cups water; simmer covered until the carrot (1/2) is just tender, ~5 minutes. Stir in quinoa; off heat.
  5. Whisk miso (2–3 Tbsp) with 1/4 cup of the stew liquid; stir back in. Add drained arame, bok choy and tamari (1 tsp).
  6. Serve topped with scallion and dulse.

Ingredients

  • Extra-virgin olive oil
  • 4 celery stalks, diced (~1 1/3 cups)
  • 4 medium carrots, peeled, diced (~1 1/3 cups)
  • 1 medium yellow onion, diced (~1 1/2 cups)
  • 2 bay leaves
  • Kosher salt, black pepper
  • 2 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 2 cups crushed tomatoes in juice
  • 3 cups cooked beans (cannellini, corona, borlotti) + cooking liquid, or 2 cans, rinsed
  • 1 oz grated Parmesan (~1/3 cup), rind reserved (omit for vegan)
  • 3–4 cups vegetable broth or water
  • 2 bunches lacinato kale, stemmed, thinly sliced (~6 cups)
  • 1/2 small head green or savoy cabbage, thinly sliced (~3 cups)

Method

  1. Soften onion (1 medium), celery and carrots (4 medium) in a few Tbsp oil over medium until golden, 8–10 minutes. Garlic (2 cloves) and bay 1 minute; season.
  2. Add tomatoes, scraping the pot, then the beans with their liquid.
  3. Add the Parmesan rind and 3–4 cups broth; simmer partly covered 20–25 minutes.
  4. Stir in kale (2 bunches) and cabbage (1/2 small head); 10–15 minutes more until tender. Season.
  5. Remove bay and rind. Serve with grated Parmesan (1 oz), an oil drizzle and crusty bread.

Ingredients

  • 2 lb large tomatoes, halved
  • 1/2 lb country bread, crust removed, 1/2-inch pieces (~4 cups)
  • 1 red bell pepper, seeded, diced
  • 1 cup chopped peeled English cucumber
  • 1 cup chopped red onion
  • 1/4 cup sherry vinegar
  • 1/4 cup extra-virgin olive oil
  • 1 garlic clove, minced
  • 1/2 tsp each ground cumin and paprika
  • 1 cup water

Method

  1. Squeeze the tomato halves over a strainer to catch ~1/2 cup juice; discard seeds. Chop.
  2. Combine tomatoes (2 lb) and juice with bread (1/2 lb), pepper (1), cucumber (1 cup), onion, vinegar, oil, garlic (1 clove), cumin and paprika (1/2 tsp); stand 1 hour at room temp.
  3. Purée in batches with the 1 cup water; press through a coarse strainer. Season; chill 2 hours to 1 day. Serve cold.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup + 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 3 medium red onions, finely chopped
  • 4 garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 large poblanos, seeded, finely chopped
  • 1 large red bell pepper, seeded, finely chopped
  • 1 medium jalapeño, minced
  • 1/3 cup pure chile powder, divided
  • 1 tsp ground cumin
  • 1 (35-oz) can Italian peeled tomatoes in juice
  • 1 cup canned tomato sauce
  • 1/2 tsp dried oregano
  • Salt
  • 1 lb extra-firm tofu, drained, patted dry
  • 1 (19-oz) can black beans, drained
  • 1/2 cup finely chopped cilantro

Method

  1. In a heavy casserole, cook onions (3 medium), garlic, poblanos (2 large), bell pepper (1 large), jalapeño, 1/4 cup of the chile powder (1/3 cup total) and cumin (1 tsp) in 1/4 cup oil over moderate heat until slightly softened, ~10 minutes.
  2. Chop the tomatoes; add with juice (1 (35-oz) can), tomato sauce, oregano (1/2 tsp) and 2 tsp salt. Simmer moderately-low until the vegetables are soft, ~15 minutes.
  3. Dice tofu (1 lb) 1/2-inch, pat dry, toss with the remaining 4 tsp chile powder. Sear in 2 Tbsp oil over moderately-high 3 minutes to toast the chile; salt. Add to the pot, deglazing the skillet with 1/3 cup water into the chili.
  4. Stir in beans (1 (19-oz) can); simmer 10 minutes, stirring. Half the cilantro (1/2 cup) in, half on top.

Best after standing an hour — or overnight.

Ingredients

  • 1 pkg (12 oz) Smart Ground Original
  • 1 cup each white onion, green bell pepper, celery
  • 1 can red kidney beans, undrained
  • 1 can whole kernel corn, undrained
  • 2 cans whole peeled tomatoes, undrained
  • 1 (6-oz) can tomato paste
  • 2 cloves garlic
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp each chili powder and ground cumin
  • 1 tsp salt
  • 2 Tbsp vegetable oil

Method

  1. Sauté onion (1 cup), pepper and celery in the oil until translucent.
  2. Add everything else; simmer 1–1 1/2 hours.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups cranberry beans, soaked overnight
  • 2 bay leaves
  • 1/2 cup extra virgin Greek olive oil
  • 1 red onion, finely chopped
  • 2 medium carrots, peeled, diced
  • 1 red bell pepper or 2 Florina peppers in brine, diced
  • 2 large garlic cloves, minced
  • 2 tsp ground cayenne, or more
  • 2 scant tsp ground cumin
  • 1/2 tsp or more ground cinnamon
  • 1 (28-oz) can chopped tomatoes
  • 1 tsp dried Greek oregano
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste in 1 cup water
  • 2 cups diced pumpkin or butternut (~3/4 lb)
  • Salt; 2 Tbsp Greek balsamic vinegar
  • 1/2 cup each chopped cilantro and parsley
  • 1/2 cup crumbled feta, optional

Pita chips

  • 3–4 pitas, each cut in 6 wedges
  • 1/2 cup olive oil, 1 Tbsp coarse sea salt
  • 2 garlic cloves, slivered; 2 Tbsp Greek oregano

Method

  1. Cook drained beans (2 cups) with bay in ample water until softened but not mushy, 35–40 minutes; drain, keep warm. (Do 1–2 days ahead.)
  2. Sauté onion (1) and carrot in half the oil ~8 minutes; add pepper and garlic (2 large cloves) a few minutes.
  3. Stir in the spices; as they darken, add tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can). Simmer; add beans, squash and oregano (1 tsp). Salt.
  4. Add the tomato-paste water; simmer covered 35–40 minutes until tender and thick. Vinegar in the last 10 minutes; herbs in the last minute.
  5. Chips: toss pita with oil, salt, garlic and oregano; bake at 350°F until crisp, 7–8 minutes, turning once.
  6. Serve hot, drizzled with remaining oil, feta (1/2 cup) over.

Ingredients

  • 1 small kabocha or butternut squash (~1 1/2 lb), peeled, 1-inch cubes
  • 3 Tbsp olive oil, divided
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 4 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 2-inch piece ginger, minced
  • 1 tsp each ground turmeric and cumin
  • 1/2 tsp red pepper flakes
  • 1 cup yellow split peas, rinsed
  • 5 cups vegetable stock
  • 2 handfuls spinach or chard
  • 1 lime; cilantro to finish

Method

  1. Roast the squash (1 small) with 2 Tbsp oil, salt and pepper at 425°F until browned and tender, ~25 minutes.
  2. Meanwhile soften the onion (1) in the remaining oil; add garlic (4 cloves), ginger and spices for 1 minute.
  3. Add split peas (1 cup) and stock; simmer partly covered until the peas are soft and stew-thick, 35–45 minutes.
  4. Fold in the squash and greens until wilted; season, finish with lime and cilantro.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp coconut or neutral oil
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 3 garlic cloves + 1-inch ginger, minced
  • 2 Tbsp curry powder
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 1 cup red lentils, rinsed
  • 1 (14-oz) can coconut milk
  • 4 cups vegetable stock
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 lime; cilantro

Method

  1. Soften the onion (1) in oil; add garlic, ginger, curry powder (2 Tbsp) and tomato paste (2 Tbsp) and fry 2 minutes.
  2. Add lentils (1 cup), coconut milk (1 (14-oz) can), stock (4 cups) and salt. Simmer 20–25 minutes until the lentils collapse.
  3. Blend half for body if you like; brighten with lime, top with cilantro.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 2 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 1 lb carrots, sliced
  • 1/2 head cauliflower, in florets
  • 1 tsp ground cumin
  • 5 cups vegetable stock
  • Kosher salt
  • Zest and juice of 1 lemon

Method

  1. Soften onion (1) and garlic (2 cloves) in the oil. Add carrots (1 lb), cauliflower (1/2 head), cumin (1 tsp) and salt; stir 2 minutes.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 4 garlic cloves, chopped
  • 2 Tbsp chili powder + 1 tsp cumin
  • 3 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 3/4 cup farro, rinsed
  • 2 (15-oz) cans pinto or black beans, with liquid
  • 1 (28-oz) can crushed tomatoes
  • 2 1/2 cups water or stock
  • Kosher salt
  • Toppings: cheddar, sour cream, scallions, lime

Method

  1. Soften the onion (1); add garlic (4 cloves), spices and tomato paste (3 Tbsp) and fry until brick-dark, 2 minutes.
  2. Add farro (3/4 cup), beans (2 (15-oz) cans) with their liquid, tomatoes (1 (28-oz) can), water and salt.
  3. Simmer, stirring now and then, until the farro is chewy-tender and the chili thick, 30–40 minutes (splash in water as needed).
  4. Season and load up the toppings.

Ingredients

  • 1/3 cup olive oil + 2 sprigs rosemary
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 3 garlic cloves, sliced
  • 1 large head cauliflower, in florets
  • 4 cups vegetable stock
  • Kosher salt and white or black pepper
  • Squeeze of lemon

Method

  1. Warm the oil with the rosemary over low until fragrant, ~5 minutes; set the oil aside, discard sprigs.
  2. In 2 Tbsp of that oil, soften onion (1) and garlic (3 cloves). Add cauliflower (1 large head), stock (4 cups) and salt; simmer until very soft, ~20 minutes.
  3. Blend completely smooth; season with pepper and lemon.
  4. Bowl it and drizzle generously with the rosemary oil.

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • 2 garlic cloves, minced
  • 1 Tbsp tomato paste
  • 1 tsp ground cumin
  • Kosher salt, black pepper, pinch of cayenne
  • 4 cups stock + 2 cups water
  • 1 cup red lentils, rinsed
  • 1 large carrot, diced
  • Juice of 1/2 lemon; cilantro

Method

  1. Soften the onion (1) and garlic (2 cloves) in oil. Stir in tomato paste (1 Tbsp), cumin, salt, pepper and cayenne; fry 2 minutes.
  2. Add stock, water, lentils (1 cup) and carrot. Simmer uncovered until the lentils fall apart, ~30 minutes.
  3. Purée about half (immersion blender, quick pulse) so it's creamy but textured.
  4. Off heat: lemon juice. Serve with cilantro, an oil drizzle and a dusting of chili.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp unsalted butter (or olive oil)
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more for drizzling
  • 1 large yellow, white or red onion, chopped
  • 4 garlic cloves, thinly sliced or chopped
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1 tsp cumin seed or ½ tsp ground cumin (optional)
  • 1 tsp crushed red pepper flakes, plus more (optional)
  • 1 small acorn, kabocha or butternut squash (1¾–2 lb.), peeled, seeded, cut into ½"–1" pieces
  • 1½ cups yellow or red lentils
  • 8 cups water or broth
  • 2–3 tsp sherry vinegar or white wine vinegar
  • 2 cups coarsely chopped mixed herbs (cilantro, dill, chives, scallion)

Method

  1. Soften the onion (1, chopped) in butter (2 Tbsp) and olive oil (2 Tbsp) with a pinch of salt, about 8–10 minutes.
  2. Stir in the spices (cumin, 1 tsp red pepper flakes), then add the garlic (4 cloves) and squash (1¾–2 lb.) and cook until the squash starts collapsing, 15–20 minutes.
  3. Add lentils (1½ cups) and water or broth (8 cups); simmer gently 30–35 minutes until the lentils and squash have mostly melted into a creamy soup.
  4. Season with vinegar (2–3 tsp) and salt and pepper.
  5. Ladle into bowls and top with herbs (2 cups) and a drizzle of olive oil.

Keeps 5 days refrigerated or 2 months frozen; stir in the herbs fresh when serving. Use water or vegetable broth. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ¼ cup olive oil, plus more
  • 5 garlic cloves (4 thinly sliced, 1 for grating)
  • Kosher salt
  • 1 lb. mixed mushrooms (oyster, maitake, baby bella, shiitake), bite-size pieces
  • 1 medium leek or 6 scallions, thinly sliced
  • 1 (12 oz.) package soft or silken tofu
  • 3 Tbsp soy sauce, plus more to taste
  • 1 Tbsp white or rice wine vinegar, plus more to taste
  • Freshly ground black pepper or crushed red pepper flakes
  • 5 cups water
  • Crushed red pepper flakes, chili oil, chili crisp or toasted sesame oil, for serving

Method

  1. Fry the sliced garlic (4 cloves) in olive oil (¼ cup) until golden, 2–3 minutes; lift out onto a plate and salt it.
  2. In the same pot, brown the mushrooms (1 lb.) hard with salt and pepper.
  3. Wilt most of the leek (1, keep some raw for serving), then add soy sauce (3 Tbsp), vinegar (1 Tbsp), grated garlic (1 clove) and water (5 cups); simmer gently 5–10 minutes.
  4. Tear the tofu (12 oz.) into the broth and warm it through 5–10 minutes; adjust soy and vinegar.
  5. Serve topped with the toasted garlic, raw leek and chili oil or flakes.

Water-based broth, so no chicken stock needed; thorough mushroom browning is what builds the flavor. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3 Tbsp canola or olive oil
  • 4 garlic cloves, thinly sliced
  • 2 large shallots, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and ground black pepper
  • 1½ lb. mixed mushrooms (maitake, oyster, cremini or shiitake), torn into bite-size pieces
  • 1–2 fresh red or green chiles (such as Fresno), thinly sliced, or ¾ tsp red-pepper flakes
  • ½ cup low-sodium soy sauce, plus more to taste
  • ¼ cup unseasoned rice wine vinegar, plus more to taste
  • 8 cups water
  • 8–10 oz. noodles (udon, soba, rice or spaghetti)
  • 2 cups fresh herbs (cilantro, mint, chives, parsley or a mix), for serving
  • Sesame seeds and/or sesame oil, for serving (optional)

Method

  1. Cook garlic (4 cloves) and shallots (2) in oil (3 Tbsp) with salt and pepper until golden, 3–4 minutes.
  2. Add mushrooms (1½ lb.) and half the chile (1–2 chiles) and cook until deeply browned and soft, 10–15 minutes.
  3. Pour in soy sauce (½ cup), rice vinegar (¼ cup) and water (8 cups); simmer gently 15–20 minutes and adjust seasoning.
  4. Boil noodles (8–10 oz.) separately in salted water until just al dente, then drain.
  5. Divide noodles among bowls, ladle over the soup, and top with the remaining chile, herbs (2 cups) and sesame.

Vegan as written; umami comes from soy sauce and browned mushrooms. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 3 garlic cloves, finely grated or chopped
  • 1 jalapeño, finely grated or chopped
  • 2 lemons (zested and juiced)
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 4 cups vegetable broth
  • ¼ cup soy sauce
  • 10 oz. mushrooms (enoki, white button, cremini or oyster)
  • 1 bunch asparagus, ends trimmed, thinly sliced
  • 1½ cups peas (fresh or frozen)
  • 1 (12–14 oz.) package soft tofu, drained (or firm tofu, bite-size pieces)
  • Olive oil, for drizzling
  • 4 scallions, very thinly sliced

Method

  1. Mix garlic (3 cloves), jalapeño (1) and lemon zest (2 lemons) in a bowl with salt and pepper.
  2. Bring broth (4 cups) and soy sauce (¼ cup) to a simmer and taste for seasoning.
  3. Simmer the mushrooms (10 oz.) 3–5 minutes, then add asparagus (1 bunch), peas (1½ cups) and half the garlic-jalapeño mix; cook 2–4 minutes until bright.
  4. Put tofu (12–14 oz.) in bowls, spoon on the rest of the garlic mix and ladle broth and vegetables over.
  5. Finish with olive oil, lemon juice and scallions (4).

Source offers vegetable or chicken broth; vegetable broth used here. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 large yellow or white onions, finely chopped
  • 4 garlic cloves, chopped
  • 3/4 tsp ground cumin
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 1 1/2–2 lb zucchini or summer squash, large chunks
  • 1 jalapeño, very thinly sliced
  • 8 cups vegetable broth
  • 2 (15-oz) cans hominy, drained and rinsed
  • 3 ears corn, kernels cut off (~2 1/2 cups frozen works)
  • 1 bunch cilantro, finely chopped
  • 2–4 limes, halved

Method

  1. Warm the oil (2 Tbsp) in a big pot over medium-high and brown three-quarters of the onions, the garlic (4 cloves) and cumin (3/4 tsp) with salt and pepper until soft and browned at the edges, 5–8 minutes.
  2. Add the squash (1 1/2–2 lb) and half the jalapeño; season again and cook until it starts to soften and color, 8–10 minutes.
  3. Pour in the broth (8 cups), hominy (2 cans) and corn (3 ears); simmer 20–25 minutes until the squash collapses into the broth.
  4. Stir in the rest of the jalapeño and half the cilantro; adjust seasoning.
  5. Bowl it; finish with the reserved raw onion, remaining cilantro and a hard squeeze of lime.

Keeps 3 days (store without cilantro/lime) or freezes for months; add the fresh finishers when serving. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp butter + 2 Tbsp olive oil, plus more
  • 1 large onion, thinly sliced
  • Kosher salt and black pepper
  • 2 (15-oz) cans white beans, rinsed
  • 4 cups vegetable broth
  • 1/4 head cabbage (~8–10 oz), coarsely chopped
  • 1 Tbsp white vinegar or lemon juice
  • 1 cup dill, coarsely chopped
  • Sour cream, to serve (optional)

Method

  1. Frizzle the onion (1 large) in butter (2 Tbsp) and oil (2 Tbsp) over medium-high with salt and pepper until crisp-edged, 5–8 minutes; set a quarter aside for topping.
  2. Add the beans (2 cans); crush some against the pot so the broth goes creamy.
  3. Broth in (4 cups); simmer 15–20 minutes. Add cabbage (1/4 head) and vinegar (1 Tbsp); simmer 10–15 more until silky.
  4. Stir in half the dill (1/2 cup). Serve topped with the rest, the reserved onions, oil, pepper and sour cream.

Her recipe allows chicken broth – vegetable keeps it on-board. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Slow Cooker

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup olive oil
  • 4 onions, chopped
  • 2 green + 2 red bell peppers, seeded, chopped
  • 4 cloves garlic, minced
  • 1 (14-oz) package firm tofu, drained, cubed
  • 4 (15.5-oz) cans black beans, drained
  • 2 (15-oz) cans crushed tomatoes
  • 2 tsp salt, 1/2 tsp black pepper
  • 2 tsp ground cumin
  • 6 Tbsp chili powder
  • 2 Tbsp dried oregano
  • 2 Tbsp distilled white vinegar
  • 1 Tbsp hot pepper sauce (Tabasco)

Method

  1. Sauté onions (4) in the oil over medium-high until softening; add peppers, garlic (4 cloves) and tofu (1 (14-oz) package) and cook until lightly browned and tender, ~10 minutes total.
  2. Beans (4 (15.5-oz) cans) into the slow cooker on Low; stir in the vegetables and tomatoes (2 (15-oz) cans).
  3. Season with salt (2 tsp), pepper, cumin (2 tsp), chili powder, oregano (2 Tbsp), vinegar (2 Tbsp) and hot sauce. Stir gently, cover, cook on LOW 6–8 hours.

Ingredients

  • 1 tsp olive oil
  • 1 large onion, diced
  • 1 Tbsp kosher salt, divided
  • 2 medium red or yellow potatoes, diced
  • 1 Tbsp curry powder
  • 1 Tbsp packed brown sugar
  • 1 Tbsp grated fresh ginger
  • 3 cloves garlic, minced
  • 1/8 tsp cayenne (optional)
  • 2 cups low-sodium vegetable broth, divided
  • 2 (15-oz) cans chickpeas, drained, rinsed
  • 1 green + 1 red bell pepper, diced
  • 1 medium head cauliflower, bite-size florets
  • 1 (28-oz) can diced tomatoes with juices
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper
  • 1 (10-oz) bag baby spinach
  • 1 cup coconut milk

Method

  1. Sauté onion (1 large) with 1 tsp of the salt ~5 minutes; add potatoes (2 medium) and another 1 tsp salt until edges turn translucent.
  2. Add curry, sugar (1 Tbsp), ginger (1 Tbsp), garlic and cayenne (1/8 tsp), 30 seconds; deglaze with 1/4 cup broth and scrape into a 6-qt slow cooker.
  3. Add remaining broth (2 cups total) and salt (1 Tbsp total), chickpeas, peppers, cauliflower (1 medium head), tomatoes and pepper (1/4 tsp) — liquid should come halfway up. Cover; HIGH 4 hours.
  4. Stir in spinach (1 (10-oz) bag) and coconut milk (1 cup); rest a few minutes to wilt. Season. Serve alone or over couscous/orzo.

Halve for a smaller cooker.

Ingredients

  • 1 Tbsp extra-virgin olive oil
  • 1 lb ground turkey or chicken (93% lean)
  • 1 medium yellow onion, diced
  • 1 cup uncooked quinoa
  • 2 (10-oz) cans enchilada sauce (mild or medium)
  • 1 (15-oz) can black beans, drained, rinsed
  • 1 (15-oz) can fire-roasted diced tomatoes in juices
  • 1 cup corn kernels
  • 1 red + 1 green bell pepper, cored, diced
  • 2 Tbsp chili powder, 1 Tbsp cumin, 1 tsp garlic powder
  • 1 cup shredded Mexican-blend cheese, divided
  • To serve: cilantro, avocado, green onion, sour cream/Greek yogurt

Method

  1. Brown the turkey and onion (1 medium) in oil, breaking up, ~5 minutes; into the slow cooker.
  2. Add quinoa (1 cup), enchilada sauce, beans (1 (15-oz) can), tomatoes, corn, peppers, spices and 1/2 cup water; stir.
  3. HIGH 2 1/2–3 hours or LOW 5–6, until the liquid is absorbed and quinoa tender.
  4. Stir in 1/2 cup cheese, top with the rest; cover 10–15 minutes on high to melt. Serve with toppings.

Ingredients

Soup

  • 1 lb dried green split peas
  • 1 small onion, chopped
  • 2 medium carrots, peeled, diced
  • 2 celery stalks, diced
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 2 bay leaves
  • 1 tsp salt; black pepper
  • 6 cups water

Croutons

  • 4 slices sourdough, bite-size squares
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil
  • 4 oz smoked cheese (gouda or mozzarella), thin slices

Method

  1. Layer peas (1 lb), onion, carrot, celery (2 stalks), oil, bay, salt and pepper in the slow cooker; add water (no stirring). Cook HIGH 5–6 hours or LOW 8–10, until soft.
  2. Stir to incorporate; season.
  3. Croutons: broiler on. Toss bread in oil on a sheet; lay cheese (4 oz) on half the pieces, broil to melt, sandwich together and broil golden. Serve hot on the soup.

Breads & Crackers

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 cups (360 ml) warm water (100–110°F)
  • 2 3/4 tsp instant or active dry yeast
  • 4 cups (520 g) bread flour, plus more
  • 1 Tbsp granulated or brown sugar (or barley malt syrup)
  • 2 tsp salt
  • Nonstick spray or 2 tsp olive oil, for the bowl
  • Egg wash: 1 egg white + 1 Tbsp water

Boil

  • 2 quarts water
  • 1/4 cup (60 g) honey (or barley malt syrup)

Method

  1. Whisk warm water (1 1/2 cups) and yeast (2 3/4 tsp) in a stand-mixer bowl; cover 5 minutes. Add flour (4 cups total), sugar (1 Tbsp) and salt; beat on low with the dough hook 2 minutes — stiff, slightly dry dough.
  2. Knead by hand on a floured surface 6–7 minutes (too heavy for the mixer). Poke test: slow bounce-back = ready.
  3. Rise in a greased, covered bowl 60–90 minutes until doubled.
  4. Line two sheets. Punch down, divide into 8, ball up, poke a 1.5–2-inch hole through each. Rest under a towel while the bath heats.
  5. Oven 425°F. Boil the 2 quarts water with honey (1/4 cup) whisked in; reduce to medium-high. Boil bagels 2–4 at a time, 1 minute per side.
  6. Egg-wash tops and sides; 4 per sheet.
  7. Bake 20–25 minutes, rotating halfway, until dark golden. Cool 20 minutes on the pans, then rack.
  8. Keep airtight a few days, or refrigerate up to a week.

Ingredients

  • 2 tsp (6 g) active dry yeast
  • 4 1/2 tsp (19 g) granulated sugar
  • 1 1/4 cups (300 ml) warm water (± 1/4 cup more)
  • 3 1/2 cups (440 g) bread + high-gluten flour (up to 1/2 cup more for kneading)
  • 4 1/2 tsp vital wheat gluten
  • 1 1/2 tsp (6 g) salt
  • Egg wash; toppings of choice

Method

  1. Pour sugar (4 1/2 tsp) and yeast (2 tsp) into 1/2 cup of the warm water; don't stir. After 5 minutes, stir to dissolve.
  2. Mix flour, wheat gluten (4 1/2 tsp) and salt; well in the middle; add the yeast mixture and 1/3 cup warm water, mixing in more water as needed for a moist, firm dough.
  3. Knead ~10 minutes on a floured counter, working in flour for a stiff dough.
  4. Rise in an oiled, towel-covered bowl 1 hour until doubled; punch down, rest 10 minutes.
  5. Divide into 8; ball each by dragging against the counter in circles. Flour a finger, punch through the center, stretch the ring to about a third of the bagel's diameter. Rest 10 minutes on an oiled sheet; heat oven to 425°F.
  6. Boil a large pot of water; lower bagels in. They float in seconds: 1 minute per side (2 minutes per side for chewier, more NY-style).
  7. Top as they come out of the water (or egg-wash then top). Bake on a lined sheet 20–25 minutes until golden. Cool on a rack — or don't, and eat one hot.

Ingredients

Dough

  • 2 tsp Platinum yeast
  • 1 Tbsp granulated sugar
  • 3/4 cup (180 ml) whole milk, warmed to ~110°F
  • 3 Tbsp (45 g) unsalted butter, soft
  • 1 large egg
  • 2 1/3 cups (290 g) all-purpose flour, plus more
  • 1 tsp salt

Filling

  • 8 oz brick cream cheese, soft
  • 3 Tbsp butter, soft
  • 1–2 tsp Italian seasoning; 1/2 tsp garlic powder
  • 3/4 cup shredded mozzarella; 1/4 tsp salt

Topping

  • 1 Tbsp each poppy and sesame seeds
  • 1 1/2 tsp each dried minced onion and garlic flakes
  • 1 tsp coarse salt
  • 2 Tbsp melted butter

Method

  1. Whisk warm milk (3/4 cup) over yeast (2 tsp) and sugar (1 Tbsp); rest covered 5–10 minutes until frothy.
  2. Add butter (3 Tbsp), egg, flour (2 1/3 cups total) and salt (1 tsp); beat on low 3 minutes. Knead 1 minute on a floured surface (add 1–3 Tbsp flour if too sticky — keep it soft); ball it.
  3. Rise in a greased covered bowl in a warm spot 60–90 minutes until doubled (a turned-off pre-warmed 200°F oven works). Grease a 9×5 loaf pan.
  4. Beat the filling ingredients together. Mix the topping seeds (1 Tbsp)/salt in a small bowl.
  5. Punch down; roll ~1/4 inch thick on a floured surface. Spread with filling; cut 3.5–4-inch circles (~12). Fold each in half and line up in the pan, round side up.
  6. Cover; rise ~45 minutes until puffy. Oven 350°F, rack lower third.
  7. Brush with the melted butter (2 Tbsp); scatter everything seasoning generously.
  8. Bake 40–45 minutes until golden (tent with foil if browning fast). Cool 10 minutes; serve warm. Refrigerate leftovers up to a week.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup all-purpose flour
  • 1 cup yellow cornmeal
  • 2/3 cup granulated sugar
  • 2 tsp salt
  • 3 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 1/3 cup neutral oil or melted butter
  • 1 large egg
  • 1 cup milk

Method

  1. Grease a 9-inch round pan or cast-iron skillet; oven 400°F.
  2. Whisk the dry ingredients. Make a well; add oil, milk (1 cup) and egg, and stir just until combined — a few lumps are fine.
  3. Bake 20–25 minutes until deep golden and a toothpick comes out clean. Serve hot.

Yellow cornmeal for the color; not tamale-grind. Muffins ~15 minutes; doubled in a 9×13, 35–40. GF: swap 1:1 baking flour.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups almond flour
  • 1/2 tsp sea salt
  • 1 large egg, beaten
  • Optional: egg wash + seeds/black pepper on top

Method

  1. Oven 175°C; line a sheet with parchment.
  2. Mix flour (2 cups) and salt; add the egg (1 large) and mix to a dough (a processor works).
  3. Roll between two sheets of parchment to ~1/16 inch (patch into a rectangle as needed). Cut into rectangles; egg wash + seeds if you like.
  4. Bake 8–12 minutes until golden.

Ingredients

  • 1 lb pizza dough (store-bought is fine), room temp
  • 4 Tbsp melted butter
  • 2 garlic cloves, grated
  • 1 tsp Italian seasoning
  • 1 1/2 cups grated mozzarella + 1/2 cup Parmesan
  • Parsley; marinara for dipping (optional)

Method

  1. Cut the dough (1 lb) into ~24 pieces and roll into rough balls.
  2. Toss the balls in the garlic (2 cloves)-herb butter (4 Tbsp), then in the cheeses.
  3. Pile into a buttered loaf pan or skillet; rest 30 minutes.
  4. Bake at 375°F until golden, cooked through and molten between the layers, 35–40 minutes. Pull, don't slice.

Ingredients

  • 1 stick (115 g) unsalted butter, melted, plus soft butter to serve
  • 1 cup (145 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1 cup (130 g) coarse cornmeal
  • 1/2 cup (90 g) packed light brown sugar
  • 1 Tbsp baking powder
  • 1 1/4 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 tsp coarse black pepper (optional)
  • 2 large eggs
  • 3/4 cup (170 g) mayonnaise, sour cream, or a mix
  • 1/2 cup buttermilk
  • Honey and flaky salt, to serve

Method

  1. Heat oven to 400°F. Grease a 9-inch pie plate, cake pan or skillet with a spoonful of the melted butter (1 stick).
  2. Whisk flour (1 cup), cornmeal, brown sugar (1/2 cup), baking powder, salt (1 1/4 tsp) and pepper (1 tsp). Separately whisk eggs (2 large), mayo and buttermilk (1/2 cup).
  3. Combine wet into dry just until it comes together (lumps fine); fold in the rest of the melted butter.
  4. Bake until the edges are deeply browned and the top is sunflower-gold (cracks welcome), 20–25 minutes.
  5. Whip soft butter with honey and flaky salt until glossy; serve warm slices with it.
  6. Yes, mayonnaise (3/4 cup) — it's just oil and eggs. The board's other cornbread is the traditionalist option.

Ingredients

  • 3 cups/450g all-purpose flour, plus more for dusting
  • 1 tablespoon baking powder
  • 2 teaspoons kosher salt
  • 1½ teaspoons sugar
  • ¼ teaspoon baking soda
  • 1 cup/8 oz. (2 sticks) unsalted butter, chilled and cut into 1-inch pieces
  • 1¼ cups buttermilk, plus more for brushing
  • Flaky sea salt, for sprinkling (optional)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F and line a sheet pan with parchment.
  2. Whisk the flour (3 cups), baking powder (1 Tbsp), salt (2 tsp), sugar (1½ tsp) and baking soda (¼ tsp), then press the cold butter (2 sticks) into the flour with your fingers until it looks like pebbly pie dough.
  3. Add the buttermilk (1¼ cups) in two additions, mixing until a shaggy ball forms; knead briefly on a floured surface.
  4. Pat to a rectangle about 9 by 5 inches and 1½ inches thick, cut into 8 pieces, and set on the parchment.
  5. Brush tops with buttermilk, add flaky salt, and bake 20–25 minutes, turning the pan once, until deep golden with layered sides.

Dough can be chilled 1–48 hours before baking, or frozen up to 2 weeks and baked from frozen with 5–8 extra minutes. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 head garlic, cloves separated, peeled, and smashed
  • ½ cup olive oil
  • ½ cup unsalted butter, softened
  • Pinch red pepper flakes (optional)
  • Kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 1–2 baguettes or ciabatta, halved lengthwise
  • Flaky sea salt
  • Fresh parsley, finely chopped (optional)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F.
  2. Warm the garlic (1 head) in olive oil (½ cup) in a small pot over medium until sizzling, about 3 minutes, then drop to the lowest heat and cook 15–20 minutes until golden and soft.
  3. Scrape into a bowl with the butter (½ cup) and pepper flakes, mash into a paste, and season with salt and pepper.
  4. Slather over the cut sides of the bread (1–2 loaves), place on a sheet pan, and bake 8–10 minutes until crisp at the edges but soft inside.
  5. Finish with flaky salt and parsley.

Original includes an optional tin of anchovies (2 oz), omitted here. Garlic butter keeps 1 week refrigerated, tightly wrapped. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup/120g heavy cream, plus more for brushing
  • ½ cup/110g sour cream
  • 1¾ cups/255g all-purpose flour, plus more for work surface
  • 1½ cups/125g finely grated parmesan or pecorino cheese, plus more for sprinkling
  • 2–3 teaspoons coarsely ground black pepper, plus more for sprinkling
  • 1 tablespoon/12g baking powder
  • 1 tablespoon/12g sugar
  • 1¼ teaspoons/5g kosher salt
  • 1 medium leek or 6 scallions (white and light-green parts only), very thinly sliced
  • ¼ cup/12g finely chopped fresh dill (optional)
  • 1 stick/4 ounces/115g cold unsalted butter, cut into small pieces
  • Flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F and line a sheet pan with parchment; stir the heavy cream (½ cup) and sour cream (½ cup) together.
  2. Whisk the flour (1¾ cups), cheese (1½ cups), pepper (2–3 tsp), baking powder (1 Tbsp), sugar (1 Tbsp), salt (1¼ tsp), most of the leek (1) and the dill (¼ cup).
  3. Press the cold butter (1 stick) into the dry mix into flat, sizable flakes.
  4. Add the cream mixture and knead gently to a dough; shape into a 6 by 9-inch slab about 1½ inches thick and cut into 8 triangles.
  5. Optionally chill 20–30 minutes, then brush with cream and top with flaky salt, pepper, cheese and remaining leek.
  6. Bake 25–30 minutes until golden.

Dough keeps refrigerated 6–8 hours; baked scones can be made a few hours ahead and reheated. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1¼ cups (10 oz) whole milk
  • 2¼ teaspoons (1 packet) active dry yeast
  • 2 teaspoons (12g) sugar
  • 3 cups (450g) all-purpose flour
  • ½ cup finely chopped fresh dill (about 30g)
  • ½ small white or yellow onion, finely chopped (about 80g)
  • 2 teaspoons dill seed (optional; caraway or celery seed also work)
  • 2¼ teaspoons (7g) kosher salt
  • 6 tablespoons (85g) unsalted butter, melted, plus more for pan and pot
  • 1 egg, beaten (or buttermilk, milk, heavy cream or softened butter) for brushing
  • Flaky salt and cracked black pepper
  • Salted butter, for serving

Method

  1. Warm the milk (1¼ cups), take off the heat, and whisk in the yeast (1 packet) and sugar (2 tsp) until dissolved.
  2. Stir the flour (3 cups), dill (½ cup), onion (½), dill seed (2 tsp) and salt (2¼ tsp) in a large bowl, pour in the milk mixture, then work in the melted butter (6 Tbsp) to form a dough.
  3. Cover and let it double, about 90 minutes.
  4. Cut the dough into 12 pieces, shape each into a tight smooth ball, and set in a buttered 9 by 13-inch dish (or spaced on a sheet pan).
  5. Cover and rest 45–60 minutes until puffy; heat oven to 425°F.
  6. Brush with egg (1), add flaky salt and pepper, and bake 35–40 minutes until deep golden and firm; serve with salted butter.

Bake a day ahead and wrap in foil; reheat wrapped at 400°F for 8–10 minutes. Best the same day; good for breakfast sandwiches. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Dough: 1 cup whole milk or buttermilk, 1/4 cup sugar, 2 1/4 tsp active dry yeast, 3 3/4 cups flour, 1 1/2 tsp baking powder, 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt, 2 eggs, 1 stick butter in pieces
  • Filling: 1 cup light brown sugar, 1 Tbsp cinnamon, 3/4 tsp salt, 1/2 cup soft butter
  • 2/3 cup maple syrup, divided
  • Flaky salt (optional)

Method

  1. Stir milk (1 cup), sugar (1/4 cup) and yeast (2 1/4 tsp) in a stand mixer; add flour (3 3/4 cups), baking powder and salt on low to shaggy, then the eggs (2) one at a time until smooth and sticky.
  2. Knead in the butter (1 stick) piece by piece, 3–4 minutes to silky. Rise covered in the fridge 12–24 hours.
  3. Grease a 9×13; pour in about half the maple (1/3 cup). Mix the filling sugar (1 cup), cinnamon (1 Tbsp) and salt.
  4. Roll the cold dough to ~12×24 inches, spread with the soft butter (1/2 cup), scatter the filling, roll up tight from the long side; cut 12 rolls.
  5. Nestle in the dish; proof 30–35 minutes (50–60 from cold) while the oven heats to 350°F. Bake 35–40 minutes to golden.
  6. Brush the hot rolls with the remaining maple (1/3 cup); flaky salt; eat warm.

Best day-of; leftovers reheat gently in the oven. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Desserts & Baking

Ingredients

Hazelnut crust

  • 1 1/4 cups/180 g raw hazelnuts
  • 1 cup/125 g rice flour
  • 1/4 tsp salt
  • 1/2 cup/112 g sugar
  • 6 Tbsp/100 g softened butter, more as needed

Cranberry curd

  • 12 oz/340 g cranberries
  • 1 cup/225 g sugar
  • Peel (orange part only) and juice of 1 orange (~1/2 cup)
  • 4 oz/113 g softened butter (1 stick)
  • 2 eggs + 2 egg yolks

Method

  1. Roast hazelnuts (1 1/4) at 325°F 10–15 minutes until skins crack; rub skins off in a towel, cool.
  2. Grind nuts with half the rice flour (1) to coarse cornmeal; pulse in the rest with the salt (1/4 tsp).
  3. Cream sugar (1/2) and butter (6) until pale; work in the nut mixture (1–2 Tbsp extra butter or cold water if crumbly). Press into a 10-inch tart pan — half for the sides, half for the bottom. Prick; freeze 30 minutes.
  4. Bake at 350°F until lightly brown, ~15 minutes. Cool.
  5. Curd: simmer cranberries (12), sugar, orange juice and peel until popped, ~10 minutes. Mill or sieve (or blend then sieve for the most vibrant color); whisk the butter (4) into the warm purée.
  6. Beat eggs and yolks; temper with a cup of the warm liquid, combine, and cook over low until nearly bubbling and thickened, ~10 minutes. Cool (or refrigerate up to 1 day, wrap pressed to the surface).
  7. Fill the shell, smooth, bake 10 minutes at 350°F to set. Cool on a rack. Room temp up to 2 days.
