Ingredients

  • 1 pack digestive biscuits
  • 70 g butter, melted
  • 1 (397 g) tin sweetened condensed milk
  • 2 eggs
  • 3 lemons

Method

  1. Oven 180°C. Crush biscuits (1) fine, mix with butter (70 g), press into a tart tin with a glass or your palm.
  2. Mix condensed milk (1 (397 g)) and eggs. Zest one lemon; juice all three. Whisk the juice in slowly — it thickens. Add the zest.
  3. Pour over the base; bake 10 minutes. Cool, then refrigerate before serving.

Ingredients

  • 2 large eggs + 2 egg whites
  • 1/2 cup Key lime juice
  • 1 tsp grated lime rind
  • 1 (14-oz) can sweetened condensed milk
  • 1 (6-oz) graham cracker crust
  • 1 1/2 cups frozen reduced-calorie whipped topping, thawed

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Beat eggs and whites until blended; gradually beat in juice (1/2 cup), rind (1 tsp) and milk (1 (14-oz) can).
  2. Pour into the crust (1 (6-oz)); bake 20 minutes until almost set (center firms as it chills). Cool on a rack.
  3. Cover loosely, chill 4 hours; spread whipped topping (1 1/2 cups) over.

Ingredients

Crust (×2)

  • 2 1/2 cups (280 g) fine graham cracker crumbs
  • 1/3 cup + 1 Tbsp sugar
  • 1/4 tsp ground cardamom
  • 1 large pinch sea salt
  • 9 Tbsp (128 g) unsalted butter, melted

Filling

  • 3/4 cup cold water
  • 1/2 cup sugar, divided
  • 2 Tbsp + 1/4 tsp powdered gelatin (2 1/2 packets)
  • 1/2 cup heavy whipping cream, chilled
  • 4 oz cream cheese, room temp
  • 1 (30-oz) can Alphonso mango purée (3 1/4 cups)
  • 1 large pinch sea salt

Method

  1. Mix crumbs (2 1/2 cups), sugar, cardamom and salt (1 large); stir in butter (9 Tbsp). Press half into each of two 9-inch metal pie pans, compacting hard up the sides (a second pan or measuring cup helps).
  2. Freeze crusts 15 minutes; bake at 325°F until golden, ~12 minutes. Cool.
  3. Put the cold water (3/4 cup) in a large bowl. Mix 1/4 cup sugar with the gelatin; sprinkle evenly over the water; bloom a few minutes.
  4. Whip cream (1/2 cup) with remaining 1/4 cup sugar to medium-stiff peaks.
  5. Warm 1 cup of the mango purée (1 (30-oz) can) to body temperature; whisk into the gelatin until dissolved, then whisk in the rest of the purée.
  6. Soften the cream cheese (4 oz) smooth; add to the mango with the salt and immersion-blend until completely smooth. Tap out air bubbles.
  7. Fold a quarter of the mango into the whipped cream, then the cream back into the rest — no streaks.
  8. Divide between crusts, smooth, chill 5 hours or overnight. Serve chilled.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup buttermilk baking mix
  • 1 2/3 cups canned pumpkin
  • 1 (12-oz) can evaporated milk
  • 1/3 cup milk
  • 4 eggs
  • 2 tsp pumpkin pie spice
  • 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 cup each brown and white sugar

Method

  1. Oven 375°F; grease a 10-inch fluted pie pan, dust with baking mix (1/2 cup).
  2. Mix pumpkin (1 2/3 cups), both milks and eggs (4). Stir in baking mix, spice (2 tsp), vanilla and salt (1/2 tsp); gradually add the sugars.
  3. Bake 10 minutes; reduce to 350°F and bake ~35 more, until a knife near the center comes out clean.

Ingredients

  • 1 (15-oz) can 100% pumpkin purée
  • 2 eggs
  • 3/4 cup sugar
  • 1 Tbsp pumpkin pie spice (cinnamon, ginger, cloves, cardamom)
  • 1 (12-oz) can evaporated milk
  • 1 Tbsp gluten-free flour
  • 1/2 tsp salt

Method

  1. Oven 425°F; butter a pie pan. Combine sugar (3/4 cup), salt and spice.
  2. Beat eggs (2) lightly; stir in pumpkin and the sugar mix; slowly stir in evaporated milk (1 (12-oz) can), then the GF flour (1 Tbsp).
  3. Bake 15 minutes at 425°F, then 40–50 at 350°F until a knife near center comes out clean. Cool 2 hours; serve or refrigerate.

Ingredients

  • 4 eggs
  • 1/2–3/4 cup sugar, to taste
  • Pinch of salt
  • 1/2 cup ground almonds
  • 1/2 cup cream
  • 1/2 cup sliced almonds, more for garnish
  • 1 lemon, zest and juice
  • 2 Tbsp butter
  • Powdered sugar

Method

  1. Oven 400°F. Combine eggs (4), sugar (1/2–3/4 cup), salt, ground almonds (1/2 cup), cream, sliced almonds, lemon (1) zest and juice.
  2. Melt butter (2 Tbsp) in an 8-inch ovenproof skillet over low; when the foam subsides, add the batter, tilting to spread.
  3. Cook on the stovetop until the edges just set, then finish in the oven 10–15 minutes.
  4. Broil ~1 minute until just golden; dust with powdered sugar and sliced almonds (1/2 cup).

Ingredients

Crust & topping

  • 1 1/2 cups/225 g all-purpose flour
  • 1/3 cup/55 g cornmeal
  • 1/3 cup/50 g confectioners' sugar
  • 1/4 cup/55 g light brown sugar
  • 1 tsp baking powder
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 3/4 cup/170 g unsalted butter, melted, cooled slightly

Filling

  • 1 lb/455 g blueberries (or mixed berries)
  • 1/2 cup/110 g light brown sugar
  • 2 Tbsp apple cider vinegar or lemon/lime juice
  • 2 Tbsp all-purpose flour
  • Pinch kosher salt

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Whisk the dry crust ingredients; add melted butter (3/4) and mix to a coarse dough with no dry spots.
  2. Press 3/4 of it into a 9-inch tart pan (or cake/springform/8×8), evenly across the bottom and 1/2 inch up the sides — a flat measuring-cup bottom helps. Chill shell and remaining crumble.
  3. Toss the filling together; pour into the crust.
  4. Crumble the remaining mixture over in large clumps — sporadic coverage is right; berries should peek through.
  5. Bake on a rimmed sheet until bubbly and browned top and bottom, 50–55 minutes.
  6. Cool completely before slicing. Keeps tightly wrapped 2–4 days (fridge if hot/humid). Vanilla ice cream: obvious, correct.

Ingredients

  • Butter for pan
  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, plus more
  • 1 tsp baking soda + 1 tsp baking powder
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 3 large eggs
  • 1 cup granulated sugar
  • 1/2 cup fruity Greek extra-virgin olive oil
  • 2 Tbsp lemon zest (~2 lemons)
  • 1/4 cup whole milk
  • 1/4 cup lemon juice (~2 lemons)
  • Powdered sugar, for dusting

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Butter a 9×2 round pan, line with parchment, butter the parchment, flour the pan.
  2. Whisk flour (1 1/2 cups total), soda, baking powder and salt (1/2 tsp).
  3. Beat eggs (3 large), sugar (1 cup), oil and zest on medium-high until pale and thickened, ~3 minutes. On low: milk (1/4 cup), then lemon juice (1/4 cup), then the flour mixture in two additions — don't overmix.
  4. Bake until golden and a tester comes out clean, ~35 minutes. Cool 15–20 minutes, turn out rounded side up, dust with powdered sugar.

Ingredients

  • 1 3/4 cups confectioners' sugar
  • 1/4 cup lemon juice
  • Zest of one lemon (or more)
  • 1 tsp salted butter (+ tiny pinch of salt if you like)

Method

  1. Combine sugar (1 3/4 cups), juice and zest in a large glass measuring cup; add the butter (1 tsp).
  2. Microwave on high 45 seconds; whisk until smooth and lump-free.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup all-purpose flour, sifted
  • 1/2 tsp each baking powder and baking soda
  • 1/8 tsp salt
  • 2 oz unsalted butter, softened
  • 2/3 cup sugar
  • 1/2 tsp vanilla
  • 1 large egg
  • 1/2 cup Greek yogurt
  • 1 cup blueberries

Method

  1. Oven 375°F, rack middle. Line a 9-inch springform (or round) with parchment; grease the sides.
  2. Sift flour (1 cup), leaveners and salt (1/8 tsp). Separately beat butter (2 oz) and sugar until fluffy; add vanilla (1/2 tsp) and egg, beat pale and creamy.
  3. On low, add the flour in 2 batches with the yogurt (1/2 cup) in between; don't overmix.
  4. Spread in the pan; scatter blueberries (1 cup) over. Bake until golden and a tester is clean, 20–35 minutes.
  5. Cool on a rack ~40 minutes before releasing (or 30–40 in a plain pan, then invert).

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 2 tsp baking powder
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 cup plain whole-milk yogurt
  • 1 1/3 cups sugar, divided
  • 3 extra-large eggs
  • 2 tsp grated lemon zest (2 lemons)
  • 1/2 tsp vanilla
  • 1/2 cup vegetable oil
  • 1/3 cup lemon juice

Glaze

  • 1 cup confectioners' sugar
  • 2 Tbsp lemon juice

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Grease an 8 1/2 × 4 1/4 loaf pan; line the bottom, grease and flour (1 1/2 cups).
  2. Sift flour, baking powder (2 tsp), salt. Whisk yogurt (1 cup), 1 cup sugar, eggs, zest (2 tsp) and vanilla. Whisk dry into wet; fold the oil in with a spatula.
  3. Bake ~50 minutes until a tester is clean.
  4. Simmer the 1/3 cup lemon juice with remaining 1/3 cup sugar until clear. Cool cake 10 minutes, set on a rack over a sheet, and pour the syrup over while warm.
  5. When cool: whisk the glaze and pour over.

Ingredients

  • 1 1/2 cups (190 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 3 large eggs
  • 3/4 cup (180 g) Greek yogurt
  • 1 cup (200 g) packed light brown sugar
  • 1/2 cup (120 ml) vegetable/canola oil (or cooled coconut oil)
  • 1/4 cup (60 ml) fresh Ruby Red grapefruit juice, strained
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp grapefruit zest
  • 1 tsp vanilla + seeds of 1/2 vanilla bean

Glaze

  • 1 cup (120 g) confectioners' sugar, sifted
  • 2 Tbsp grapefruit juice, strained
  • 1/4 tsp vanilla

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; grease a 9×5 loaf pan.

Ingredients

  • 3 large eggs
  • 1 cup granulated sugar
  • 1 cup (8 oz) sour cream or Greek yogurt
  • 1/2 cup canola or vegetable oil
  • 2 Tbsp lemon zest
  • 1–2 Tbsp lemon extract (tablespoons — not juice, not oil)
  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 2 tsp baking powder
  • 1/2 tsp salt

Glaze

  • 1 cup confectioners' sugar
  • ~3 Tbsp lemon juice

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; grease and flour a 9×5 pan.
  2. Whisk eggs (3 large), sugar (1 cup) and sour cream; drizzle in the oil. Add zest (2 Tbsp) and extract.
  3. Add flour (1 1/2 cups), baking powder and salt (1/2 tsp); stir just combined — lumps stay.
  4. Bake 50–52 minutes until domed and a toothpick in the center crack is clean; foil-tent the last 10 minutes.
  5. Cool 30 minutes in the pan, then fully on a rack. Whisk the glaze and drizzle; spread extra on cut slices like butter. Keeps 5 days airtight (not the fridge); freezes 6 months.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup butter, melted
  • 18 oz yellow cake mix (one box)
  • 3 eggs, divided
  • 1 (8-oz) package cream cheese, quartered
  • 1/2 tsp almond or vanilla extract
  • 4 cups confectioners' sugar

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; grease a deep 9×13.
  2. Stir melted butter (1/2 cup) and 1 egg into the cake mix; press the sticky dough into the pan.
  3. Beat cream cheese (1 (8-oz) package), extract (1/2 tsp), confectioners' sugar (4 cups) and remaining 2 eggs 3 minutes until lump-free; pour over and spread.
  4. Bake 30–45 minutes with a cookie sheet on the rack below (it can bubble over), until the thin sugar crust turns darkish golden — gooey underneath is the point, don't overbake.
  5. Cool completely before cutting; dust with confectioners' sugar.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup + 2 Tbsp (225 g) sugar
  • 2 medium lemons — zest of both + juice of 1
  • 1/2 cup (110 g) butter, room temp
  • 3 eggs L, room temp
  • 3/4 cup (115 g) extra-fine corn flour
  • 2 1/2 cups (225 g) almond flour
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 1 pinch salt
  • Icing sugar, to finish

Method

  1. Grease a 9-inch/22 cm pan, line the bottom, grease the paper, dust with corn flour (3/4 cup). Oven 325°F, rack middle.
  2. Sift corn flour, almond flour (2 1/2 cups), baking powder (1 1/2 tsp) and salt.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup vegetable oil, plus more for the pan
  • 7 large eggs, separated
  • 1 cup sugar
  • 1 Tbsp almond extract
  • 4 1/2 cups + 1 Tbsp (420 g) almond flour
  • 2 tsp ground cardamom
  • 1–2 Tbsp confectioners' sugar
  • 3–4 Tbsp finely chopped pistachios

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; oil a 9-inch pan. Whip the whites stiff but not dry.
  2. Whisk yolks and sugar (1–2 Tbsp); whisk in extract (1 Tbsp) and oil, then almond flour and cardamom (2 tsp).
  3. Stir a third of the whites in to lighten, then fold in the rest just until incorporated.
  4. Bake ~50 minutes until a toothpick is clean. Cool 10 minutes, unmold, finish cooling; dust with confectioners' sugar (1 cup) and pistachios (3–4 Tbsp).

Ingredients

  • 150 g dried apricots
  • 250 ml cold water
  • 2 cardamom pods, cracked
  • 200 g ground almonds
  • 50 g fine polenta (not instant)
  • 1 tsp baking powder (GF if required)
  • 150 g caster sugar
  • 6 large eggs
  • 2 tsp lemon juice
  • 1 tsp rosewater
  • To decorate: 2 tsp apricot (or rose petal) jam + 1 tsp lemon juice, 2 1/2 tsp finely chopped pistachios

Method

  1. Boil apricots (150 g) with the water and cardamom 10 minutes — the pan should be nearly dry but not scorched. Cool off the heat.
  2. Oven 180°C. Grease a 20 cm springform; line the base.
  3. Set aside 5 apricots, torn in half. Discard cardamom husks (keep seeds); scrape the rest into a food processor.
  4. Add almonds (200 g), polenta, baking powder (1 tsp), sugar and eggs (6 large); blitz well. Scrape down, add lemon juice (2 tsp) and rosewater, blitz again.
  5. Pour into the tin; ring the apricot halves around the edge. Bake 40 minutes (foil at 30 if browning) — edges pulling away, firm top, tester nearly clean.
  6. Stir lemon juice into the jam, brush over, sprinkle pistachios; cool in the tin.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup unsalted butter
  • 3/4 cup sugar, plus more for topping
  • 1 cup unbleached flour, sifted
  • 1 tsp baking powder
  • Pinch salt (optional)
  • 2 eggs
  • 24 halves pitted purple plums (or cranberries + apple)
  • Lemon juice + ~1 tsp cinnamon, for topping

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Cream sugar (3/4 cup total) and butter (1/2 cup); add flour (1 cup), baking powder, salt and eggs (2); beat well.
  2. Spoon into an 8-, 9- or 10-inch springform. Cover the top with plum halves, skin side up. Sprinkle with sugar and lemon juice to taste, then cinnamon.
  3. Bake 1 hour. Serve plain or with whipped cream; refrigerates and freezes well.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup/114 g butter, room temp, plus more
  • 1/2 cup/100 g granulated sugar, more for sprinkling
  • 1/4 cup/55 g light brown sugar
  • 3 large eggs, room temp
  • 1 Tbsp finely grated lemon zest
  • 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1 1/4 cups/156 g all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 tsp fine sea salt
  • 1/2 tsp grated nutmeg
  • 1/4 tsp baking powder
  • 4 1/2 cups summer berries (blue/rasp/blackberries, mixed or single)
  • Cinnamon (optional) + confectioners' sugar

Method

  1. Oven 375°F; butter a 9-inch round pan.
  2. Beat butter (1/2 total) and both sugars fluffy; eggs (3 large) one at a time; zest (1 Tbsp) and vanilla.
  3. Whisk flour (1 1/4), salt, nutmeg, baking powder (1/4 tsp); mix in just combined.
  4. Fold in the berries, spread in the pan, sprinkle with granulated sugar (1/2). Bake 40–50 minutes until golden and cooked through.
  5. Cool; dust with cinnamon (if using) and confectioners' sugar (1/4).

Ingredients

  • Unsalted butter, for the pan
  • 2 lb cream cheese, room temp
  • 1 1/2 cups sugar
  • 6 large eggs
  • 2 cups heavy cream
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1/3 cup all-purpose flour
  • Sherry, for serving (optional)

Method

  1. Rack middle; oven 400°F. Butter a 10-inch springform and line with two overlapping 16×12 sheets of parchment rising 2 inches above the rim — pleats and creases are fine. Set on a rimmed sheet.
  2. Paddle cream (2 cups) cheese (2 lb) and sugar on medium-low until completely smooth, ~2 minutes.
  3. Medium speed: eggs (6 large) one at a time, 15 seconds each. Scrape; low speed: cream, salt (1 tsp), vanilla, ~30 seconds.
  4. Sift the flour (1/3 cup) over; beat low 15 seconds, scrape, then beat until silky, ~10 seconds.
  5. Bake 60–65 minutes: deeply golden brown on top, still very jiggly in the center.
  6. Cool (it collapses dramatically — correct), unmold, cool fully, peel the parchment. Serve room temp, ideally with sherry. Made a day ahead: chill, then de-chill several hours before serving.

Ingredients

Dough

  • 1/2 cup whole milk
  • 1 package (1/4 oz) active dry yeast
  • 1/3 cup sugar, plus a pinch
  • 4 1/4 cups (531 g) all-purpose flour, more as needed
  • 1 1/2 tsp fine sea salt
  • 1 tsp vanilla; 1 tsp lemon zest (optional); 1/2 tsp nutmeg
  • 4 large eggs, room temp, lightly beaten
  • 10 Tbsp (140 g) butter, room temp, plus more

Fudge filling

  • 1/2 cup sugar; 3/4 cup heavy cream or half-and-half; pinch salt
  • 6 oz extra-bittersweet chocolate (66–74%), chopped
  • 8 Tbsp (1 stick) butter, diced, room temp
  • 2 tsp vanilla

Streusel

  • 1/2 cup flour; 3 Tbsp sugar; 1 1/2 Tbsp cocoa; 1/2 tsp salt
  • 4 1/2 Tbsp (64 g) butter, melted
  • 1/3 cup mini semisweet chips

Syrup

  • 2/3 cup sugar + 2/3 cup water

Method

  1. Warm milk (1/2 cup) to ~110°F; add yeast (1 package) and a pinch of sugar (1/3 cup); foam 5–10 minutes.
  2. Mix flour (4 1/4 cups), 1/3 cup sugar, salt, vanilla (2 tsp), zest and nutmeg (dough hook or processor). Beat in yeast mixture and eggs (4 large) until a soft mass forms (~2 minutes; add flour 1 Tbsp at a time if it won't come together).
  3. Add half the butter (10 Tbsp total); beat until smooth and elastic 3–5 minutes. Rest of the butter; beat 5–7 more until smooth and stretchy.
  4. Rise in a buttered covered bowl in a warm place 1–2 hours until puffed; press down, refrigerate overnight (or 4+ hours).
  5. Filling: simmer sugar, cream and salt (1 1/2 tsp) until dissolved, ~5 minutes; stir in chocolate (6 oz), butter and vanilla until smooth. Cool to room temp. (Keeps 1 week.)
  6. Streusel: stir dry ingredients, mix in melted butter to moist crumbs, stir in chips (1/3 cup). (Keeps 3 days.)
  7. Syrup: simmer sugar and water 2 minutes.
  8. Butter two 9-inch loaf pans; line with parchment handles. Halve the dough; roll one piece 9×17. Spread half the filling edge to edge; roll into a tight coil from a long side; freeze 10 minutes. Repeat.
  9. Split each coil lengthwise, twist the halves together cut-side up, fold to ~9 inches, tuck into a pan. Rise covered 1–1 1/2 hours until puffy (or overnight in the fridge; 1 hour at room temp before baking).
  10. Oven 350°F. Clump streusel over the tops. Bake 40–50 minutes — tester meets no rubbery resistance; internal 185–210°F.
  11. Straight from the oven, skewer all over to the bottom and pour half the syrup over each. Cool completely on a rack.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup butter
  • 1 cup sugar
  • 2 eggs, beaten
  • 3 bananas, finely crushed (4 for seriously moist)
  • 1 1/2 cups flour
  • 1 tsp baking soda
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 tsp vanilla (optional)

Method

  1. Oven 350°F. Cream butter (1/2 cup) and sugar; add eggs (2) and bananas (3).
  2. Sift flour (1 1/2 cups), soda (1 tsp) and salt; add with vanilla (1/2 tsp), mixing just until combined.
  3. Greased, floured loaf pan; bake 55 minutes. Keeps well refrigerated.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups minus 2 Tbsp (8 1/2 oz) cake flour
  • 1 2/3 cups (8 1/2 oz) bread flour
  • 1 1/4 tsp baking soda
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
  • 1 1/2 tsp coarse salt
  • 2 1/2 sticks (1 1/4 cups) unsalted butter
  • 1 1/4 cups (10 oz) light brown sugar
  • 1 cup + 2 Tbsp (8 oz) granulated sugar
  • 2 large eggs
  • 2 tsp natural vanilla
  • 1 1/4 lb bittersweet chocolate disks/fèves (60%+ cacao)
  • Sea salt

Method

  1. Sift flours, soda (1 1/4 tsp), baking powder (1 1/2 tsp) and salt.
  2. Cream butter (2 1/2 sticks) and sugars very light, ~5 minutes. Eggs (2 large) one at a time; vanilla (2 tsp). Dry ingredients on low just until combined, 5–10 seconds.
  3. Fold in the chocolate disks without breaking them. Press plastic to the dough; refrigerate 24–36 hours (up to 72).
  4. Oven 350°F. Scoop 3 1/2-oz mounds (generous golf balls), 6 per lined sheet; turn any poking chocolate horizontal. Sprinkle lightly with sea salt (1 1/2 tsp).
  5. Bake 18–20 minutes until golden but still soft. 10 minutes on the sheet, then a rack. Eat warm, big napkin.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 lb (2 sticks) unsalted butter, room temp
  • 3/4 cup light brown sugar, lightly packed
  • 3/4 cup granulated sugar
  • 2 tsp vanilla
  • 2 extra-large eggs, room temp
  • 1 3/4 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1 tsp baking soda
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 1/4 cups old-fashioned oats
  • 3/4 lb bittersweet chocolate, chunked
  • 3/4 cup dried cranberries
  • Fleur de sel

Method

  1. Oven 375°F; line 3 sheets with parchment.
  2. Beat butter (1/2 lb) and both sugars light and fluffy, 3 minutes. Low speed: vanilla (2 tsp), then eggs (2) one at a time; scrape.
  3. Sift flour (1 3/4 cups), soda and salt (1 tsp); mix in the oats (1 1/4 cups). Add to the butter mixture on low — don't overbeat. Stir in chocolate (3/4 lb) and cranberries with a spatula.
  4. Scoop 1 3/4-inch balls; sprinkle lightly with fleur de sel. Bake 10–12 minutes until nicely browned. Warm or room temp.

Ingredients

  • 1/4 cup brown sugar
  • 1/2 tsp baking soda
  • 1/4 tsp salt
  • 2 Tbsp butter, room temp
  • 2 tsp vanilla
  • 1 large egg
  • 2 cups almond flour
  • 1/2 cup chocolate chips

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; line a sheet.
  2. Mix sugar (1/4 cup), soda, salt and butter (2 Tbsp) smooth. Add vanilla (2 tsp), egg and almond flour (2 cups); scrape and beat 1 minute on medium. Stir in chips (1/2 cup).
  3. Scoop level tablespoons; flatten to ~3/8 inch.
  4. Bake 9–12 minutes until golden. Cool 2 minutes on the pan, then on a rack.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup (113 g) unsalted butter, room temp
  • 1/2 cup tahini, well stirred
  • 1/2 cup granulated sugar + 1/2 cup light brown sugar
  • 1 large egg + 1 yolk
  • 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1 1/4 cups (160 g) all-purpose flour
  • 3/4 tsp baking soda
  • 3/4 tsp kosher salt
  • 6 oz bittersweet chocolate, chopped in chunks
  • Sesame seeds and flaky salt, for tops

Method

  1. Beat butter (1/2 cup), tahini and both sugars until light, ~3 minutes. Add egg, yolk and vanilla (1 tsp).
  2. Stir in flour (1 1/4 cups), soda and salt (3/4 tsp) just until combined; fold in the chocolate (6 oz). Chill the dough at least 2 hours (overnight is better — same logic as the NYT cookie).
  3. Oven 350°F. Scoop 2-Tbsp balls, roll the tops in sesame seeds, and space out on lined sheets; flaky salt on top.
  4. Bake 11–13 minutes until the edges set and the centers are still soft. Cool on the pan 5 minutes.

Cheesecake swirl

  • 12 oz cream cheese or Neufchâtel (1 1/2 packages)
  • 1 large egg
  • 1 Tbsp flour
  • 1/2 cup sugar
  • 1/2 tsp vanilla

Method

  1. Oven per box; prepare the brownie batter.
  2. Beat cream cheese smooth; add egg (1 large), flour (1 Tbsp), sugar and vanilla (1/2 tsp).
  3. Pour all but ~1/2 cup brownie batter into a prepared 9×13. Spoon the cheesecake over, then the reserved batter; swirl with a knife.

Ingredients

  • 1 stick (1/2 cup) melted unsalted butter, plus more
  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 2 tsp baking powder
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 2 cups sugar, divided
  • 1 cup whole milk
  • 12 oz fresh blackberries
  • 1 cup boiling water

Sassy sauce

  • 1 (14-oz) can sweetened condensed milk
  • 1 cup heavy cream
  • Splash rum

Method

  1. Oven 350°F; butter a 10-inch cast-iron skillet.
  2. Whisk flour (1 1/2 cups), baking powder, salt (1 tsp), 1 cup sugar, the melted butter (1 stick total) and milk (1 cup); pour into the skillet.
  3. Top with blackberries (12 oz), sprinkle the remaining cup of sugar (2 cups total) over, then pour the boiling water (1 cup) over everything.
  4. Bake until bubbling with a golden top, 35–40 minutes.
  5. Sauce: warm condensed milk (1 (14-oz) can) and cream, whisking; spike with rum. Pour over to serve.

Ingredients

  • 2 Tbsp brown sugar
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp + 1 cup unbleached flour, divided
  • 1/2 tsp cinnamon, divided
  • 1/4 tsp ground ginger
  • 10–12 Italian prune plums, halved and pitted
  • 1/4 tsp vanilla bean powder or 1/2 tsp vanilla extract (optional)
  • 3/4 cup granulated sugar
  • 1 tsp baking powder
  • 1/4 tsp salt
  • 1 egg, well beaten
  • 1/2 cup unsalted butter, melted

Method

  1. Oven 375°F. Mix brown sugar (2 Tbsp), 1 1/2 Tbsp flour, 1/4 tsp cinnamon and the ginger (1/4 tsp); toss with the plums (10–12). Arrange cut-side up in a 9-inch pie plate on a baking sheet (leaks happen).
  2. Combine granulated sugar (3/4 cup), baking powder, the 1 cup flour, remaining 1/4 tsp cinnamon, vanilla powder (1 tsp) and salt. Stir in the egg (1) (and extract (1/4 tsp)); squeeze with your hands into soft clumps and scatter over the plums.
  3. Pour the melted butter (1/2 cup) evenly over; bake 30–35 minutes until browned and tender. Warm or room temp, with ice cream, crème fraîche, mascarpone, yogurt or whipped cream.

Ingredients

  • 4 lb apples, preferably Granny Smith, peeled and sliced
  • 1 Tbsp vanilla
  • 3/4 cup dark brown sugar
  • 1 tsp cinnamon + nutmeg + dash of salt

Topping

  • 1 1/4 cups old-fashioned oats
  • 3/4 cup all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 cup brown sugar
  • 1 tsp cinnamon
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 cup butter, cubed (not margarine)

Method

  1. Oven 375°F, rack center; grease a 9×13 (or two 9-inch pie plates).
  2. Toss apples (4 lb) with vanilla (1 Tbsp), brown sugar (3/4 cup), cinnamon, nutmeg and salt (1/2 tsp); into the pan.

  4. Bake ~45 minutes until bubbly and golden. Rest 15 minutes; serve warm with vanilla ice cream or whipped cream.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup raisins
  • 1/2 cup sweet red wine (Manischewitz or similar)
  • 6 crisp red apples (Gala, Fuji), peeled, finely chopped
  • 1 1/2 cups almonds, finely chopped
  • 2 tsp ground cinnamon
  • 1 lemon, zested and juiced
  • Honey, to taste

Method

  1. Soak the raisins (1/2 cup) in the wine (1/2 cup) while you prep.
  2. Mix apples and almonds (1 1/2 cups); stir in cinnamon (2 tsp), lemon zest, half the juice, and the raisins with their wine.
  3. Balance with honey and more lemon (1); refrigerate at least 30 minutes so the apples drink the wine.

Ingredients

  • Dough: 2 1/2 cups flour, 1 cup (2 sticks) cold butter, 1/2 cup sugar, 2 egg yolks, 2 Tbsp sour cream, pinch salt
  • Filling: 3 lb tart apples, peeled and coarsely grated or thinly sliced
  • 1/3 cup sugar; 1 tsp cinnamon
  • 1 Tbsp lemon juice; 2 Tbsp breadcrumbs
  • Powdered sugar, to finish

Method

  1. Work the dough ingredients into a soft shortbread-like dough; chill 30 minutes in two portions, one slightly larger.
  2. Cook the apples with sugar, cinnamon and lemon until jammy but with texture, ~10 minutes; cool.
  3. Press the larger dough into a 9-inch pan and up the sides; dust with breadcrumbs, spread the apples, and coarsely grate or lattice the second dough over.
  4. Bake at 375°F until deep golden, 50–60 minutes. Cool fully; snow with powdered sugar.

Ingredients

  • 1 cup shelled pistachios, ground fine, plus chopped for topping
  • 2 3/4 cups flour
  • 2 1/2 tsp baking powder; 1 tsp kosher salt; 1/2 tsp ground cardamom
  • 1 cup (2 sticks) butter, room temp
  • 1 3/4 cups sugar
  • 4 eggs
  • 1 cup plain yogurt
  • 1 tsp vanilla or almond extract
  • Glaze: 1 cup powdered sugar + 2 Tbsp lemon juice

Method

  1. Whisk ground pistachios (1 cup), flour, baking powder, salt and cardamom.
  2. Cream butter (1 cup) and sugar until fluffy; eggs (4) one at a time, then extract (1 tsp).
  3. Alternate the dry mixture and yogurt (1 cup) into the batter; pour into a buttered, floured bundt.
  4. Bake at 350°F until a skewer comes out clean, 55–65 minutes. Cool 15 minutes, turn out, cool fully.
  5. Glaze and shower with chopped pistachios; let set before slicing.

Ingredients

  • Base: 1 3/4 cups flour, 2 tsp instant yeast, 3 Tbsp sugar, 1/2 tsp salt, 6 Tbsp warm milk, 6 Tbsp soft butter, 1 egg
  • Topping: 12 Tbsp soft butter, 1 1/2 cups sugar, 1/4 cup light corn syrup or honey, 2 Tbsp milk, 1 egg, 1 tsp vanilla, 1 1/4 cups flour, 1/2 tsp salt
  • Powdered sugar

Method

Ingredients

  • Shell: 1 1/4 cups flour, 1/4 cup cocoa, 1/4 cup powdered sugar, 1/2 cup cold butter, 1 yolk, splash cold water
  • Caramel: 1 1/2 cups sugar, 6 Tbsp butter, 3/4 cup heavy cream, 1/2 tsp flaky salt
  • Ganache: 4 oz dark chocolate, 1/2 cup heavy cream
  • Sea salt, for the top

Method

  1. Blitz the shell ingredients to a dough; press into a 9-inch tart pan, chill, then blind-bake at 350°F until set, ~20 minutes. Cool.
  2. Caramel: melt the sugar to deep amber; whisk in butter, then cream (carefully), then salt. Pour into the shell; chill until set, ~1 hour.
  3. Ganache: pour hot cream over the chocolate, rest, stir smooth; spread over the caramel.
  4. Chill until sliceable; flaky salt just before serving. Thin slices — it's serious.

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup (1 stick) butter, room temp
  • 3/4 cup brown sugar + 1/4 cup granulated
  • 2 Tbsp white miso
  • 1 egg + 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1 1/2 cups flour

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

  • Sponge: 4 eggs, 2/3 cup sugar, 1 cup flour, 1 tsp baking powder, 2 Tbsp melted butter
  • 1/2 cup raspberry jam
  • Pastry cream: 2 cups milk, 5 yolks, 1/2 cup sugar, 1/4 cup cornstarch, 1 tsp vanilla, 2 Tbsp butter
  • 2 cups heavy cream, whipped to firm peaks
  • 14 oz marzipan, tinted green + powdered sugar for rolling
  • A marzipan rose or a raspberry, for the top

Method

  1. Sponge: whip eggs and sugar to the ribbon stage, ~5 minutes; fold in flour and baking powder, then butter. Bake in a 9-inch pan at 350°F ~25 minutes; cool and split into 3 layers.
  2. Pastry cream (2 cups): heat the milk; temper into whisked yolks, sugar and cornstarch; cook until thick, finish with vanilla and butter; chill with wrap pressed on top.
  3. Stack: sponge, jam, a third of the pastry cream; sponge, remaining pastry cream folded with a third of the whipped cream; final sponge.
  4. Dome the remaining whipped cream over the top, higher in the middle; chill 1 hour.
  5. Roll the green marzipan on powdered sugar into a wide round and drape it over the dome, smoothing down; trim. Dust with powdered sugar, crown with the rose.
  6. A project, but every component can be made a day ahead. Fitting for the Stockholm era.

Adapted in our own words. Original on NYT Cooking →

Ingredients

Vanilla syrup

  • 1/2 cup granulated sugar
  • 1 vanilla bean, split and scraped (or 1 1/2 tsp vanilla)

Frangipane

  • 2/3 cup blanched almonds, toasted
  • 1/2 cup granulated sugar
  • 6 Tbsp unsalted butter, soft
  • 1 large egg
  • 2 Tbsp all-purpose flour
  • 1 Tbsp dark rum (or amaretto, or skip)
  • 3/4 tsp salt; 1/2 tsp almond extract

Assembly

  • 1 loaf brioche (~3/4 lb), 8 thick slices, day-old
  • 1/2 cup apricot jam
  • 1/2 cup sliced almonds
  • Confectioners' sugar

Method

  1. Syrup: simmer the sugar (1/2 cup), 1/2 cup water and the vanilla bean (1) until dissolved; cool. (Keeps a week.)
  2. Frangipane: grind the toasted almonds (2/3 cup) with sugar in a food processor, then work in butter (6 Tbsp), egg, flour (2 Tbsp), rum, salt and extract until creamy. (Keeps a day.)
  3. Line up the brioche (1 loaf) on a parchment sheet; brush generously with syrup, spread a thin coat of apricot jam (1/2 cup), then a thick layer of frangipane edge to edge.
  4. Scatter sliced almonds (1/2 cup); bake at 350°F until the frangipane is set and caramelized at the edges, 15–18 minutes.
  5. Dust with confectioners' sugar. French toast × almond croissant, no griddle duty.

Ingredients

Topping

  • 1 1/2 cups (190 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 cup (50 g) rolled oats
  • 1/3 cup (75 g) light or dark brown sugar
  • 1/3 cup (65 g) granulated sugar
  • 1/4 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 tsp ground cinnamon
  • 1/2 tsp ground cardamom, ginger or allspice (or lemon zest)
  • 1/2 cup (115 g) unsalted butter, melted and cooled

Filling

  • 2–5 Tbsp light brown sugar, depending on the fruit's sweetness
  • 2 Tbsp cornstarch
  • 8 cups mixed berries (fresh or frozen), or cubed stone fruit, or a combination
  • Ice cream or whipped cream, to serve

Method

  1. Heat oven to 350°F. Whisk the dry topping ingredients; stir in the butter (1/2 cup), then squish with your hands into coarse crumbs — some 1/2-inch, some smaller.
  2. The signature move: spread the crumbs on an ungreased rimmed sheet and bake them alone until they're set when poked and smell toasty, ~15 minutes (they barely change color). Cool. (Keeps airtight 2 days.)
  3. Whisk sugar (1/3 cup) and cornstarch, then gently toss with the fruit — taste the fruit first; sweet berries (8 cups) need the low end of the sugar (2–5 Tbsp), tart plums the high end. Mound in an ungreased 2-quart gratin dish or 10-inch cake pan.
  4. Spoon the pre-baked crumbs over, set the dish on a rimmed sheet (juice insurance), and bake until the filling bubbles energetically at the edges, 55–65 minutes.
  5. Because the crumbs went in already dried out, they stay crisp and cookie-like instead of soaking. Best a little warm, with ice cream; holds at room temp 8 hours and rewarms at 350°F.

Ingredients

Berries

  • 4 1/2 cups fresh blueberries
  • 1/3 cup sugar
  • 1 Tbsp all-purpose flour

Dough

  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp sugar
  • 2 1/4 tsp baking powder
  • 6 Tbsp cold unsalted butter, 1/2-inch pieces
  • 3/4 cup heavy cream, plus more for serving

Method

  1. Heat oven to 375°F. Toss the berries with their sugar (1/3 cup) and flour (1 Tbsp); tip into a 1 1/2-quart gratin dish.
  2. Dough: mix flour, salt (1/2 tsp), sugar and baking powder (2 1/4 tsp); cut in the cold butter (6 Tbsp) to coarse meal; add cream (3/4 cup total) and mix just until moistened.
  3. Pat the dough into 2–2 1/2-inch patties, 1/2-inch thick, and arrange over the berries — gaps are the point; the juice bubbles up between them.
  4. Bake until the topping browns and the juices bubble thickly, 35–40 minutes. Cool slightly; serve warm with cream to pour over.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups (280 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1 tsp baking powder; 1/2 tsp baking soda
  • 1 1/4 cups (250 g) granulated sugar
  • 3 Tbsp fresh lemon zest
  • 3/4 cup (168 g) lemon olive oil (or good olive oil + extra zest)
  • 1 cup (240 g) whole-milk ricotta, room temp
  • 2 large eggs, room temp
  • 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice
  • Lemon glaze + fresh zest, to top

Method

  1. Heat oven to 350°F; line and grease a 9-inch springform.
  2. Whisk the dry ingredients. In a big bowl, rub sugar (1 1/4 cups) and zest together 1 minute until fragrant; whisk in oil, ricotta (1 cup), eggs and lemon juice (1/4 cup) until smooth.
  3. Fold in the flour (2 cups) mixture just until no streaks remain; pour into the pan and tap out air bubbles.
  4. Bake 44–45 minutes, until springy, pulling from the sides, and a toothpick shows a few crumbs.
  5. Cool 30 minutes, unclip, cool fully; glaze (the board's lemon icing works) and finish with fresh zest (3 Tbsp).
  6. Richer, plusher cousin of the Greek lemon olive oil cake two cards over.

Ingredients

All-butter dough

  • 2 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1 generous Tbsp sugar
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 cup (2 sticks) butter, 1/2-inch cubes, frozen 15 minutes
  • 1/2 cup ice water + 1 tsp white vinegar

Filling

  • 2 1/2 lb firm baking apples (mix Fuji, Honeycrisp, Granny Smith), peeled, sliced
  • 1/2 cup + 1 Tbsp packed dark brown sugar
  • 3 Tbsp all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 tsp cinnamon; 1/4 tsp allspice
  • 1/2 tsp kosher salt
  • 1 Tbsp apple cider vinegar

Finish

  • 1 egg beaten with 1 Tbsp cream, for brushing
  • Granulated sugar, for sprinkling

Method

  1. Dough: whisk flour (2 1/4 cups), sugar and salt (1 tsp). Work the frozen butter (1 cup) in until you have pea-size bits and inch-long shingles — the shingles are the flake.
  2. Stir the vinegar into the ice water; drizzle in gradually, stirring, stopping when a pinched handful holds together but still looks shaggy (you won't need it all). Divide, flatten into two discs, wrap, chill 2+ hours.
  3. Filling: toss the apple slices with brown sugar (1 generous Tbsp), flour, spices and salt; add the cider vinegar (1 Tbsp) last.
  4. Heat oven to 425°F with a sheet pan on the bottom rack. Roll one disc to a 12-inch round, line a 9-inch plate, trim to a 1-inch overhang; freeze 10 minutes.
  5. Fill, top with the second round, tuck the top edge under the bottom, crimp, and cut 4–6 vents. Egg-wash and sugar the top.
  6. Bake 15 minutes at 425°F, then 45–60 at 375°F until the vents bubble thickly and the crust is deep gold (tent edges if needed).
  7. Cool at least 3 hours before slicing — that's what sets the filling.

Ingredients

  • 2 sticks (225 g) unsalted butter, room temp
  • 1 1/2 cups (300 g) granulated sugar
  • 1 large egg
  • 1 1/3 cups (225 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1/4 cup (45 g) corn flour
  • 2/3 cup (65 g) freeze-dried corn powder (blitz freeze-dried corn)
  • 3/4 tsp baking powder; 1/4 tsp baking soda
  • 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt

Method

  1. Cream butter (2 sticks) and sugar (1 1/2 cups) 2–3 minutes. Add the egg (1 large) and beat a full 7–8 minutes — Tosi's signature step; it goes pale and fluffy.
  2. On low, add both flours, corn powder (2/3 cup), leaveners and salt (1 1/2 tsp); mix under a minute, just to combine.
  3. Scoop big 2 3/4-oz domes (a 1/3-cup measure), flatten the tops, and chill at least 1 hour (up to a week). Never bake at room temp — they'll spread flat.
  4. Bake 4 inches apart at 350°F for ~18 minutes: cracked, spread, faintly brown at the rim, still bright yellow in the middle.
  5. Cool completely on the pans. Tastes like the platonic cornflake.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups (300 g) all-purpose flour
  • 1 Tbsp baking powder
  • 1 tsp kosher salt; 1/2 tsp baking soda
  • 3/4 cup buttermilk or thin yogurt
  • 3/4 cup (180 g) sugar, plus more for the tops
  • 1/4 cup lightly packed light brown sugar
  • 2 large eggs
  • 1/2 cup (1 stick) melted butter or olive oil
  • Optional: 1 Tbsp lemon zest, 1/4 tsp cinnamon or 1/2 tsp vanilla
  • 2 1/2 cups blueberries, fresh or frozen (don't thaw)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 425°F; line or grease 12 muffin cups.
  2. Whisk the dry ingredients in one bowl. In the other, whisk both sugars with the eggs (2 large), then the buttermilk and butter.
  3. Fold dry into wet gently — a few floury streaks are fine. Fold in 2 cups of the berries.
  4. Fill the cups high, top with remaining berries and a generous crunch of sugar (1/4 cup).
  5. Bake until domed, deeply golden and cooked through, 17–20 minutes.
  6. Sits opposite the Jordan Marsh muffins on the board: this one's craggy-topped and eggy; that one's the mashed-berry classic. Arguably better the next day.

Ingredients

  • 2 cups water
  • 1 cup/120g cocoa powder
  • ½ cup/120g sugar
  • 8 cups whole milk (or alternative milk)
  • Pinch kosher salt
  • 1¼ cups/200g 70–72% chocolate
  • 2 cups heavy cream (or alternative), for whipping
  • Powdered sugar (optional)

Method

  1. Whisk the water (2 cups) and cocoa (1 cup) in a large pot, bring to a boil while whisking to bloom the cocoa.
  2. Pour in the milk (8 cups), sugar (½ cup) and a pinch of salt, and bring to a simmer; take off the heat.
  3. Whisk in the chopped chocolate (1¼ cups) until smooth.
  4. Whip the cream (2 cups) to soft peaks, with a little powdered sugar if wanted, and spoon over each mug.

Can be made and chilled up to 5 days ahead; rewarm gently and thin with milk, as it thickens on standing. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 6 large egg yolks
  • 1 14-ounce can sweetened condensed milk
  • 1 cup whole milk
  • 3 cups heavy cream, divided
  • Pinch kosher salt
  • ⅛ teaspoon freshly grated or ground nutmeg, plus more
  • ⅛ teaspoon ground cinnamon, plus more
  • Freshly ground black pepper
  • ¾ cup dark rum

Method

  1. Whisk the yolks (6) together with the condensed milk (1 can) until combined
  2. Stream in the milk (1 cup) and 1 cup of the cream, then season with nutmeg (⅛ tsp), cinnamon (⅛ tsp), black pepper and salt (pinch), tasting and adjusting the spices
  3. Stir in the rum (¾ cup) and chill overnight so it thickens
  4. Just before serving, whip the remaining cream (2 cups) to soft peaks, sweetening with powdered sugar if you like, and dollop over each glass

Can be made and chilled up to 3 days ahead; serve cold. Condensed milk replaces sugar, in the style of Mexican rompope. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Dry

  • 1½ cups (215g) all-purpose flour
  • 2 teaspoons baking powder
  • 1 teaspoon kosher salt
  • ¾ teaspoon ground turmeric

Wet and flavorings

  • 2 lemons
  • 1 cup (200g) granulated sugar, plus 2 tablespoons for topping
  • ¾ cup (180ml) sour cream or full-fat Greek yogurt
  • 2 large eggs
  • ½ cup (115g) unsalted butter, melted

Optional, for serving

  • Whipped cream
  • Additional sour cream

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and line a greased 9 by 4-inch loaf pan with parchment, leaving a sling on the long sides
  2. Whisk the flour (1½ cups), baking powder (2 tsp), salt (1 tsp) and turmeric (¾ tsp) together
  3. Zest one lemon and juice it (about 2 tbsp each); slice the other lemon into thin rounds and pull out the seeds
  4. Rub the zest into the sugar (1 cup) until fragrant and yellow, whisk in the sour cream (¾ cup), eggs (2) and lemon juice, then fold this into the dry mix, followed by the melted butter (½ cup)
  5. Scrape into the pan, lay the lemon rounds on top, scatter with extra sugar (2 tbsp), and bake 50–60 minutes until golden and a tester comes out clean; cool before slicing

Can be baked up to 5 days ahead, wrapped tightly at room temperature. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 5 ounces/140g bittersweet chocolate (65%–70% cacao), finely chopped
  • 1½ cups/360g heavy cream
  • 1½ cups/360g whole milk
  • ¼ cup/28g unsweetened cocoa powder
  • ¾ cup/165g granulated sugar
  • 3 large egg yolks
  • ¼ cup/30g cornstarch
  • 1 teaspoon/4g kosher salt
  • 1 teaspoon/5g vanilla extract
  • Lightly sweetened whipped cream, for serving
  • Flaky sea salt, for serving
  • Cookies (chocolate wafers or gingersnaps), for crumbling or dipping

Method

  1. Put the chopped chocolate (5 oz) in a large heatproof bowl
  2. Warm the cream (1½ cups), milk (1½ cups), cocoa (¼ cup) and half the sugar (6 tbsp) in a pot, whisking, until barely simmering
  3. Whisk the yolks (3), cornstarch (¼ cup), salt (1 tsp), vanilla (1 tsp) and the other half of the sugar (6 tbsp) until smooth, temper with some of the hot liquid, then pour it all back into the pot and cook, whisking, until thick and custardy
  4. Pour over the chocolate, let it sit a moment, and whisk until glossy
  5. Pour into an 8x8-inch dish, press plastic onto the surface, chill at least 3 hours, then whisk to loosen and serve with whipped cream, flaky salt and cookies

Keeps covered in the fridge up to 5 days (3 hours minimum chill). Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Crust

  • 8 ounces spiced cookies (gingersnaps, graham crackers, or Biscoff)
  • 6 tablespoons (3 ounces/85g) unsalted butter, melted
  • 1 tablespoon light brown sugar (optional)
  • ½–1 teaspoon kosher salt

Filling

  • 1 15-ounce can pumpkin puree
  • ⅓ cup granulated sugar
  • ½ cup light brown sugar
  • 1¼ teaspoons kosher salt
  • 1 teaspoon ground cinnamon
  • 1 tablespoon freshly grated ginger or ½ teaspoon ground ginger
  • ¼ teaspoon ground black pepper
  • ⅛ teaspoon ground nutmeg
  • 3 large eggs
  • ¾ cup heavy cream
  • Lightly sweetened whipped cream, for serving

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F, grind the cookies (8 oz) into coarse crumbs, and mix with the melted butter (6 tbsp), brown sugar (1 tbsp) and salt until it feels like damp sand
  2. Press into a greased 9-inch pie plate up the sides and bake 12–15 minutes until set and lightly browned
  3. Drop the oven to 325°F and whisk the pumpkin (1 can) with both sugars (⅓ cup and ½ cup), salt (1¼ tsp), cinnamon (1 tsp), ginger (1 tbsp fresh), pepper (¼ tsp) and nutmeg (⅛ tsp)
  4. Beat in the eggs (3), then the cream (¾ cup), pour into the crust, and bake 35–45 minutes until set and matte
  5. Cool completely at room temperature, chill, and serve with whipped cream

Can be baked the day before and kept chilled in the fridge. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup/120g heavy cream, plus more for brushing
  • ½ cup/110g sour cream
  • 2 cups/290g all-purpose flour, plus more for the work surface
  • ⅓ cup/70g sugar, plus more for sprinkling
  • 1 tablespoon/12g baking powder
  • 1¼ teaspoons/5g kosher salt
  • 1 stick/4 ounces/115g cold unsalted butter, cut into small pieces
  • 2½ cups/300g raspberries or blueberries, chopped strawberries, or halved blackberries
  • Flaky sea salt, to finish

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 425°F and line a sheet pan with parchment; stir the heavy cream (½ cup) and sour cream (½ cup) together
  2. Whisk the flour (2 cups), sugar (⅓ cup), baking powder (1 tbsp) and salt (1¼ tsp), then smash the cold butter (1 stick) into it with your fingers until flaky
  3. Toss in the berries (2½ cups), pour in the cream mixture, and knead just until a shaggy dough forms
  4. Pat out to a roughly 6 x 9-inch rectangle, cut into 4 pieces and then each into 2 triangles, and set on the sheet; chill 20–30 minutes if you want extra lift
  5. Brush with cream, sprinkle with sugar and flaky salt, and bake 25–30 minutes until golden

Cut dough can be refrigerated 6–8 hours before baking; baked scones are best within a few hours and can be reheated. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Finely grated zest of 3 lemons
  • ¾ cup/165g plus ¼ cup/55g granulated sugar
  • Flaky sea salt
  • 2½ sticks/10 ounces/283g unsalted butter, cold, cut into 1-inch pieces
  • ¼ cup/30g powdered sugar
  • ½ preserved lemon, seeds removed, finely chopped (optional)
  • 1 teaspoon/4g kosher salt
  • 1 teaspoon/5g vanilla extract
  • 2¼ cups/325g all-purpose flour

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and line a 9x13 dish (or two 9-inch cake pans) with parchment
  2. Rub one-third of the zest into the ¼ cup sugar with a pinch of flaky salt until yellow and fragrant; set aside as the topping
  3. Beat the butter (2½ sticks) with the powdered sugar (¼ cup), the ¾ cup sugar, remaining zest, preserved lemon (½), kosher salt (1 tsp) and vanilla (1 tsp) until fluffy, then fold in the flour (2¼ cups)
  4. Press the dough into the pan, prick with a fork, and press the lemon sugar over the top
  5. Bake until deeply browned all over, 30–35 minutes (20–25 in round pans), cool a bit, cut into bars or wedges, and cool fully

Dough keeps 5 days refrigerated or 1 month frozen; baked cookies keep up to 5 days at room temperature. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 6 tablespoons/3 ounces/85g unsalted butter
  • 1 vanilla bean, split lengthwise, or 1 teaspoon vanilla extract
  • 3 tablespoons/36g dark rum or bourbon
  • 1½ cups/360g heavy cream
  • 1½ cups/360g whole milk
  • Pinch of kosher salt
  • 5 large egg yolks
  • ¼ cup/38g cornstarch
  • ¼ cup/55g granulated sugar
  • ½ cup/105g light brown sugar
  • Whipped cream, crème fraîche, or mascarpone, for serving

Method

  1. Brown the butter (6 tbsp) in a skillet, add the vanilla seeds (1 bean), then take it off the heat and add the rum (3 tbsp)
  2. Bring the cream (1½ cups), milk (1½ cups) and salt (pinch) to a simmer in a pot and turn off the heat
  3. Whisk the yolks (5), cornstarch (¼ cup) and white sugar (¼ cup) until pale, slowly stream in the hot dairy, return to the pot, and cook, whisking, about 15–18 minutes until thick and bubbling
  4. Whisk in the rum butter and brown sugar (½ cup) until smooth
  5. Portion out or pour into one bowl, cover, and chill at least 2 hours; top with whipped cream, crème fraîche or mascarpone

Keeps covered in the fridge up to 5 days; good with gingersnaps and amaro. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 cinnamon stick or ¼ teaspoon ground cinnamon
  • ⅓ cup/70g long-grain rice (jasmine, basmati, or Jasmati)
  • 3 cups/720g whole milk (or unsweetened oat or almond milk)
  • ½ cup/110g sugar
  • 1 vanilla bean, split lengthwise, or ½ teaspoon vanilla extract (optional)
  • Pinch of kosher salt
  • 2 egg yolks (optional)

Method

  1. Toast the rice (⅓ cup) and cinnamon stick (1) in a dry pot over medium-high heat, stirring, until nutty-smelling and barely golden, about 4–5 minutes
  2. Pour in the milk (3 cups), water (2 cups), sugar (½ cup), salt (pinch) and vanilla, bring to a simmer, then lower to medium-low for 20–25 minutes until the rice is tender
  3. Turn the heat to low and stir constantly 15–20 minutes more so the starch releases and it turns pudding-thick
  4. Off the heat, whisk in the ground cinnamon (¼ tsp, if using instead of stick) and yolks (2) if you like, and serve warm or chilled with plastic pressed onto the surface

Can be made 5 days ahead, covered and refrigerated. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Cooking spray
  • 2 cups/290g all-purpose flour
  • 2 teaspoons/8g baking powder
  • ¾ teaspoon/3g kosher salt
  • 1 stick/4 ounces/115g unsalted butter, room temperature
  • ⅓ cup/70g granulated sugar
  • ⅓ cup/75g light brown sugar
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla extract
  • 2 large eggs
  • ¾ cup/180g buttermilk
  • 10 ounces/295g strawberries, hulled and sliced ¼ inch thick
  • 3 tablespoons demerara sugar

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and line the bottom of a sprayed 9-inch cake pan with parchment; whisk the flour (2 cups), baking powder (2 tsp) and salt (¾ tsp)
  2. Beat the butter (1 stick) with both sugars (⅓ cup each) and vanilla (1 tsp) about 5 minutes until pale and fluffy, then beat in the eggs (2) one by one
  3. On low speed, add the flour mixture and buttermilk (¾ cup) in alternating additions, starting and finishing with flour
  4. Fold half the strawberries (about 5 oz) into the batter, scrape into the pan, and top with the rest of the berries and the demerara (3 tbsp)
  5. Bake 45–50 minutes until deep golden and pulling from the sides, then cool completely

Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 9-inch disc of chocolate cake
  • 2 quarts mint chip ice cream (or preferred flavor)
  • 2 cups/460g heavy cream
  • ¼ cup/30g powdered sugar
  • Pinch of salt

Method

  1. Line a 9-inch springform with plastic wrap, leaving overhang, and set the cake disc (1) in the bottom
  2. Let the ice cream (2 quarts) soften about 10 minutes, spread it over the cake, cover with plastic, press flat, and freeze while you make the cream
  3. Whip the cream (2 cups) to medium peaks, add the powdered sugar (¼ cup) and salt (pinch), and whip to stiff peaks
  4. Spread about 1 cup of the whipped cream over the frozen layer and freeze at least 2 more hours, keeping the rest chilled
  5. Unmold, peel away the plastic, frost the sides and top with the remaining cream, and serve or re-freeze

Can be assembled 1 week ahead, tightly wrapped and frozen. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Cooking spray, for the pan
  • 2½ cups/360g all-purpose flour
  • 1 cup/115g unsweetened cocoa powder
  • 2 teaspoons/8g baking powder
  • 1 teaspoon/6g baking soda
  • 2 teaspoons/8g kosher salt
  • 1¼ cups/275g granulated sugar
  • 1 cup/200g light brown sugar
  • 1½ teaspoons/7g vanilla extract
  • 3 large eggs
  • 1½ cups/330g buttermilk
  • ¾ cup/155g vegetable oil
  • 1 cup/220g sour cream

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and line a sprayed half-sheet pan (or three 9-inch pans) with parchment
  2. Whisk the flour (2½ cups), cocoa (1 cup), baking powder (2 tsp), baking soda (1 tsp) and salt (2 tsp) in a bowl
  3. In a bigger bowl whisk both sugars (1¼ cups and 1 cup), vanilla (1½ tsp), eggs (3), buttermilk (1½ cups), oil (¾ cup) and sour cream (1 cup) until even
  4. Slowly whisk the dry mix into the wet, pour into the pan(s), and bake until the cake springs back, about 30–35 minutes (20–25 for layers)
  5. Cool fully before frosting or building an ice cream cake

Wrapped tightly: 3 days at room temperature, 5 in the fridge, or 1 month frozen. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Crust

  • 1⅓ cups/200g all-purpose flour
  • ⅔ cup/80g powdered sugar
  • 1½ teaspoons/6g kosher salt
  • 1½ sticks/6 ounces/170g unsalted butter, melted and cooled slightly

Assembly

  • Vanilla Pastry Cream, cooled
  • ½ pound/225g assorted fresh red fruits (raspberries, strawberries, currants, boysenberries), halved or sliced, or roasted fruits

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and stir the flour (1⅓ cups), powdered sugar (⅔ cup) and salt (1½ tsp) together
  2. Work in the melted butter (1½ sticks) by hand until it forms a pliable, play-dough-like dough, without kneading
  3. Press it into a 9-inch tart pan with a removable bottom, prick all over, and bake 18–20 minutes until golden like shortbread; cool completely
  4. Whisk the cooled pastry cream smooth and spread it into the shell
  5. Top with the fruit (½ lb) and chill 1 hour before serving

Baked crust keeps 2 days at room temperature; assembled tart keeps 2 days refrigerated but gets soggy after. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 4 cups/945g whole milk
  • 1 cup/220g sugar
  • 1 vanilla bean, split lengthwise, or 1½ teaspoons/7g vanilla extract
  • 8 large egg yolks
  • ½ cup/65g cornstarch
  • 1 teaspoon/4g kosher salt
  • 4 tablespoons/2 ounces/60g unsalted butter

Method

  1. Heat the milk (4 cups) with half the sugar (½ cup) and the scraped vanilla bean and pod (if using) until just simmering, then remove from the heat
  2. Whisk the yolks (8) with the remaining sugar (½ cup), cornstarch (½ cup), salt (1 tsp) and extract (if using instead of bean)
  3. Whisk in about a cup of the hot milk to temper, then the rest, and return it all to the pot
  4. Cook over medium heat, whisking nonstop 8–10 minutes, until it goes from thin and foamy to thick with big slow bubbles, then whisk in the butter (4 tbsp)
  5. Spread into a shallow dish with plastic pressed onto the surface, chill, and whisk smooth before serving

Keeps tightly covered in the fridge up to 5 days. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 8 ounces Philadelphia Cream Cheese
  • ½ cup full-fat sour cream, Greek yogurt, or labne
  • 3 tablespoons sugar
  • Pinch kosher salt
  • 1 cup raspberries, blackberries, or strawberries
  • 1–2 tablespoons fresh lemon juice
  • A few crumbled cookies (Biscoff, graham, Nilla, or shortbread)
  • Flaky salt (optional)

Method

  1. Blend the cream cheese (8 oz), sour cream (½ cup), 2 tablespoons of the sugar and the salt (pinch) in a processor or mixer
  2. Scrape the bowl a couple of times and keep going until perfectly smooth
  3. Mash the berries (1 cup) with lemon juice (1–2 tbsp) and the last tablespoon of sugar until juicy
  4. Layer the cream, berries and crumbled cookies in a glass, ending with cookies and a little flaky salt if you like

No-bake, five-ingredient parfait; the crumbled cookies give the crunch. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup/1 stick/112g unsalted butter
  • 1½ cups/225g all-purpose flour
  • 2 teaspoons baking powder
  • 1 teaspoon kosher salt
  • 3 large eggs
  • 2 cups/1 pound/453g cottage cheese or ricotta
  • ¾ cup/175g sugar
  • ⅓ cup honey or light brown sugar
  • 1½–2 pounds/680–900g apricots, plums or peaches, pitted and sliced about ½-inch thick, divided

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 400°F, line a 9-inch cake pan with parchment, and melt the butter (1 stick), browning it if you want extra flavor
  2. Whisk the flour (1½ cups), baking powder (2 tsp) and salt (1 tsp) in a large bowl
  3. In another bowl whisk the eggs (3), cottage cheese (2 cups), sugar (¾ cup) and honey (⅓ cup) smooth, then stir into the dry mix along with the butter
  4. Fold in about three-quarters of the fruit (1½–2 lb total), pour into the pan, top with the rest of the fruit, and sprinkle with a little sugar
  5. Bake 40–45 minutes until deep brown, shiny and set in the middle, then cool fully

Can be made 4 days ahead, wrapped tightly at room temperature; good with whipped cream and honey. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Pudding

  • 8 large egg yolks
  • ⅓ cup cornstarch
  • ¾ cup granulated sugar, divided
  • 2 cups heavy cream
  • 2 (14-ounce) cans full-fat coconut milk
  • Pinch of kosher salt
  • Pinch of ground turmeric (optional)
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla extract (optional)
  • ¼ cup light brown sugar
  • 4 tablespoons (½ stick) unsalted butter, softened, cut into 1-inch pieces

Assembly

  • 2 cups heavy cream
  • ½ cup confectioners' sugar
  • Pinch of kosher salt
  • 1 cup full-fat Greek yogurt
  • 6 firm bananas, peeled and thinly sliced
  • 2 tablespoons fresh lime juice
  • 1 (11-ounce) box Nilla wafers or graham crackers
  • Maraschino cherries (optional)

Method

  1. Beat the yolks (8) with the cornstarch (⅓ cup) and half the sugar (6 tablespoons) until pale and thick.
  2. Bring the cream (2 cups), coconut milk (2 cans), rest of the sugar (6 tablespoons), salt, turmeric and vanilla to a simmer, then stream half into the yolks while whisking and pour everything back into the pot. Keep whisking over heat until thick and bubbling, 3–5 minutes.
  3. Off the heat, stir in the brown sugar (¼ cup) and butter (4 tablespoons) until smooth. Spread in a wide dish, press plastic onto the surface and chill at least 1 hour.
  4. Whip the cream (2 cups) with confectioners' sugar (½ cup) and salt to medium-stiff peaks, then fold in the yogurt (1 cup). Toss the sliced bananas (6) with lime juice (2 tablespoons).
  5. Layer pudding, wafers (1 box), pudding, bananas, then whipped cream in a serving dish; repeat and finish with cream. Chill at least 2 hours and top with cherries if you like.

Pudding keeps refrigerated up to 3 days; assembled dessert can be made 1 day ahead before the wafers get too soft. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Shortcakes

  • 1¼ cups (181g) all-purpose flour
  • ½ cup (78g) coarse yellow cornmeal
  • 2 tablespoons (27.5g) light brown sugar
  • 2 tablespoons (25g) granulated sugar
  • 1 tablespoon (12g) baking powder
  • ¾ teaspoon (2g) kosher salt
  • ½ cup (113g) unsalted butter, cut into 1-inch pieces, room temperature
  • ½ cup (113.5g) heavy cream

Filling

  • 5 cups (750g) strawberries, hulled and halved
  • ½ cup (100g) granulated sugar
  • 2 tablespoons (14g) cornstarch
  • 1 tablespoon (14.5g) fresh lime or lemon juice
  • 2 tablespoons (14g) heavy cream, for brushing
  • 2 tablespoons (27.5g) light brown sugar, for sprinkling

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F. Stir together the flour (1¼ cups), cornmeal (½ cup), both sugars (2 tablespoons each), baking powder (1 tablespoon) and salt (¾ teaspoon), then rub in the butter (½ cup) until no big lumps remain.
  2. Add the cream (½ cup), mix until it comes together, and knead on a floured surface about 2 minutes until less sticky.
  3. Pat the dough about 1 inch thick and stamp out 2-inch rounds, gathering and re-patting scraps until it is all used.
  4. Toss the strawberries (5 cups) with sugar (½ cup), cornstarch (2 tablespoons) and citrus juice (1 tablespoon) in a 9-inch skillet or baking dish and set the rounds on top.
  5. Brush the rounds with cream (2 tablespoons), sprinkle with brown sugar (2 tablespoons), and bake 25–30 minutes until golden with juices bubbling at the edges. Rest briefly so the juices settle.

Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 recipe (2 disks) The Only Piecrust
  • All-purpose flour, for rolling
  • 1 large egg, beaten
  • 4 pounds ripe peaches, unpeeled, pitted and sliced ½ inch thick
  • ⅓ cup mild honey
  • ¼ cup granulated sugar
  • 1 tablespoon lime zest
  • ¼ cup fresh lime juice
  • 3 tablespoons cornstarch
  • 1 tablespoon finely grated fresh ginger
  • Pinch of kosher salt
  • ½ cup Demerara sugar

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 375°F. Roll each dough disk (2) into a 14-inch round on a floured surface, stack on a parchment-lined sheet with parchment between them, and chill.
  2. Mix the peaches (4 pounds) with honey (⅓ cup), granulated sugar (¼ cup), lime zest (1 tablespoon), lime juice (¼ cup), cornstarch (3 tablespoons), ginger (1 tablespoon) and salt.
  3. Fit one round into a 9-inch pie plate, pile in the filling, brush the rim with beaten egg (1, loosened with 1 teaspoon water), then cap with the second round and crimp to seal.
  4. Cut three slits in the top, brush with egg, and cover with Demerara sugar (½ cup).
  5. Bake on the parchment-lined sheet for an hour or more, rotate, then bake another 30–45 minutes until deep golden, tenting the edges if they darken too fast. Cool about 4 hours before slicing.

Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • ½ cup unsalted butter
  • ½ vanilla bean, split and seeded (or 1 teaspoon vanilla extract)
  • Non-stick spray
  • ⅔ cup light brown sugar, divided
  • 2 large unpeeled apples, cored and sliced
  • 1¼ cups all-purpose flour
  • ½ teaspoon baking powder
  • ½ teaspoon baking soda
  • ½ teaspoon ground cinnamon
  • ½ teaspoon kosher salt
  • 2 large eggs
  • ¾ cup buttermilk

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F. Brown the butter (½ cup) over medium-high heat 2–3 minutes until nutty and amber, drop in the vanilla bean and let it cool.
  2. Grease an 8- to 9-inch cake pan, scatter half the brown sugar (⅓ cup) over the bottom, and arrange the apple slices (2 apples) on top.
  3. Whisk the flour (1¼ cups), baking powder, baking soda, cinnamon, salt (½ teaspoon each) and remaining brown sugar (⅓ cup) together.
  4. Beat the eggs (2) with buttermilk (¾ cup), fold into the dry mix until just combined, then whisk in the cooled brown butter.
  5. Pour over the apples and bake 25–30 minutes until golden and springy. Flip onto a plate right away while the sugar is still molten.

Invert immediately out of the pan; lining with parchment makes release easier. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Shortcake topping

  • 1⅓ cups (200g) all-purpose flour
  • ½ cup (55g) quick-cooking oats
  • ¼ cup (50g) granulated sugar, plus more for sprinkling
  • 1 tablespoon baking powder
  • ¾ teaspoon kosher salt
  • ½ cup (115g) unsalted butter, cut into 1-inch pieces, chilled
  • ½ cup (120ml) heavy cream, plus more for brushing
  • Flaky sea salt for sprinkling

Filling

  • 2 pounds (900g) apricots, pitted and halved (or quartered if large)
  • ¼ cup (60ml) honey
  • 1 tablespoon cornstarch
  • 2 tablespoons fresh lime or lemon juice
  • Pinch of kosher salt

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F. Combine the flour (1⅓ cups), oats (½ cup), sugar (¼ cup), baking powder (1 tablespoon) and salt (¾ teaspoon), then work in the cold butter (½ cup) until only small bits remain.
  2. Stir in the cream (½ cup), tip onto a floured counter, knead briefly, pat 1 inch thick and cut 2-inch rounds (8–9), re-patting scraps.
  3. Mix the apricots (2 pounds) with honey (¼ cup), cornstarch (1 tablespoon), citrus juice (2 tablespoons) and a pinch of salt in a 2-quart dish, cut sides up.
  4. Set the rounds over the fruit, brush with more cream, and sprinkle with sugar and flaky salt.
  5. Bake 40–45 minutes until the tops are golden and the juices are thick and bubbling. Cool slightly; best served at room temperature.

Best the day it is made, but can be baked a day ahead and stored tightly wrapped at room temperature. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 3 tablespoons sugar
  • 1½ teaspoons baking powder
  • 1½ teaspoons baking soda
  • 1¼ teaspoons kosher salt
  • 2½ cups buttermilk
  • 2 large eggs
  • 3 tablespoons unsalted butter, melted
  • Coconut oil for the pan (vegetable or canola oil also fine)

Method

  1. Whisk the flour (2 cups), sugar (3 tablespoons), baking powder, baking soda (1½ teaspoons each) and salt (1¼ teaspoons) in a large bowl.
  2. Beat the eggs (2) into the buttermilk (2½ cups), fold into the dry mix, then stir in the melted butter (3 tablespoons). Lumps are fine; batter can rest in the fridge 1–2 hours.
  3. Get a griddle or cast-iron pan hot over medium, drop the heat to medium-low, and add oil (1–2 tablespoons). Ladle in ¼–⅓ cup batter per pancake.
  4. Flip once bubbles form and the underside browns, 2–4 minutes, then cook about 2 more minutes. Hold finished pancakes on a rack over a baking sheet in a 325°F oven.

Cook low-ish and slow-ish so the pancakes stay crisp-edged and do not deflate; batter can be made 1–2 hours ahead. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 cup plus 2 tablespoons (2¼ sticks) cold salted butter, cut into ½-inch pieces
  • ½ cup granulated sugar
  • ¼ cup (packed) light brown sugar
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla extract
  • 2¼ cups all-purpose flour
  • 6 ounces semisweet or bittersweet chocolate, chopped
  • 1 large egg, beaten to blend
  • Demerara sugar, for rolling
  • Flaky sea salt

Method

  1. Line baking sheets with parchment. Beat the butter (2¼ sticks) with both sugars (½ cup and ¼ cup) and vanilla (1 teaspoon) 3–5 minutes until very light and fluffy.
  2. Add the flour (2¼ cups) and chocolate (6 ounces) on low speed just until combined.
  3. Split the dough in two, shape each into a log 2–2¼ inches wide in plastic wrap, and chill about 2 hours until firm.
  4. Heat the oven to 350°F. Brush the logs with egg (1) and roll them in Demerara sugar.
  5. Slice into ½-inch rounds, set 1 inch apart, top with flaky salt, and bake 12–15 minutes until the edges just start to color.

Dough keeps refrigerated up to 1 week or frozen up to 1 month; baked cookies keep 5 days airtight. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

Crust

  • 3 cups (450g) all-purpose flour
  • 2 tablespoons granulated sugar
  • 1½ teaspoons kosher salt
  • 1½ cups (340g) unsalted butter, chilled, cut into 1-inch pieces
  • 1 tablespoon apple cider vinegar
  • ⅓ cup ice water, plus more as needed

Filling and assembly

  • 2½ pounds firm, tart baking apples, cored and thinly sliced (about 8 cups)
  • ¾ cup (180ml) mild honey
  • ½ cup (100g) granulated sugar, plus 2 tablespoons for sprinkling
  • 2 tablespoons all-purpose flour
  • 1 tablespoon apple cider vinegar or fresh lemon juice
  • 1 teaspoon ground cinnamon
  • 1 large egg
  • 3 tablespoons raw white sesame seeds (optional)
  • Flaky sea salt
  • Vanilla ice cream, for serving

Method

  1. Mix the flour (3 cups), sugar (2 tablespoons) and salt (1½ teaspoons), smear in the butter (1½ cups) into large flat pieces, and freeze about 5 minutes.
  2. Sprinkle over the vinegar (1 tablespoon) mixed with ice water (⅓ cup) and toss until shaggy, adding water only if needed. Pat into a 1-inch disc and chill at least 2 hours.
  3. Heat the oven to 375°F. Toss the apples (2½ pounds) with honey (¾ cup), sugar (½ cup), flour (2 tablespoons), vinegar or lemon (1 tablespoon) and cinnamon (1 teaspoon).
  4. Roll the dough into an 18-inch round, ease it into a greased 9-inch springform or deep pie plate, add the apples and juices, and fold the overhang over, leaving the center exposed.
  5. Brush the crust with egg (1 plus 1 teaspoon water), scatter sesame seeds, remaining sugar (2 tablespoons) and flaky salt, and bake on a sheet 65–75 minutes until deep golden and bubbling. Cool at least 2 hours before slicing; serve with ice cream.

Can be baked in a 9-inch springform or deep-dish pie plate; cool at least 2 hours before cutting. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 disc The Only Pie Crust
  • 4 tablespoons (½ stick) unsalted butter
  • ⅓ cup light brown sugar
  • ½ teaspoon ground cinnamon
  • 1½ pounds unpeeled crisp, tart apples (such as Pink Lady or Honeycrisp), thinly sliced
  • 1 egg, beaten
  • Granulated sugar, for sprinkling
  • Flaky salt, for sprinkling

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 375°F. Roll the dough disc (1) into a 15–16 inch circle, slide it onto a parchment-lined sheet and chill.
  2. Brown the butter (4 tablespoons) over medium-high heat for 2–3 minutes, then cool a little.
  3. Stir the brown sugar (⅓ cup) and cinnamon (½ teaspoon) together.
  4. Pile the apples (1½ pounds) onto the dough leaving a 2-inch border, drizzle with the brown butter and scatter the cinnamon sugar.
  5. Fold the border up over the fruit, brush with egg (1), sprinkle with granulated sugar and flaky salt, and bake 60–75 minutes until deeply golden.

Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 2½ cups all-purpose flour
  • 2 teaspoons sugar
  • 1 teaspoon kosher salt
  • 1¼ cups (2 sticks plus 4 tablespoons) unsalted butter, cut into 1-inch pieces, chilled
  • 1 tablespoon apple cider vinegar or distilled white vinegar
  • ¼ cup ice water, plus more as needed

Method

  1. Whisk the flour (2½ cups), sugar (2 teaspoons) and salt (1 teaspoon) together, then rub in the cold butter (1¼ cups) until you have long, thin, flaky pieces and no big chunks. Tip onto the counter.
  2. Mix the vinegar (1 tablespoon) into the ice water (¼ cup), sprinkle it over, and work it in with your fingertips.
  3. Knead with your palms, folding dry bits up from the bottom; add up to 2 tablespoons more water if needed, but keep the dough on the dry side.
  4. Once shaggy, knead once or twice more, halve it, pat each half into a 1-inch disc, wrap, and chill at least 2 hours.

Make up to 4 days ahead, tightly wrapped in the fridge, or freeze up to 1 month. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1 cup raw walnut pieces or halves
  • 4 Honeycrisp or Pink Lady apples (or comparable sweet-tart variety)
  • 2 tablespoons apple cider vinegar, plus more to taste
  • Flaky sea salt and freshly ground black pepper
  • 2 tablespoons honey, plus more to taste
  • Olive oil, for drizzling

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 325°F and toast the walnuts (1 cup) on a sheet 8–10 minutes until golden and fragrant. Let cool, then crush roughly.
  2. Slice the apples (4) very thin and fan them on a platter.
  3. Drizzle with vinegar (2 tablespoons), season with salt and pepper, and toss lightly.
  4. Drizzle with honey (2 tablespoons), taste, and adjust the sweet-sour balance.
  5. Scatter the walnuts on top and finish with olive oil, flaky salt and pepper.

Aim for more tart than sweet; pears and other nuts work, and lemon juice or white-wine vinegar can replace cider vinegar. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 6 tablespoons (¾ stick) unsalted butter
  • 2½ cups confectioners' sugar
  • ¾ cup unsweetened cocoa powder
  • 1 teaspoon kosher salt
  • 2 large egg whites
  • 1 large egg
  • 8 ounces bittersweet chocolate (at least 67% cacao), chopped
  • ½ cup finely chopped hazelnuts, almonds, pistachios, pecans or walnuts (optional)
  • Flaky sea salt, such as Maldon

Method

  1. Heat the oven to 350°F and line two sheets with parchment. Brown the butter (6 tablespoons) over medium heat 3–4 minutes until foamy and nutty, then cool fully.
  2. Sift or whisk the confectioners' sugar (2½ cups), cocoa (¾ cup) and salt (1 teaspoon) to remove lumps.
  3. Stir in the egg whites (2), whole egg (1) and cooled butter until smooth, then fold in the chocolate (8 ounces) and nuts (½ cup) if using.
  4. Drop quarter-sized mounds 2 inches apart, top with flaky salt, and bake 6–8 minutes until flattened and crinkly. Cool before moving.

Can be baked up to 2 days ahead, wrapped tightly, and kept at room temperature. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Her blueberry riff on the olive-oil cake family.

One of the few recipes still subscriber-only on her site – opens with your login. Paste the text here anytime and it becomes a full card. Open recipe →

Ingredients

Red wine ice

  • 1 cup dry red wine
  • 2 cups water
  • ½ lemon, thinly sliced

Punch

  • ½ cup honey
  • ½ cup sugar
  • 1 cup water
  • 5 cups Maker's Mark 46 bourbon
  • 2½ cups fresh lemon juice
  • 4 (12-ounce) cans seltzer, plus more as desired
  • Lemon slices and ice, for serving

Method

  1. Combine the wine (1 cup), water (2 cups) and lemon slices (½ lemon) in a 9-inch cake pan and freeze solid, at least 4 hours or overnight.
  2. Simmer the honey (½ cup), sugar (½ cup) and water (1 cup) just until dissolved, then cool completely.
  3. Stir the bourbon (5 cups), lemon juice (2½ cups) and cooled syrup together in a big punch bowl.
  4. At serving time, float the wine ice block in the bowl and top with seltzer (4 cans).
  5. Ladle over ice into cups, garnish with lemon slices, and add more seltzer to taste.

Wine ice keeps frozen several weeks; punch base (no ice or seltzer) keeps refrigerated up to 2 days; honey syrup keeps refrigerated several weeks. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup (1 stick) butter, plus more for the pan
  • 3/4 cup + 2 Tbsp sugar, plus more for the pan
  • 8 oz bittersweet chocolate (67%+), finely chopped
  • 1/2 cup Nutella, almond butter or tahini
  • 6 large eggs
  • 1/2 cup hazelnut or almond flour
  • 2 Tbsp cocoa powder
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • Topping: 1 cup heavy cream, 1/4 cup confectioners' sugar, pinch salt, 1 cup sour cream, 1/4 cup Nutella; cherries optional

Method

  1. Heat oven to 350°F. Butter a 9-inch springform and dust it with sugar.
  2. Melt the chocolate (8 oz), Nutella (1/2 cup) and butter (1 stick) over simmering water until smooth.
  3. Separate 4 of the eggs. Whisk the 4 yolks with the 2 whole eggs, nut flour (1/2 cup), cocoa (2 Tbsp) and salt (1 tsp); fold into the chocolate.
  4. Whip the 4 whites foamy, rain in the sugar (3/4 cup), and beat to stiff, tripled volume; fold in gently.
  5. Scrape into the pan, scatter the last sugar (2 Tbsp) on top, bake 35–40 minutes until puffed, cracked and pulling from the sides.
  6. Cool fully – the sunken middle is the point; it makes the edges crisp. Whip the topping cream (1 cup) with confectioners' sugar and salt, whisk in sour cream (1 cup) and Nutella (1/4 cup); serve alongside, cherries if you have them.

Bakes up to 2 days ahead; wrap tightly, room temperature. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • Crust: 8 oz vanilla wafers or graham crackers, 3 Tbsp light brown sugar, 6 Tbsp melted butter, pinch salt
  • 1 lb cream cheese, room temp
  • 1 cup sour cream, Greek yogurt or labne
  • 1/2 cup granulated sugar
  • 2 large eggs
  • 2 Tbsp finely grated citrus zest
  • Pinch kosher salt
  • Thin citrus slices, to top (optional)

Method

  1. Heat oven to 325°F. Grind the cookies (8 oz); stir in brown sugar (3 Tbsp), melted butter (6 Tbsp) and salt to damp sand. Pack into a 9-inch pie plate or springform.
  2. Bake the crust until golden at the edges, 10–12 minutes.
  3. Blitz cream cheese (1 lb), sour cream (1 cup) and sugar (1/2 cup) smooth; add eggs (2), zest (2 Tbsp) and salt and blend silky.
  4. Pour into the warm crust; bake until set with a center wobble, 20–35 minutes. Oven off, door propped, 20 minutes more.
  5. Cool 30 minutes, then chill ~2 hours. Citrus slices over to serve.

Keeps 5 days wrapped. Sits between the Basque and the Almost Cheesecake on the board's cheesecake spectrum. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →

Ingredients

  • 1/2 cup Demerara sugar, for pan and top
  • 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1/2 cup cocoa powder
  • 1 tsp baking soda
  • 1 tsp kosher salt
  • 6 Tbsp butter, room temp
  • 1/3 cup granulated + 1/4 cup light brown sugar
  • 1 tsp vanilla
  • 1 large egg
  • 5 very ripe bananas – 4 mashed, 1 halved lengthwise
  • 1/2 cup mascarpone, sour cream, yogurt or labne

Method

  1. Heat oven to 350°F. Grease a 9×5 loaf pan and coat it with half the Demerara (1/4 cup).
  2. Whisk flour (1 1/2 cups), cocoa (1/2 cup), soda (1 tsp) and salt (1 tsp).
  3. Beat the butter (6 Tbsp) with both sugars and vanilla until fluffy, 3–5 minutes; beat in the egg (1) ~2 minutes.
  4. Low speed: the dry mix, then fold in mashed bananas (4) and mascarpone (1/2 cup).
  5. Top with the split banana (1) and remaining Demerara (1/4 cup); bake 90–100 minutes until the center springs back.
  6. Cool completely before slicing – it's fudgy when warm.

The board's plain banana bread for purists is in this section too. Adapted in our own words. Original on alisoneroman.com →
